# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

Please choose versions by [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

* MAJOR version when you make incompatible API changes,
* MINOR version when you add functionality in a backwards-compatible manner, and
* PATCH version when you make backwards-compatible bug fixes.

## Unreleased

- fix: **a session whose permission channel is dead now reads as unable-to-act instead of as progress, and the two reasons it never fired are both fixed.** `stuck_reason` gains a fourth cause, `permission-channel`, read over a WINDOW rather than the trailing run: `trailing_failures` resets on any tool that ran, which is right for "did the worker exhaust its retries" and wrong for "is the channel dead" — a worker whose writes abort while its reads keep working resets the run on every successful `Read`, so the trailing rule read it clean at every point between aborts. Measured 2026-10-28 on session `b6e447e4`: 12 aborts in two minutes (09:46:33–09:48:41Z) interleaved with successful reads and Edits, `trailing_run=0`, `stuck_reason=None`. The second reason was the read window itself: `read_tail`'s fixed 400 KB reaches only the last 28 tool results of an 11.5 MB transcript, while the aborts sat at the file's midpoint — every one of them outside it — so `read_recent_tail` now walks backward in chunks until the text spans `PERM_CHANNEL_WINDOW_S` (15 min) of transcript time, anchored on the transcript's own last stamp so a stored transcript stays reproducible. Three aborts inside the window is the threshold; the cause clears by AGEING OUT, never by a success, which is what keeps it bounded where the rejected longest-run reading was not. `permission-channel` sits after `stream-closed` (same cause, most immediate — a case that already fires keeps its name) and before `blocked-closer` (a measured fact about the transport beats the worker's own account of the symptom). `stuck-heal.py` treats it as a death rather than a park and takes the same ladder, because a resume is what restored the channel on `b6e447e4` and reporting it would leave the session that owns a live repair unable to run it; `round-readings.py`'s `stuck_verdict` gains a `permission-channel` carrier so the manager round's own output names it, reading the rule from the watcher rather than restating it — and bounded by `PERM_CHANNEL_STALE_S`, because a row carries every id it ever ran under and the window is anchored on the transcript's own last stamp, so an id whose session died with its last 15 minutes full of aborts would otherwise pin a healthy live row to `permission-channel` forever; the watcher deliberately does not apply that bound, since a session that is genuinely gone is settled by `is_gated`'s `unregistered` branch before the stuck limb and a staleness bound there would blind it to a session whose channel is dead and whose transcript has therefore gone quiet. `commands/steps/round-tail.md` gains the cause in its `stuck:` list. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/stuck-heal.py`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `scripts/tests/test_stuck.py`, `scripts/tests/test_stuck_heal.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.259.0

- feat: `scripts/resume-check.py` answers whether a resume actually ran a turn, from the transcript rather than from the spawn response — a manager could not tell a productive resume from an empty one, because `spawn_agent(resume=…)` answers `running` with a wrapper id either way (measured 2026-11-05: agent_12 / agent_13 returned `num_turns: 0` with an empty result while agent_14 worked). It reads `<projects>/*/<session-id>.jsonl` by session id, deduplicated by inode, counts a turn only from an `assistant` record or a `result` with `num_turns > 0`, and counts the synthetic `<task-notification>` records the CLI injects on resume separately — so the input that produced the false green can never be read as work. Verdicts `productive` / `zero-turn` / `no-evidence` / `running`; `--since` and `--until` bound the window, because a transcript keeps growing on later resumes and an unbounded window credits that later work to the resume being judged; `--fail-on-zero-turn` exits 1 for a manager loop. Change set: `scripts/resume-check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_resume_check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.258.1

- fix: a headless worker whose last `result` carries `num_turns: 0` now settles `status: error` with `error: zero-turn: …` and `result.zero_turn: true` instead of `done`, (on the stream-throw path too), and the idle-worker end-of-life keeps an otherwise-idle session on a zero-turn `result` for a 120 s grace window, then re-runs its full decision (wrapper state, parked question, open cards) and ends it only if still idle — on resume the CLI's own `<task-notification>` for orphaned background agents settled as that zero-turn result ahead of the caller's queued prompt, the wrapper counted it against `pending_turns`, and the supervisor stopped the session before the prompt ran

## v0.258.0

- feat: **the fleet round now asserts its own invariant every tick — every live session has exactly one keeper — instead of discovering the gaps one incident at a time.** `scripts/keeper-census.py` joins `session-liveness.py --list --json` to the `claude_session_id` values on topic, goal and task pages, resolves each session's keeper tier from `docs/session-tiers.md` § Keep-alive (the rule source it cites), and reads whether that keeper's leg *exists* from the keep-alive step files' own text (`commands/steps/keepalive*.md` plus `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`) rather than from a hardcoded list — so a leg added in a new step file is picked up with no edit here, and a tier nobody ships reads red until one is. A keeper read that cannot answer is a third row state — `cannot-tell`, counted in the header and never counted red — because a failed probe must not be reported as a definite gap. It exits `1` naming every unkept and double-kept row, `0` with `keeper-census: unkept=0 double-kept=0 sessions=<n>` as its last line, and `2` when the registry or the vault cannot be read — a refusal, never a fleet of zero. It is the sixth reading of `commands/steps/readings.md` (router row 15) and posts **one** card per red episode under a dedup key carrying the episode start and day, so a still-red census does not re-ask and a re-lapse is asked again. ⚠️ **Ownership is provisional:** the shared resolver from the single-ownership-read task has not landed, so the census carries a marked adapter (`owner()`) implementing the same definition — LIVE **and** loop `OK`/`STALE` — and nothing else in the file decides ownership, so the swap is one function. ⚠️ **`unkept` means "a page records it and nobody keeps it"**; a live session **no page records** is a fourth state, `unrecorded` — printed, named in the `MISMATCH` block and counted in the header, never counted red and never carded. That distinction is what makes the clean line reachable at all: 20 of 80 live sessions on 2026-11-08 are outside the session tree, and counting them as keeper gaps fired the card every episode-day forever. Real reading after the change: `keeper-census: unkept=0 double-kept=0 sessions=80`, `unrecorded=20 cannot-tell=1`. Two joins are the repo's shared readers rather than local copies: `scripts/goals-declaration.py` for `goals:` (five shapes, YAML `''` escape — a local extractor read 78 pages as `Fleet''s` and dropped a bare-item declaration) and `scripts/session-ids.py`'s field set for session ids (`claude_session_id` **plus** `metrics_sessions[].session_id` — 26 pages record only the latter). Change set: `scripts/keeper-census.py`, `scripts/tests/test_keeper_census.py`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.257.2

- fix: **`send_agent_message` reaches a headless worker by resolving its registry name and handing it to the caller, instead of refusing it with a channel the caller cannot address.** Measured 2026-11-08 on session `6d16758d`: `send_agent_message(agent_id=<id>)` refused with *"no pane … reach it mid-task with SendMessage"*, `SendMessage(to="6d16758d…")` answered `No agent named … is reachable`, and `SendMessage(to="claude-1f")` delivered — the worker was live and registered the whole time, under a name nothing in the refusal told the caller how to obtain. `resolveMessageChannel` gained a `peer` channel fed by an injected resolver (the module stays a pure function over the record), `liveness.mjs` gained `namesForSessionId` — the inverse of `sessionIdsNamed` — and the handler returns `{ sent: false, channel: 'peer', name }` with the `SendMessage` call to make, because the server is not a Claude session and cannot call that tool itself. ⚠️ **`sent: false` is the contract, not a failure report:** claiming `sent: true` would repeat the defect the change removes — a channel reported as working while delivering nothing. Two names for one id are refused rather than guessed, and so is a single name that ANOTHER live session also answers to — `SendMessage` resolves a name to a session, so a shared name reaches whichever the harness picks (both would reach the wrong conversation), an unreadable registry is retryable and distinct from "no entry", and a worker with no entry at all (the session exited, or it runs in-process under the SDK, which registers no name) still refuses — the fix widens the success path, never the failure one. `scripts/goal-condition.py`'s headless refusal and `docs/fleet-surface.md` are reworded to the reason that still holds (the spawn path performs no second `SendMessage`), since "no pane" was never the whole truth. Change set: `server/message-channel.mjs`, `server/message-channel.test.mjs`, `server/liveness.mjs`, `server/liveness.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `scripts/goal-condition.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.257.1

- fix: **`closer-gate-lint` blocks a re-emitted closer once more instead of releasing it through the `stop_hook_active` loop guard, and recognises `gh pr ready` in the form CLAUDE.md mandates.** The retry was admitting already-authorised verbs back to the operator as gates — 21 such admissions on the deciding 7-day window `2026-11-01T10:43:59Z → 2026-11-08T10:43:59Z`, each one a closer the lint had already blocked. The guard keeps its purpose and gains a bound: when the previous `Stop` blocked this same closer and it is re-emitted unchanged, it is blocked once more (`blocked-retry`), and only a third identical stop is allowed, so the worst case is two blocks and never a loop; the bound is read from the hook's own record, whose every failure path still allows. Separately, the `gh-pr-ready` entry required `gh` and `pr ready` to be adjacent, so `gh -R <owner>/<repo> pr ready N` — the only form the global rules' § Git permits — fell through as `not-allow-listed` while the bare form was released; the entry now uses the same loose shape as `gh-pr-merge`, with `--admin` still denied globally. Change set: `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/37-gh-pr-ready-with-repo-flag.jsonl`, `hooks/hooks.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.257.0

- feat: **a topic round that reads `resume` / `restart` and does nothing now reports itself incomplete.** Measured 2026-11-08: a topic manager read `resume` for three goal managers on two consecutive ticks, resumed none, blamed the per-topic N = 3 — and `manager-receipt.py --check` stayed green because every field it knew resolved. `topic-keepalive.py --receipt <topic>` now writes each `resume` / `restart` verdict onto the round's receipt as owed; the round records `--record-outcome` (`executed`, `refused:<token>` from a fixed producer list, or `deferred:busy-target`); `--check` prints `INCOMPLETE ROUND — skipped: keepalive` for a verdict with no outcome, an unknown token, or an `executed` with no `--record-resume` stamp (resume) / `kind=spawn` exit record (restart) inside the round window. The step is conditional, so a goal round and a topic round with no such verdict owe nothing. `commands/topic-drive.md` and `agents/topic-drive.md` now say N = 3 gates a start and never a resume. The resume store now also records which session each `--record-resume` confirmed (a `sessions` list beside the cap's unchanged `resumes`), so `executed` needs the verdict's own id, and it resolves the vault through `realpath` — a vault reached through a symlink moves to its real name's folder once, resetting that vault's 30-minute crash-loop count.

## v0.256.0

- feat: **the fleet round now keeps alive a goal manager whose goal no topic declares.** `docs/session-tiers.md` assigns those goals to the fleet manager, but the goal-branch keep-alive ran only inside a topic manager's round, so such a manager was resumed by nobody when it died (measured 2026-11-08 on private-personal: 29 non-terminal goals carried a manager id and no topic declared any of them). `commands/steps/keepalive.md` gains § Restart a lapsed goal manager whose goal no topic declares. It runs the new `scripts/topicless-goal-keepalive.py`, which selects goals at `status: in_progress` + `phase: execution` that carry a `claude_session_id` and that no topic's `## Goals` list names, and asks `topic-keepalive.py --branch goal` for each verdict. The script decides and never acts. The phase filter is load-bearing: `topic-keepalive.py` ignores phase, and 5 of the 8 `resume` verdicts in the census sat outside `execution`. A topic-declared goal is never selected, because the topic round owns it. `scripts/crashed-managers.py` now treats the same set as covered (`fleet_keeps`, one home), so it no longer cards a manager the leg restarts. `scripts/fleet-legs.py` gains the `topicless-goal-managers` key under `keep-alive`, rendered in `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 14.
- fix: **every keep-alive section now resolves its folders from the named vault's own config entry.** Before, each snippet in `commands/steps/keepalive.md` took the *first* `vault-cli config list` entry that carried the key. On the operator's machine that is `private-starcitizen` (`23 Goals`) and `seibert-agent` (`tasks`), so private-personal resolved to the wrong folders (measured 2026-11-08). `scripts/resolve-folders.py` gains `--vault <path>`, which resolves by path instead of by cwd, and all three snippets now use it. `--require <keys>` lets each section demand only the folders it reads; any other unconfigured key prints empty. The resolved folders are absolute, and the loops now glob them directly. Effect on private-personal: the dead-worker section had been globbing a nonexistent `tasks/` and covering nothing; it now reaches the 288 non-terminal id-carrying task pages. Terminal pages are skipped in the shell before the per-page call, which would otherwise be ~3,900 process starts, about 44 min per round.

## v0.255.0

- feat: **every parked call carries a permission tier — `allowlist`, `manager` or `operator` — so a worker's routine writes reach its owning manager instead of the operator's board, while the operator-only class always reaches the operator.** The machine half (`scripts/permission-tier.py`, `server/permission-tier.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, the 111-case shared fixture) merged in #688 and ships inert until this doc lands, because routing lives here: `docs/permission-allowlist.md` states the three tiers, the operator-only `Form` table, and the manager's rubric with its cannot-verify → escalate branch, and is cited from the manager loop's gate step and `agents/goal-wrangler.md`; `commands/steps/worker-channels.md` answers by tier. The rubric is the second gate the operator's 2026-11-06 ruling asks for — *"read only => allowed · write => send to manager … that is a second gate and can allow it"* — and it is bounded: a manager that cannot verify a shape escalates rather than allowing it. `make check-manager-allowlist` now also asserts the doc's operator-only forms equal the fixture's forms, so the prose and the fixture cannot drift apart silently. `scripts/replay-tier-baseline.py` reproduces the SC3 numbers from a frozen card set, checking the moved set against an independent regex that is not the classifier. Git reads stay off the auto-allow list: `git status` runs `core.fsmonitor`, so they are `manager`, never `allowlist`. Change set: `docs/permission-allowlist.md`, `agents/goal-wrangler.md`, `commands/steps/worker-channels.md`, `scripts/check-manager-allowlist.mjs`, `scripts/replay-tier-baseline.py`, `scripts/tests/test_permission_tier.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the goal round's receipt now covers the drive leg, and a topic round is judged on what a topic round owes.** `STEPS` carried `needs-you`, `sweep`, `watcher` and `readings` — none of them the leg that resumes orphaned work — so a round that read, swept, rendered a complete-looking table and stopped exited 0 and printed "Round complete". Measured 2026-11-07 on goal `No Session Sits Idle Unnoticed and a Lapsed Manager Is Detected`: five sweep readers after 13:51:15Z and **zero** drive dispatches, with `.tracked.txt` 18:22:25Z against `.drive.txt` 14:21:14Z, while `--check` read green because the receipt had no field for the leg that never ran. `drive` joins `STEPS` and the tail's `--step` enumeration, `commands/steps/goal-drive.md` persists the leg's own report to `~/.claude/state/manager-receipts/<slug>.drive` and records it, and `drive_shape()` requires the leg's own headers (`To open`, `To resume`, `Nudged`, `Not resumed`, `Escalated`, `Reaped`) so a stub fails — while a quiet leg that reports "nothing to do" still passes, because a predicate demanding a non-empty row set would report `skipped: drive` on a healthy round. ⚠️ **The tier split ships with it, not after it:** `STEPS` is the goal/topic tuple, so adding `drive` alone would print `skipped: drive` on every topic round forever — the permanent false red `FLEET_STEPS`' own comment names. `steps_for(branch, tier)` returns a new `TOPIC_STEPS` (`needs-you`, `readings`, `sweep`, `watcher`) for `--tier topic`, and `TIERS` is deliberately **not** a `BRANCHES` value: `slug()` interpolates `branch` into the state path, so a third branch would re-key every receipt already on disk and orphan it. `sweep` stays in the topic tuple because the topic round does sweep every tick — dropping it would let a round that skips its sweep report complete, this row's own defect one tier over — so `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md` gains a `--write-payload` path on its dispatch and the `--record --step sweep` call it was missing, and `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md` names the payload path as a required input. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/steps/goal-drive.md`, `commands/steps/topic-sweep.md`, `agents/topic-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **`/supervisor:open` now refuses a spawn whose session ends up named `agent_N`.** `server/supervisor.mjs` stores `label: label || id`, so a call that omits `label` is recorded under the minted agent id — the session is then unidentifiable in the fleet list and unmatchable to the row that opened it. `commands/open.md` marks `label` mandatory on the `spawn_agent` create branch, defines `<title>` as the row's subject on the wezterm fallback, and makes Step 4 refuse a result whose name is not the row's subject — `agent_\d+` is only one of the forms a missing label takes, because `''` is falsy and an empty label falls through the same `label || id` path; measured live 2026-11-08, a row with `label: ''` came up as `claude-00` and a `agent_\d+` pattern match would have passed it. `scripts/budget-baseline.json` carries the recorded size for `commands/open.md`.
- fix: **the `--flagged` batch no longer re-opens a row it opened minutes earlier.** Two commands an hour apart re-opened the same 21 rows on 2026-11-07, because the batch carried no record of what it had just opened. The record already existed — the spawn ledger — so the fix is a read, not a second store: `scripts/opened-recently.py` answers *was this subject opened inside the window* from `scripts/spawn-ledger.py`'s endpoint, matching `⚙ <subject>` and the `(2)` uniqueness suffix, and exit **2** (unreadable ledger) is kept distinct from exit 1 (not opened) because collapsing them authorises the duplicate. The batch prints the matched `spawned_at` beside each skip — without it a record skip is indistinguishable from a liveness skip — and counts them in the footer as `skipped S already opened`. ⚠️ The ledger answers *was it opened*, never *is it running*: a record reads `status: running` whether its worker is alive or hours dead, so liveness stays with `session-liveness.py`.
- fix: **the resume path refuses a truncated session id, and the batch no longer waits on a live row.** `session-liveness.py --check` accepts an 8-char prefix by design, but `--resume` needs the whole uuid — a truncated field resumed nothing, which is what the 2026-11-07 batch's `HTTP 400`s were. `PRIOR_SID` must now be a full 36-character uuid or the row is refused with `❌ TRUNCATED ID`; an empty id still falls through to CREATE. Separately, the single-task path's *wait for the live pid to exit* rule is not viable on a batch, where one live row would hold every later row behind a session that may never exit: the batch reports `⏭️ LIVE HERE`, takes no branch for that row and continues. That verdict is distinct from `🔒 LIVE ELSEWHERE`, which means live with no local process and is a refusal.
- fix: **a `role: agent` row never opens `interactive`, even when its `mode:` says so.** Operator ruling 2026-11-07. `docs/role-mode-matrix.md` Rule 2 gains the narrowing and `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 records it against the both-ways carve-out, which still governs `human`, `manager` and an `agent` row that does carry a watch-need signal. `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6 rewrites the field and prints `normalised mode interactive -> headless`, and the batch's report legend no longer claims a row with no `mode:` opens on `spawn.mode` — by Step 3 every row carries the value Step 0.6 resolved.

- fix: **the open leg's lost-hand-back guard gains its third arm, and its render carries every count it reconciles.** Five confirmation-review findings on the open-leg split. (1) `commands/steps/open.md`'s duplicate-spawn guard distinguished only "row found" from "no row", but `scripts/spawn-ledger.py`'s `read_ledger()` returns **`None` when the ledger is unreadable** — its own docstring: `None` ≠ `{}`, because collapsing them "turns a failure into a confident empty fleet" — so a failed read fell into the "nothing was opened" branch and authorised exactly the re-run the guard exists to prevent. The guard now branches four ways: no payload ⇒ re-run safe; `None` ⇒ open nothing and never re-run; a row naming a selected task ⇒ open nothing more; a readable ledger with no such row ⇒ re-run. Both unknown cases print `open leg: unknown — opened=unknown (<why>)`. (2) The `status=ok` render printed `opened: <n> of <m>` alone, so a round that resumed two rows was indistinguishable from one that lost two; it now prints `opened: <n> of <m> · resumed=<n> · jumped=<n>`. (3) `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 3 claimed to be the single home for these forms while omitting the `refused:` lines and the `opened=unknown` fallback; both are added and it stays the single home. (4) `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`'s empty-selection branch omitted `refused=0` from the four-way reconciliation; added. (5) `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Approval provenance's "Fleet open leg" row still pointed at `commands/steps/open.md` for the `flag` predicate, which the split moved into `agents/fleet-open-actor.md` § constraints — the row now points there and records that the step reads no `flag`. No guard pinned that row: `scripts/check-resume-flag-clause.py` checks the section's presence and its pointer carriers, not the table. Change set: `commands/steps/open.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the fleet open leg's duplicate-spawn guard reads the spawn ledger, its counts reconcile per branch, and its payload scopes are checkable from the files alone.** Five findings from the pre-PR review of the open-leg split, each of which made a written rule unevaluable. (1) `commands/steps/open.md`'s "did the first invocation already open a row" guard read `fleet-sessions.py`, whose roster rows are built from session **transcripts** with the ledger joined on — so a worker spawned seconds ago, before its first transcript line, had no row, which is exactly the race the guard claims to close. It now imports `scripts/spawn-ledger.py` and calls `read_ledger()`, matching each row's `label` (the task name, because `/supervisor:open` passes `label <task>`) against the block's selected names; the row is written at the spawn edge (the write is issued before the spawn call returns), not at the worker's first transcript line. (2) `opened` had no defined source: `/supervisor:open` reports three branches, and a resumed row is reachable for a selected row, so `opened + refused == selected` was false. The mapping is now stated one count per branch — `🆕 created` → `opened` (a new worker), `♻️ resumed` → a new `resumed=` count, `↪️ jumped` → a new `jumped=` count, a refusal → `refused` — with `opened + resumed + jumped + refused == selected`, and both new keys join `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`'s required set (plus a falsification test). (3) The fallback guard compared against "a row the block selected" while holding no `selected`; the step now names the payload path (`<digest minus .digest>.open`) and states the safe reading — no payload ⇒ the leg never reached its act step ⇒ nothing opened ⇒ re-run is safe. (4) The truncation payload's scope contradicted the contract: the agent said the **full** list goes to `<round>.open-refusals` while `docs/handback-contract.md` and the step expect only the remainder, so `printed + truncated == refused` was uncheckable; the agent now says remainder, matching both. (5) `Skill` cannot take a whole command line, so "invoke `selected[].action` verbatim" was impossible as written; it is now spelled as a field split — `skill: "supervisor:open"` plus `args:` carrying the byte-identical remainder — with the never-compose rule preserved as prefix removal rather than re-composition. `docs/handback-contract.md`'s open-leg required set names the four-way reconciliation. Change set: `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `commands/steps/open.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- refactor: **the fleet round's open leg (router row 16) is delegated to `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, removing the round's heaviest single leg — measured +22 575 manager-side tokens per round.** `commands/steps/open.md` carried the whole procedure: the `open-queue.py --json` invocation whose payload lists every skipped row by name and reason (130 of them on a live round), the block render, and the per-row `/supervisor:open` invocations. It is now a dispatcher: it keeps the ROUND's half — the acting warning, the `status=ok` / `unknown` / `not-run` renders, the refusal print-and-continue, the truncation reconciliation, the "never open a row the leg skipped" rule, the manager negative control, and the run-by-hand fallback for a lost hand-back — and hands the procedure to a Sonnet agent that pays the payload cost and returns an ≤ 1 KB hand-back (`status`, `selected`, `opened`, `resumed`, `jumped`, `refused`, `render`, `reason`, `truncated`, `payload`). The agent owns the one-invocation/two-field rule, `--vault`, the never-compose rule, the block's three shapes, the flag/approval split and the workers-only boundary; it holds `spawn_agent` + `send_agent_message` so `/supervisor:open` takes the headless path rather than the wezterm tab fallback, and cites `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 for the mode decision it never makes. Row 16 joins the delegate rule's carve-out and `scripts/check-acting-legs.py`, and `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` gains the leg's required set. The block is written to `<round>.open` beside the digest, so `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`'s prune clause now covers `*.open*`. No digest key is added: the leg's persisted reading is that payload, rendered by `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 3. Change set: `agents/fleet-open-actor.md`, `commands/steps/open.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/check-acting-legs.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_acting_legs.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the flagged-open fallback refuses to re-open rather than trusting a blind guard, and the keep-alive hand-back's `not-run` path now has a defined shape.** A lost hand-back used to re-run `/supervisor:open --flagged` behind two reads — the digest's `operator-flagged` line and a roster lookup — but the roster is transcript-driven and a headless worker never binds `claude_session_id`, so a just-spawned worker was invisible and the non-idempotent batch could spawn a second one onto the same task. `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` now refuses: it records `--leg operator-flagged --not-run` and reports `operator-flagged: not run — <reason>`, leaving the operator's flags to the next round. `legs=written` now means every reading the leg owes landed — a count **or** its own `not-run` reason — and `dead-scan` is `—` when `dead-workers=not-run`, because no count was written and no label applies; `docs/handback-contract.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md` and the actor agree on both. Both actors publish the truncation payload through one `python3` call instead of holding `Bash(mkdir:*)`, `Bash(mv:*)` and `Write` for a single file, and both steps use the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-…}` form for every script path. `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py` pins the per-list dropped counts, `dead-scan` and the flagged-open required set. Change set: `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **`agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` is back under the 30 KB body budget with no behaviour lost.** The file had grown to 34 685 bytes and `check-budget` refuses a new oversized body outright, so `make precommit` was red. The trim replaces restated prose with a pointer to each rule's single home — the operator-hold rule (`skills/hold/SKILL.md`), the spawn-mode path selection (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6), the hand-back truncation exception and payload mechanics (`docs/handback-contract.md` § The shape) and the digest line formats (`commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 14) — and collapses wording repeated across the four procedures. Every `⚠️` guard, script call and flag, exit-code reading, population resolver, the hold-rule behaviour and the full hand-back key set survive the trim. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the two acting legs honour the mode rule and the operator hold, and the hand-back contract's reconciliation and reason channels now exist on both sides.** `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md` carried the `spawn_agent` grant with no route to the mode rule, so `check-spawn-mode` failed and CI's `test` was red — it now cites `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 and states that the mode is the command's to pass. `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` resumed a `mode: headless` worker as a tab because it held no `mcp__supervisor__*` tool: it now holds `spawn_agent` (held for `resume=` alone — path A, the headless resume) and `SendMessage` (the follow-up a `goal_loop: true` row needs after a path-A resume — `send_agent_message` refuses a headless worker), each justified in the file, the spawn path citing the same item 6. Its nudge, topic resume, park-watch resume and worker resume now gate on a `session-holds.py is-held` read of the full session id (never the 8-char display handle) and render a held row `⏸️ HELD — <reason> · <age>` without acting, per `skills/hold/SKILL.md`; the nudge and the path-A resume had no downstream backstop. The truncation exception's caller reconciliation now has a count to check on both lists — the keep-alive hand-back emits `crash-loop=` and `cannot-tell=`, and `park-nudge:` is stated as a single summary line that never truncates — and `legs-reason=` gives the `legs: UNKNOWN — <reason>` render a channel in both delegated hand-backs. `docs/handback-contract.md`, both consuming steps and `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` agree on all of it. Change set: `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the two acting legs' truncation payloads are pruned, and their grants and write carve-outs match what they do.** `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` writes `<round>.keepalive` and `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md` writes `<round>.flagged-open` into the same store, but the prune rule at `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` deleted only `*.digest` and `*.drive` — so both grew unbounded. That clause is the rule's single home and now bounds all four suffixes at the newest 100 of each kind (byte-neutral, so its budget baseline is untouched), and each actor points at it rather than restating it. `fleet-flagged-open-actor.md` gains the keep-alive's write carve-out — `/supervisor:open --flagged` stamps the task pages it opens, so "nothing else on disk" contradicted step 1. Six dead grants leave `fleet-keepalive-actor.md` (`grep`, `basename`, `printf`, `echo`, `head`, `tr` — unused since the walks became `python3 -c`) and two leave `fleet-flagged-open-actor.md` (`cat`, `stat`). The keep-alive step's no-usable-hand-back fallback now points at its agent file rather than telling the round to run procedures the step no longer carries; the flagged-open fallback refuses to re-invoke instead. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `agents/fleet-flagged-open-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- refactor: **the fleet round's two acting legs now delegate to write-granted sub-agents, so their procedures stop re-injecting into the Fleet Manager's context on every tick.** `commands/steps/keepalive.md` (router row 5b) dispatches `supervisor:fleet-keepalive-actor` and `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` (5c) dispatches `supervisor:fleet-flagged-open-actor`; each agent carries the leg's full body — the four keep-alive procedures with every population resolver, script call and `⚠️` guard, and the `--flagged` invocation with the `flag_set_by` safety rule — and writes its own digest counts through `fleet-legs.py` (`lapsed-managers`, `dead-workers` with `--floor`/`--exact`, `operator-flagged`), so a reading still lands in the only artifact that outlives the round. Each step keeps what is the ROUND's decision: the debt gate (`--only re-arm` for keepalive) with its exit-12/exit-2 handling, the `--not-run` rules at key granularity, the failed-write render, the `⚠️ This leg acts` warning and its carve-out pointer, plus a fallback that runs the leg itself when the delegation returns no usable hand-back — except on the flagged-open side, whose batch is not idempotent and which therefore refuses and records `not-run` instead — a hand-back carrying counts but no acts is the read-only failure this leg exists to remove. `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` registers both legs, so a step reverted to its pre-split text or a hand-back that stops emitting a key the caller consumes fails; `docs/handback-contract.md` counts its ten carriers and states the three new required sets. `commands/fleet-drive.md` is unchanged — its rows, delegate rule and carve-out already covered the split.
- fix: **the keep-alive leg's hand-back can no longer exceed its 1 KB cap or silently drop subjects.** `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` emitted one `crash-loop:` / `cannot-tell:` / `park-nudge:` line per subject with no escape, so a large round had to break the cap or lose rows — while `docs/handback-contract.md` § The shape sanctions exactly one exception and `agents/fleet-drive.md` already implements it. The hand-back now carries one dropped count per truncatable list (`crash-loop-truncated=`, `cannot-tell-truncated=`) and `payload=<path>|—`: when the row lines would push it past 1 KB the leg emits the counts plus as many lines as fit, names each list's dropped count, and writes the full lists to `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/<round timestamp>.keepalive` (tmp-then-`os.replace`, sanitized by `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` § `<output_format>`), and `commands/steps/keepalive.md` step 2 prints that remainder and reconciles each list against its own count. The write rides the already-granted `Bash(python3:*)`, and `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` requires the new keys so a leg that stops emitting them fails. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `scripts/check-handback-keys.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_handback_keys.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the keep-alive leg's two population walks no longer escalate a permission prompt, so the delegated leg can run unattended.** `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md` enumerated topics and tasks with an inline `for f in …; do … done`, and the agent's `allowed-tools` grants fixed command **prefixes** only (`python3`, `vault-cli`, `grep`, …) — a `for` loop matches none of them, so every round of a leg whose entire purpose is unattended operation parked on a prompt. Each loop is now one `python3 -c` snippet under the already-granted `Bash(python3:*)`: same population (every `*.md` under the resolved directory carrying a non-empty `claude_session_id`), same per-item script invocation and arguments, same printed columns (`name<TAB>`, then the script's first two lines each tab-terminated). The three peer reader agents never used shell loops. `commands/steps/keepalive.md`'s header comment also drops its stale `/<plugin>:steps:<name>` spelling for `/<plugin>:steps/<name>`, matching `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`. Change set: `agents/fleet-keepalive-actor.md`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: report the three worker populations and their total — `worker-sessions.py --populations` and the server's `workerPopulations()` split the same ledger-and-liveness join into managers / interactive / headless, so the number the caps act on is visible rather than inferred. Managers come from the ledger's own `role: manager` records (`fleet-board.py` emits display rows with no role and did not complete inside 90 s twice on this host), `mode` decides interactive vs headless with an absent `mode` reading as interactive, and the ghost rule and auto-resume exclusion apply to the split exactly as they do to the count
- feat: **the approved-not-started reading splits its total into the two populations that need opposite levers.** `approved, not started: N` was one number over rows nobody ever opened and rows whose worker died — measured 2026-10-30, 81 of 122 were orphans, and the round could act on neither as a class. A second line now prints `never started: N → open · orphaned: M → reopen`: *never started* is a row with no `claude_session_id`, *orphaned* one whose id names a session that is not live. Each is counted on its own predicate, never as the other's remainder, so a row classified as neither shows as a mismatch instead of vanishing into a derived number; a live-owned row counts in neither, exactly as before. An unreadable registry prints `unknown` for both. The head line is byte-identical, the over-tick rows name their class, and `--json` gains `never_started`, `orphaned` and a per-row `class`. `commands/steps/readings.md` § The approved-not-started reading names both levers and states which legs act on them today — the open leg ranks both classes (resuming an orphan's recorded session), and the reopen rule (`commands/steps/keepalive.md` § Resume a worker whose session died) is the second path for orphaned rows. `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md` describes the second line too. Change set: `scripts/approved-not-started.py`, `scripts/tests/test_approved_not_started.py`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **`/supervisor:fleet-triage` can wake a live TAB session that is idle at the prompt, through a guarded pane wake (#692).** A cross-session `SendMessage` never reaches such a session: it waits for a turn that never comes (measured 2026-11-08, 16 of 16 nudges unread). `send_agent_message` reaches only roster tab workers (4 of 39 live panes). The new `scripts/pane-wake.py` joins session → pid → tty → pane as the reload lever does. It runs dry by default and refuses this session, the Fleet Manager, any `Fleet Pusher` session and any session with an open gate. It types only into panes that `scripts/pane-wake-guard.py` clears: it skips a spinner, a selection or permission modal, a `❯ 1.` option row, a `👤 You:` ask, typed text, a machine wait and an unclassifiable read. A dim next-prompt suggestion counts as empty. The guard re-checks after activation, and the wake reports `delivered` only when the composer clears and a spinner appears; otherwise it reports `unverified`. The run report gains a wake-path field (`pane:<resolution>` / `sendmessage` / `none:<reason>`), and Step 6 counts a tab row as driven only on `pane:*`. The wake targets the worker's owning manager when that manager is an idle tab session, keeping the "never drive another manager's worker" rule. It falls back to the worker only on a measured no-live-manager.
- feat: **`fleet-triage` runs in a dedicated `Fleet Pusher` session, never in the Fleet Manager.** `docs/session-tiers.md` names Fleet Pusher as its tier and moves the Fleet Manager to the banned column, because a fleet-wide pass fills the manager's context. `fleet-triage-tier.md` classifies a `Fleet Pusher` session as never closed, nudged or woken.
- fix: **`fleet-triage-state.md` no longer says an idle session wakes on a cross-session `SendMessage`.** § 3.4b's measurement was taken on headless sessions only.

- docs: **the keep-alive contract has one home — `docs/session-tiers.md` § Keep-alive.** That section holds:
  - a seven-row chain table: level, opened by, kept alive by, mechanism, guard;
  - the definition of an owner: a session reading `LIVE` and a loop reading `OK` or `STALE`;
  - the rule that a session no tier keeps is adopted or closed;
  - three invariants: restart is not open; the flag gates an open, never a restart; the per-topic N gates a start, never a resume.

  `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `agents/topic-drive.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md` and the `fleet-`/`topic-`/`worker-keepalive.py` docstrings now point there instead of restating pieces of it. The section also names two known gaps: no leg keeps a topic-less goal's manager alive, and `worker-keepalive.py` counts a stood-down manager as an owner. No behaviour changes.

- feat: **the `Stop` closer lint now blocks a `🟡 WAITING` closer whose `⏰ Ends:` waits on something that dies with the session.** Measured 2026-11-08, pane 619: `⏰ Ends: chunk 7a and 7c completion notices (background agents)` — the previous session had ended, both agents died with it, and the worker sat 23 minutes at an empty prompt on a task unmoved for 28 hours, found only by a human reading the pane. `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py` exits 2 when `⏰ Ends:` names one of five session-bound classes (background sub-agent, Monitor, `run_in_background` shell, `ScheduleWakeup`, `CronCreate`) **and** no durable observable (a file, a 32-hex card id, a `defer_date` / ISO date, or an external probe such as a PR, CI, `gh`, `kubectl` or a pod). ⚠️ The observable is banned, not the mechanism: `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` § Async State Closer requires the watcher in the `(mechanism, id)` slot and `/github-pr-watch` is itself a background shell, so a literal ban would block every compliant PR-watch closer (operator decision 2026-11-08, "observable only"). The check runs before the gate checks, because the shape ends `👤 You: nothing`, which they read as `not-a-gate`; it reads `⏰ Ends:` from the same message through the new `reap-closer.py` `last_closer_and_ends` — one scanner, never a second. A watcher's own timeout (`times out ~18:40`) is not a clock observable. Each record gains `class` and `phrase`, null unless the check fired. Tests: five positive fixtures (one per class, pane 619's line verbatim first) and six negatives, each carrying a session-bound watcher in its slot so only the durable observable keeps it clean.

## v0.254.1

- fix: **the plugin no longer declares the stdio `supervisor` MCP server, so a plugin auto-update stops resurrecting a per-session `supervisor.mjs`.** `.mcp.json` held only `mcpServers.supervisor` (`python3 scripts/mcp-start.py`), so it is deleted rather than left as an empty `mcpServers`. The long-running daemon (`127.0.0.1:18090/supervisor/mcp`) serves the same nine tools over HTTP, and sessions reach it through an `http` entry in their own `--mcp-config`. `scripts/mcp-start.py` stays for a self-registered stdio entry (and `restart-worker.py` still matches on it). `make check` now fails if `.mcp.json` reappears. Change set: `.mcp.json`, `Makefile`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.254.0

- feat: **a manager-spawned worker's plan gate is answered by the round instead of auto-denying at 15 minutes.** `commands/steps/own-parks.md` answered only class-B routine parks, and its class table named class C — a worker's `AskUserQuestion` — as the operator's. An `AskUserQuestion` park never carries an `allowlist_entry`, so the scoped wrangler escalated it, the escalation sat, and the 15-minute timeout then auto-denied it: measured 2026-11-07, a fleet manager's worker hit a plan gate, nobody answered, and its task file was found untouched twenty minutes later. The step now **routes** every class-C park — its report gains `routed <routed> (class C → step 7)` with each park's `request_id`, worker label and question, and the answered count drops its hardcoded `(class B)` and `<c>`/`<co>` become `<routed>`/`<c-operator>` — and step 7 (`commands/steps/worker-channels.md`) answers each routed id with `Manager answer, via supervisor:`. The class boundary is unchanged where it matters: a question that asks to widen the task's scope, or that names a production-touching or irreversible action, still escalates to the operator, and the new § *Is the class-C question the manager's?* names the test. Class D and E are untouched.

## v0.253.1

- fix: **step 5d reads the park field that actually holds headless parks — `pending_question`, not `pending_permissions`.** `commands/steps/own-parks.md` told the round to treat every `list_agents` row whose `pending_permissions` is `[]` as "nothing parked". For a headless worker that field is *always* `[]`: the park is carried as `pending_question` — `{request_id, kind, tool_name, tool_use_id, input}` — so the step concluded the fleet was clean and answered nothing while all 36 stayed parked. Measured 2026-11-08 on a Fleet Manager roster of **101 rows**: **0** non-empty `pending_permissions`, **36** `pending_question` parks of kind `permission`. The step had already quoted the rule it then broke — rules.md, *"An empty `pending_permissions` is not absence of a gate"*. The step now reads both fields and **splits them by whether they carry an `allowlist_entry`** — the only signal the decision policy reads. `pending_permissions` rows carry one, so their ids go to the wrangler as before; `pending_question` rows do not (`pendingQuestionOf` returns the wrapper's raw question, `server/wrapper-stream.mjs:281`), so they are **surfaced and escalated, never dispatched for an answer** — an allowlisted wrapper park is already answered upstream by `server/wrapper-policy.mjs`'s `decide()` poll, and what reaches this step is what that deliberately left. It skips only when **both** fields are empty, treats `{unreadable: true}` as unknown rather than absence (skip line: `wrapper session list unreadable`), and the ownership check remains what bounds it, since a `rehydrated` row's `pending_permissions` is `[]` while its `pending_question` is not. Change set: `commands/steps/own-parks.md`, `agents/goal-wrangler.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. This makes the parks **visible**; it does not make them answered — that is `wrapper-policy.mjs`'s path, and the 36 measured parks are the ones it escalated.

## v0.253.0

- feat: **the fleet round opens flagged goal and topic managers, not only flagged tasks.** `/supervisor:open --flagged` selects **tasks only**, and `fleet-drive.md` removed the manager-proposal row on 2026-10-29, so a flagged goal or topic with no live manager had no executor at all: measured 2026-11-08, 12 goals carried `flag: true` at `status: in_progress`, 6 had a live manager and 6 did not, and no leg of the round surfaced those 6 — the operator opened them by hand. The operator's ruling is that a flag means *open me* at every tier. New `scripts/flagged-subjects.py` computes the selection: flagged goals (`status: in_progress`) and flagged topics (non-terminal), no live manager, no open `fleet-propose-manager` / `fleet-approve-manager` card, `phase: todo` reported as `WAITING` rather than opened, and the whole set bounded by `hard cap − live managers` (both numbers imported from `propose-managers.py`'s `resolve_cap()` / `live_manager_labels()` over one board read — never restated). **"Live manager" is the session registry**, `session-liveness.py --list --json` matched on `name` **and** `formerNames` through `fleet-board.py`'s own normalizer, plus the two-hop goal→topic chain; the page `claude_session_id` is the second answer only, where `ABSENT` stays a resume and `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS` is reported and never opened. Ordering is topics then goals (a manager slot spent higher covers more subjects), goals ranked by `priority` then approved live work; a goal whose topic opens in the same run is `COVERED`, not double-managed. Tasks are absent from the script by design — they spend the worker cap, a different budget. `free is None` is UNLIMITED (the operator's `spawn.maxManagers: 0` off switch), never zero. Unreadable vault set, registry, cap, live-manager count, attention store or a vault's goals/topics directory exits **2 REFUSED**, never "nothing to open" — an unreadable directory returns `None`, never `[]`, so a read failure cannot render as a clean round. `commands/steps/flagged-open.md` gains **Step 5c.1** to run the selector and act on `OPEN` lines only, through `/supervisor:open --goal` / `--topic`; the "no fleet-tier leg opens a flagged goal manager today" paragraph is corrected in place, and `rules.md` § No manager-proposal row, `commands/read-guides.md` and `docs/session-tiers.md` now say proposing is forbidden while opening a flag is rule 2. Change set: `scripts/flagged-subjects.py`, `scripts/tests/test_flagged_subjects.py`, `commands/steps/flagged-open.md`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `commands/read-guides.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.252.3

- fix: **the sleep's wake instruction no longer silently becomes an hourly wake.** `commands/steps/round-quiet.md` told the round to *"arm `ScheduleWakeup` with a delay of `<instant> − now`"* — but `ScheduleWakeup` clamps `delaySeconds` to **[60, 3600]**, and a sleep is usually hours or a day out. The documented instruction therefore did not sleep the subject; it woke it **every hour** across the whole gap, and each of those rounds re-read the same `sleep-until` and re-slept — precisely the tick-burn the sleep verdict exists to remove, produced by the instruction meant to prevent it. Measured 2026-11-08 on the Several Clusters Manager: a 69 426 s (~19 h) sleep became an hourly wake until a one-shot `CronCreate` (`36 12 9 11 *`) was armed instead. The bullet now branches on distance: `ScheduleWakeup` at or under 3600 s, otherwise a one-shot `CronCreate` pinned to the instant's minute (`recurring: false`) — the spelling `commands/goal-loop.md` § step 3 already names for *"the tick must land on a wall-clock minute"*. Every date-gated subject with a `defer_date` more than an hour out hits this, which is most of them. Change set: `commands/steps/round-quiet.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.252.2

- fix: count live workers in both directions — the fleet counter unions the identity registry in (a headless `claude --print` worker holds a live pid but stamps no heartbeat, so it was dropped: 44 live sessions counted as 10) and drops ghost rows (a `live: true` row with no `state` that the store keeps serving after its session exited: the cap read 51 against 49 real processes)
- fix: **one failed attention-store read no longer blanks the whole feed family in a manager round's readings.** `round-readings.py` recovered the owner of each `[ hst]` feed row by re-reading `http://localhost:18080/api/1.0/attention` with a 3 s timeout, and `gate-owner-filter.headless_items` returns `[]` both for an unreachable store and for no match — so one transient timeout turned every unresolved key into `unread` for **every** row. Measured 2026-11-08 05:06:29Z: `gated=0 · clear=0 · unread=11` for 11 rows while the caller's own `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you` returned `Needs you (25)` exit 0 in the same minute; a re-run 39 s later read `gated=1 · clear=10 · unread=0`. Three changes close it. (1) `who-needs-me.py` renders `owner: <session id>` on every pane-less row — the row already knows it, so the readings leg no longer needs a second store read at all; a store read remains only as a fallback for an older feed, through the new `headless_items_checked`, which returns the error beside the records. (2) `feed_panes` retries a non-zero exit or timeout once and then reports the failure as a named `probes` entry instead of a verdict about the rows. (3) The feed family is now `read` / `partial` / `unread`: `partial` keeps every positively-gated row `gated` and marks only the rows that could own an unattributed gate `unread`, and the document carries `feed_counts`, counted from the rows themselves, so `gated + clear + unread == rows` by construction. (4) The receipt's `readings_shape` accepts `partial` — without that the new state made every degraded round read `INCOMPLETE ROUND — skipped: readings` and recorded false debt for a reading that had run. (5) `who-needs-me.py` collapses whitespace in the rendered name and detail, so a payload newline can no longer forge a continuation line such as `owner:` for a row that does not own it. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `agents/round-readings-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.252.1

- fix: **the keep-alive's `SLEEPING` line names the instant, not just the state.** `manager-liveness.py --state` prints `<STATE>\t<slug>\t<detail>` and the detail is the only place a reader learns *what* a state is waiting for — for a sleep it carries `until <ISO> (<n>s remaining)`. `topic-keepalive.py` parsed field 0 and dropped the rest, so a date-gated subject was reported as `cadence SLEEPING — waiting on its own defer_date, not a lapse` with no date: the one fact that makes a sleep actionable was the one the line omitted, and an operator could not tell a sleep ending in an hour from one ending in a week. New `cadence_state_detail()` returns `(state, detail)` from the same single bounded probe and `cadence()` becomes a thin wrapper over its first field, so every existing caller and test keeps its contract. Change set: `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_keepalive.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.252.0

- feat: **`attention-ask.py` derives `producer_kind` from the card's `producer_id` instead of defaulting every manager's card to `session`.** The board's default view filters on `producer_kind=manager` (operator ruling `7ca5385d5d226b1463778ac2e5f18b56`), and the field was written only where a caller remembered `--producer-kind manager` — measured 2026-11-07, 6–13 `manager` cards against ~1000 in the store, so the view the operator triages was near-empty. A new `scripts/session-role.py` resolves the session's **declared** role from two sources, ledger first: the supervisor session ledger's explicit `role` field, then the `claude_session_id:` stamp a manager writes on its own goal/topic page. The second source is load-bearing, not a nicety — measured 2026-11-07 on the live fleet, only **6 of 11** manager sessions had a ledger row (hand-started and `/supervisor:open` wezterm-branch managers write none), and the page stamp lifts coverage to **9 of 11 with zero false positives**. `attention-ask.py`'s `post` / `post-batch` call it with the payload's `producer_id`. `manager` is **declared, never derived** (`docs/role-mode-matrix.md` Rule 1): the resolver never infers a manager from absence-from-ledger, from a session name or from its terminal colour, and an explicit non-`session` `--producer-kind` (`cron`, `agent`, `dark-factory`) still wins. Every failure — unreachable ledger, 404, malformed row, empty id — resolves `session`, the pre-change value, so a store outage cannot relabel a card as a manager's. Keyed on `producer_id`, not `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, because `manager-attention-watch.py` posts on behalf of a parked worker: the caller is a manager and the card must read `session`. The page source matches the `claude_session_id:` **frontmatter field**, never a bare substring: a page that merely lists a worker's id in `metrics_sessions` or a Progress line must not label that worker `manager`, which is the inverse of the property this module protects. Change set: `scripts/session-role.py`, `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_role.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.251.5

- fix: **`test_permission_answer_poll` stops inheriting the host's worker mode, so `make precommit` is no longer red inside a headless supervised session.** `permission-answer-poll.py` reads `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` at the top of `main()` — the composition root, and the right place for it — so the suite driving `main()` answered for the *host* rather than for the code. Inside a headless worker the ambient value is `headless`, the three cases that expect the polling path took the early exit instead, and `test_plugin_version_census` went red beside them as a cascade: the failing poll cases leave `self.m.time.sleep` patched on the real `time` module (`test_permission_answer_poll.py:357`) and the census reload leg sleeps. Measured 2026-11-08 in one process: **74 tests, 7 red** with the ambient environment, **74 green** with `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` unset; the one-variable bisect — unset only it → green, keep only it → red — names it. The fix is a new `scripts/tests/worker_mode_isolation.py`, one assignment in one module imported for its side effect, the idiom `start_cache_isolation.py` and `fleet_sessions_isolation.py` already establish. It pins `interactive`, the documented default (`SPAWN_MODES` in `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, whose fallback returns `{ mode: 'interactive' }`) — which is also why CI never saw it. The two cases that need the other value keep injecting it themselves. Production is untouched. Change set: `scripts/tests/worker_mode_isolation.py`, `scripts/tests/test_permission_answer_poll.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.251.4

- chore: restate the `do-not-merge` gate message to name its bypass actors — the Admin repository role and Integration `3907226`, both `bypass_mode: always` in the `master-protection` ruleset — instead of asserting an unqualified block

## v0.251.3

- fix: **the drive leg's `CLOSERS` source now covers headless sessions, so a pane-less row is classifiable instead of permanently `unverifiable`.** `who-needs-me.py --section last-closers` built its population from the attention store's own records, so a session that raised no gate had no row at all — and step 4a fail-closes a candidate with no row, which made every headless worker unverifiable on every round. Measured 2026-11-07: **237 of 242** drive candidates carried no pane, **232** landed `unverifiable`, and only 14 reached the closer probe. The population is now the **heartbeat store** (`session-liveness.py --list`, union with the attention store, prefix-deduped in both directions) — the registry cannot see an in-process SDK headless worker at all — and the class is read from the **transcript**, which needs no pane; a pane-less row renders `hst` and the header prints `Last closers (<N>, headless=<H>)`. `agents/fleet-drive.md` carries the count as `headless=<H>` on the payload header and the counts line, and `docs/closer-classes.md` § The channel is pane-free records the source and the `hst` meaning.

## v0.251.2

- fix: a `claude-wrapper` restart no longer kills a streaming headless worker. The event stream
  reconnects instead of failing: every SSE frame's `id:` is tracked and sent back as
  `Last-Event-ID`, so the retry resumes at exactly the event the client stopped at, with
  exponential backoff (100 ms → 5 s, 24 attempts, 2 min cap) sized to ride out a daemon restart.
  A refused connection is treated as a restart rather than a failure. A `409` is never retried —
  it means the wrapper no longer retains that resume point, and serving a different one would
  leave an invisible hole in the transcript, so it raises `StreamGapError` instead. The backoff
  budget is **per outage, not per stream** — a parked or long-running worker whose stream has
  been open for hours still reconnects, because the clock starts when a connection fails and
  resets only on real progress (a new event id), so a wrapper stuck re-sending one event cannot
  loop forever. Every gap class — `StreamLaggedError`, `StreamGapError`, `StreamExhaustedError`
  (retry budget spent) and `StreamSessionGoneError` (a `404`/`410` mid-stream) — carries a `gap`
  marker, and `runAgentLoop` settles `error` on that marker rather than on a list of class
  names, so a captured mid-turn `result` can never settle a holed transcript `done`. The
  end-of-stream confirmation read is guarded too: a failed read there is a gap, not an
  unconfirmed `done`. A connection that carried frames but no `id:` fails loudly instead of
  reconnecting blind and duplicating the transcript — checked per connection, so a successor
  that starts serving frames without ids is caught as well. Previously undici's `TypeError: terminated` reached `runAgentLoop`'s catch and the
  worker settled `error` while its child was still generating.

## v0.251.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:open`'s RESUME branch picks its launcher by running code, not by reading prose.** § 3.1 exec'd `"<claude_script>" --resume` and left the value to a sentence in the same file (*"apply the rule yourself: `vault-cli task get … launcher`"*), so whichever session ran the resume chose the launcher and nothing checked the result. Measured 2026-11-07: two `role: agent` rows with no `launcher:` were resumed as `cc-private-claude --resume … /color cyan` because their resumer ran on Opus. New `scripts/resolve-launcher.py` prints `<launcher>\t<source>\t<role>\t<chip>` from `restart-worker.py`'s `resolve_launcher` — the same rule the restart path uses, so the two cannot disagree — and the call site now uses both fields. `resolve_launcher` also gains the `role: human` arm (`_human_launcher`), a Python port of `applyHumanLauncherRule`: the `-claude` sibling ahead of a configured `manager_launcher`, then the manager chain, with a task's own `launcher:` still winning. The chip follows the ROLE through the published role map rather than defaulting to the caller's colour. ⚠️ **A manager resume is detected first** (`restart-worker.py` `resume_role` → `session_subject`): a manager session carries no task page, so a claimant-only lookup would have read it as a worker and resumed it on the worker default — the same defect one layer down. It reads the goal/topic page's `claude_session_id`, never the live session registry: the registry is deleted when a session exits, and a manager only reaches the resume path once it has exited, so a registry-based lookup answers "not a manager" exactly where the resume happens. The chip then follows `manager` → `orange`; a manager with no goal or topic page (the root) still reads as `agent` for the chip, since the page is the only source that survives an exited session. `_human_launcher`'s last rung is the vault's `claude_script`, never the worker default — matrix Rule 4 and `applyHumanLauncherRule` both end there, and returning the worker resolution would put a human row on `-deepseek` in a vault that configures `worker_launcher` but has no `-claude` sibling and no `manager_launcher`. `session_subject` refuses two pages claiming one session id, the same guard `session_claimant` carries. ⚠️ **Both values are shape-checked before they reach the spawn**: the launcher against the same plain-path rule `resolve_launcher` applies (the `SUPERVISOR_LAUNCHER` arm skips it), and the chip against the eight colours `/color` accepts — a corrupt role map carrying shell text would otherwise be re-parsed by the `bash -lc` string that re-emits it. ⚠️ **The emitted `role` is narrowed to the three known values** — it is author-controlled frontmatter, and the output is TAB-DELIMITED and split with `cut -f4` at the call site, so a tab in a `role:` would shift every field after it and hand that text to `$CHIP`. `session_subject` matches a page's whole id set (`claude_session_id` plus `metrics_sessions`), the same rule `session_claimant` already carried. `commands/open.md` § 3.1's precedence prose is deleted and replaced by a pointer to `docs/restart-worker.md`; the body grew 78389 → 79230 bytes and its `scripts/budget-baseline.json` entry was raised deliberately. Change set: `scripts/resolve-launcher.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_resolve_launcher.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `docs/role-mode-matrix.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **every open PR whose branch is behind master is now rebased in a scratch worktree and handed to the operator as one push line — never merged, never pushed by the agent.** Measured 2026-11-06 in `bborbe/claude-supervisor`: 8 of 12 open PRs DIRTY, 4 of them APPROVED, and four merges that evening re-broke the set four times, because each release cut renames `## Unreleased` and re-conflicts every other open branch. `scripts/rebase-agent.py` sweeps an explicit two-repo set (`claude-supervisor`, `vault-cli`) — not "every repo", since the conflict class exists only where a release cut renames a heading that open branches also edit — and for each candidate creates a **detached** worktree at a temp path (no branch ref exists to damage, the main worktree is never entered), rebases onto `origin/master`, and either emits a push line mapping a throwaway `refs/rebase-agent/pr/<n>` onto the live branch under `--force-with-lease` or a failure report. Every value in that line is shell-quoted, since the operator pastes it into a shell and the branch name comes from the PR; a PR whose head lives in a fork is refused rather than handed a line that would push to the wrong remote. ⚠️ **The mechanism is the trap, not the conflict**: `git merge origin/master` writes a merge commit, and measured on `#652` that state was APPROVED, `MERGEABLE`/`CLEAN`, and would have merged cleanly while permanently failing the merge train's linearity probe — so the linearity gate is the negative control, and `#664` and `#653` each came out linear from a branch that carried a merge commit. A conflict in any path other than `CHANGELOG.md` is refused and named (a genuine content conflict is a worker's); a `CHANGELOG.md`-only conflict is resolved deterministically from the branch's own added bullets, and refused when the branch does more than add bullets. Seven gates block rather than warn — `git log --merges` empty, the `^[-+]## ` heading guard, the `^--[^-]` dropped-bullet probe, and the `awk` placement assertion as the **authority** (an empty heading-grep is ambiguous and is never read as a pass), plus `grep -c '^## Unreleased$'` printing 1 because a duplicate heading reads as one to the heading probes, the park size limit, and a no-op guard. `origin/master` is fetched before every sweep, since the rebase and the behind-count both read the local remote-tracking ref. Exercised on the live queue 2026-11-07: 6 push lines, 4 refusals, including `vault-cli#354` refused on a real content conflict in `scripts/session-self-exit.py` and `#660` refused after a **clean** rebase silently lost `v0.250.2`/`v0.250.1`. Poll trigger is `launchd/com.bborbe.rebase-agent.plist` at 900 s, since "detected within one poll interval with no human starting the run" cannot be met by a slash command. Rule: `docs/rebase-agent.md`; tests: `scripts/tests/test_rebase_agent.py` (14 cases, each end-to-end case on a throwaway repo). Prod-pin PRs are refused before any rebase — that approval is the operator's.

## v0.251.0

- feat: **a date-gated manager subject sleeps to its earliest `defer_date` instead of standing down.** `manager-quiet.py` gained a third verdict: when every quiet row is quiet *for a future `defer_date`* and none is runnable, it prints `sleep-until` with the earliest instant instead of `stood-down`. Standing such a subject down put a human in the loop every morning — measured 2026-11-07, the Several Clusters Manager was re-armed by hand twice in one day, fired one round, and stood itself down again. `manager-liveness.py` gained a matching fifth state: `--sleep --until <ISO>` writes `<slug>.sleeping`, `--state` reads `SLEEPING` while the instant is future (never `STALE`, which the deliberately frozen cadence would otherwise produce), a **passed** instant falls through to the normal arithmetic so a manager that failed to wake still reads `STALE`, and `--arm` clears the marker. `round-quiet.md` arms the wakeup at that instant instead of calling `/supervisor:stop`, and every liveness consumer was taught the new state — `topic-keepalive.py`, `crashed-managers.py`, `fleet-keeper.py`, `propose-managers.py` (a sleeping manager owns its subject, so it must suppress a manager proposal), `round-liveness.md` (arming clears `.sleeping`, so a sleeping subject must not be armed), `docs/sweep-output.md` (a `sleeping` disposition ranking with `stood-down`), `drive-share.py`, the two topic-sweep readers and `keepalive.md`.

## v0.250.7

- fix: **the park nudge's duration is measured per watching episode, and suppression is keyed on the episode itself.** `watching_min` was published as the span from the session's *first* `🟡 WAITING` closer ever, so it never dropped and the suppression ledger's re-arm clause was dead: a second park in the same session was either suppressed for its whole duration, or nudged instantly with a lifetime figure while its panel read *"you have sat `watching` 300 min"*. `session-activity.py` (vault-local) now restarts the span at the first `🟡 WAITING` closer of the current episode and clears it on any panel naming another state. The reading also publishes that episode's timestamp as **`watching_since`**, and the ledger keys suppression on it — the duration comparison alone is not enough, because the round cadence is 15 minutes and rounds are routinely skipped, so a reading can land after one park ended and the next began with the duration never falling. A reading that carries no `watching_since` keeps the old duration fallback. Also: a `watching` row whose duration could not be measured is counted in the round's `<m> watching` tally (matching the reading's own `counts.watching`) and keeps its ledger entry rather than losing suppression across a measurement gap. Docs in `commands/steps/keepalive.md` and the script header say episode, not lifetime. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.6

- fix: **the park nudge reads the watching-state duration instead of transcript silence, so a session that re-arms its watcher is finally nudged.** `park-nudge.py` selected on `quiet_min` — mtime age — and a watcher re-arm writes to the transcript, so the clock reset while the wait continued. Measured 2026-11-06: a session carried **49 `🟡 WAITING` closers** across **3 h 34 m** with no terminal closer after, was re-armed **20+ times**, and read `quiet 12m` — comfortably inside the 15-minute allowance, so it was never nudged and `~/.claude/state/park-nudge/ledger.json` stayed `{}`. The selection now reads **`watching_min`**, the span from the session's first `🟡 WAITING` closer, published by `session-activity.py` (vault-local, not part of this plugin — the two halves land separately). A row with no `watching_min` is skipped rather than treated as 0. The emitted line keeps its `quiet <N>m` field name for parser compatibility, but N is now the state duration; the ledger's `last_quiet` key carries it too. Suppression is unchanged in shape — one nudge per watching episode, keyed on the session id, never on the duration. Change set: `scripts/park-nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/test_park_nudge.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/park-nudge-activity.json`, `commands/steps/keepalive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.5

- fix: **a successful spawn now invalidates the worker-count cache, so a failed liveness read after a spawn refuses instead of releasing the next spawn on a pre-spawn number.** The cache is written only by a successful live count, so a spawn that landed after the last count left a cached value that predated it; the next spawn whose live read failed decided on that value, and every further spawn in the same 60 s window was allowed on it — a burst during a store outage ran past the very ceiling that exists to bound it. `invalidateCountCache` (`server/worker-sessions.mjs`) deletes the file, and `spawn_agent` calls it on every non-error result (`server/supervisor.mjs`), covering the tab, headless, cluster and resume paths in one place. The fallback is unchanged where it was added for: a transient store failure with no spawn immediately before it still decides on a fresh count, labelled on stderr. Change set: `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.4

- fix: **a failed delivery's durable record now names its producer.** `--failed` accepts `--pid` and `--row-ts` — the sender's process id and the timestamp of the ledger row it wrote in the same run — and `read_pending` carries both through every rewrite. Without them a hand-written `liveness-pending.json` reproduces every field of a real failed send exactly, so "the record was written by the tick" could only be asserted, never shown. No behaviour change to the retry, the ack or the detection limb.

## v0.250.3

- fix: **a lapse report that cannot reach its transport is retried instead of being marked reported and forgotten.** `--check` wrote `notified[s] = now` at DETECTION time, so a send that failed left the entry current and every later poll was silent — the operator learned of the lapse only by noticing it, which is the one thing the goal's criterion excludes. Measured 2026-11-07 over `~/.claude/state/manager-liveness-loop/ledger.txt`: **43** `FAILED:` deliveries, none retried, 35 of them TeamVault credential timeouts and only 4 the powered-off endpoint the goal page recorded — so this was never a one-off. **Detection is untouched**: `classify()`, `observed_period()` and `SLACK` are unchanged and the limit is still `period + SLACK` (one observed period plus 180 s). What changes is the bookkeeping: an undelivered lapse lives in `liveness-pending.json`, is re-emitted on every poll — that re-emission IS the retry — and moves to `liveness-notified.json` only when the new `--delivered <slug>` verb acks it, which the sender calls only after the transport returned success. `--failed <slug> --error <text>` records the attempt count and the transport error, so the durable record says why it could not be sent. The ack stores the CROSSING time rather than the ack time, so a manager that re-armed while its report was owed still starts a NEW lapse on its next `STALE`. A lapse whose subject recovered before delivery reads `RECOVERED-PENDING` rather than `STALE` — a recovery does not cancel an unreported lapse. `RECOVERED` and the legacy-list upgrade path are unchanged.

## v0.250.2

- fix: **the fleet round now runs `fleet-legs.py --check` at its tail, so the `legs:` verdict item 14 specifies actually reaches a render.** `commands/steps/output-shape.md` item 14 names `scripts/fleet-legs.py --check` *"the mechanical guard"* and specifies its renders, but **nothing invoked it** — no router row, no leg, no tail — so the guard was correct and its verdict was never read. Measured 2026-11-07 on `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/2026-11-06T23-46Z.digest`: its `LEGS` section still held the sweep's seed, carried no `unactioned:` line, and `--check` exited **1** naming all three keys. `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md` gains a section that runs the check and branches on the **exit code**, never on the output, carrying that code to the `Legs` render at router row 20. ⚠️ **Every `--check` message is written to stderr** while only `legs ok: …` goes to stdout, so the section requires capturing both — a read of stdout alone hands item 14 an empty `<reason>`. The format strings keep their single home in item 14 and are not restated at the call site. Change set: `commands/steps/fleet-round-tail.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.250.1

- fix: **the manager allowlist no longer carries `uniq` or `diff`.** `server/manager-allowlist.json` is the file that decides which prompts a manager answers *on its own* instead of escalating, and its own note states the rule the worker policy was narrowed to in `v0.249.0` / `v0.250.0`: a whole-token prefix is listed only when **every** extension of it is read-only. Two entries broke it — `uniq IN OUT` writes `OUT`, an arbitrary-path write that bypasses the policy's `Write(cwd)` confinement, and `diff -l` / `--paginate` runs `pr(1)`. The worker tier lost both already; the manager tier was never touched, so `uniq /dev/null /etc/authorized_keys` and `diff --paginate a b` were auto-answered with no operator in the loop — strictly worse than the worker case, where an escalation at least parks the request with a human. Both entries are **removed** rather than special-cased: a prefix rule cannot exclude a positional or a flag. `docs/permission-allowlist.md` is updated in the same change (its *every extension* note now names all four failures), `server/manager-allowlist.test.mjs` pins both directions — `uniq /tmp/in /tmp/out` and `diff -l a b` refuse while `ls -la /tmp`, `cat f`, `grep -rn x .` and `jq -r .x f` still allow — and `make check-manager-allowlist` still reports the doc and the JSON agreeing.

## v0.250.0
