The class vocabulary, the routine-continue table and the join rule now live once in the new `docs/closer-classes.md`, and `agents/fleet-drive.md` points at it — the two agent bodies sat 2 and 7 bytes under the 30 KB body budget before this change, so the growth had to be paid for by moving the table out rather than by ratcheting the cap. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/closer-verdict.py`, `docs/closer-classes.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_verdict.py`.

## v0.214.3

- fix: **a session whose pane does not resolve is observed, not carded, and no longer consumes a cap slot.** Measured 2026-10-22: of 114 unverifiable rows at streak 2, 12 resolved a pane and 102 did not — so the drive leg raised a card that could only ever say "no pane resolves", which is nothing the operator can act on, and each one spent one of the round's three action slots on a row nobody could act on. `decide()` now returns a third outcome, `observation`, when no pane resolves: the row is still named in the `UNVERIFIABLE` block, contributes nothing to `NUDGES` or `ESCALATIONS`, and — because `plan()` resolves the pane before the cap slice — never reaches `max_actions`. ⚠️ **The observation is recorded in a NEW `unverifiable_observation` ledger field, never in `unverifiable_action`**: `drive-share.py`'s `action_taken()` counts any non-empty action marker as an action taken, so reusing that field would have inflated the detected→verified numerator by one on every pane-less row — the metric this rule exists to keep honest. Like the action marker it fires once per silence and is cleared with the streak, so a gone session is named once rather than every round. Unreadable panes and panes that render no closer still escalate, pinned by negative controls in the same replay. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a skipped stall incident is retired instead of staying pending forever, and the escalate leg says so when its liveness probe fails.** Three edges of the gone-session gate shipped with #506 left the same silent failure one surface out. (1) `cmd_escalate` skipped the gate for the whole run when `live_sessions()` returned `None` and wrote nothing to stderr, while `cmd_stall` warned — so a probe outage disabled the gate in the leg that actually POSTS the cards, and its only visible effect was a missing skip line, indistinguishable from "no gone sessions". Both legs now share one warning line. (2) A skipped incident stayed `pending` with no terminal stamp: `escalate` re-listed it, re-paid the liveness probe and left it in `share`'s all-time denominator with no possible numerator, permanently depressing the one number the ledger reports. It now reaches the terminal `skipped-gone`, which `prune` retires on the same clock as any other terminal outcome and `share` EXCLUDES from the denominator (reported beside it instead) — a skipped incident is one that could not be resolved, not one that was not. (3) An empty-but-successful probe (`[]` at exit 0) returned an empty set, so every eligible row read as gone and the stall class went quiet behind a plausible skip line; an empty read and a `degraded=` count above zero now both read as unavailable and fail OPEN, the same direction as an outage. ⚠️ **A malformed `sessionId` fails open rather than aborting the round** — `.lower()` on a non-string raised `AttributeError` and killed the leg, so ids are guarded and an unjudgeable id escalates instead of being retired. ⚠️ **The skip line is bounded** to a count plus five names and a `… (+N more)` remainder: the drive ledger keeps a row for every session that was ever unverifiable, and a 120-gone-row round rendered one unbounded line. ⚠️ **A `--dry-run` prints `would retire`, never `retired`**, matching `would escalate` and `would close` — a dry run that claimed the state change would be indistinguishable from a real one in its own output. The retirement also records the **probe's** timestamp, not the write's, so a session that restarted in between is explainable from the record. Change set: `scripts/incident-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_incident_ledger.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a session whose pane does not resolve is observed, not carded, and no longer consumes a cap slot.** Measured 2026-10-22: of 114 unverifiable rows at streak 2, 12 resolved a pane and 102 did not — so the drive leg raised a card that could only ever say "no pane resolves", which is nothing the operator can act on, and each one spent one of the round's three action slots on a row nobody could act on. `decide()` now returns a third outcome, `observation`, when no pane resolves: the row is still named in the `UNVERIFIABLE` block, contributes nothing to `NUDGES` or `ESCALATIONS`, and — because `plan()` resolves the pane before the cap slice — never reaches `max_actions`. ⚠️ **The observation is recorded in a NEW `unverifiable_observation` ledger field, never in `unverifiable_action`**: `drive-share.py`'s `action_taken()` counts any non-empty action marker as an action taken, so reusing that field would have inflated the detected→verified numerator by one on every pane-less row — the metric this rule exists to keep honest. Like the action marker it fires once per silence and is cleared with the streak, so a gone session is named once rather than every round. Unreadable panes and panes that render no closer still escalate, pinned by negative controls in the same replay. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.214.2

- docs: **the README now says where a headless worker's process lives, because it decides who can answer its prompts.** A park belongs to whoever holds the child's stdin; that is now `claude-wrapper`, so the prompt is released over its REST route and the wrapper session id names the target. The section also states the cost plainly — the policy tier (`decide()`, the manager allowlist, the carried decision, the 15-minute deny) does not run for these workers, and a spawn reply carries a `policy_gap` naming each one as not-run, so **a successful spawn is not a policed spawn**. `pending_question` / `wrapper_session_id` are documented as status fields, never gates.

## v0.214.1

- fix: **the share reads BOTH live digest renders, so the compact one no longer contributes zero rows.** The digest has two row shapes in production at once and they differ in the separator, not in an optional column: `⚙ exactly-once-fix [79c6d892] · shell · 9h · parked · Exactly-Once Still Fails…` separates cells with `·`, while `Agents Spawn and Finish Withou [c328d8e3] waiting 6h lapsed — b— http://…` truncates the name and drops every `·`. The parser split only on `·`, got one part, skipped each row as too short, and returned **0 rows with 0 unparsed** — a silent zero indistinguishable from a fleet with nothing parked, and therefore invisible to the warning that exists to make exactly this loud. Measured 2026-10-29: rounds `05-29-16Z` and `06-01-14Z` were both that shape, and `06-01-14Z` printed `4/50` where the digest and ledger together hold **63** — short by the 13 digest-only parked rows. The row is now split into cells by whichever separator the render uses (`row_cells`), ` || ` is split first because the compact render joins several rows onto one line, and the class is the first recognised token among the **three** cells after the `[sid]` — bounded, because scanning further walks into the task title and reads `Parked Ideas.md` as a parked row. The `.md` guard survives the render change: a task basename is still never the class. `class_index` is replaced rather than extended — its two hardcoded positions were the enumerated-shape assumption this render broke a third time. Verified against twelve consecutive live digests: `unparsed` is 0 everywhere the render is complete, and `06-01-14Z` now reproduces the hand-count exactly at `4/63`. Change set: `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.214.0

- feat: **a wrapper-spawned headless worker's prompt is answerable over REST, starting with the event-stream adapter.** `server/wrapper-stream.mjs` reads a `claude-wrapper` child's `GET /api/1.0/sessions/{id}/events` stream as the same `AsyncIterable` of SDK messages `runAgentLoop` already consumes, so the loop, the ledger, the heartbeat and the resume guards stay unchanged and only the source of `q` moves. ⚠️ **The end of the stream is not one condition**: the wrapper ends the response both when the child exits and when a subscriber falls far enough behind that the stream stopped being a faithful account of it, while the loop settles a worker as `done` the moment iteration ends — so a lag mistaken for an exit marks a still-generating worker finished. The adapter therefore ignores the sentinel's silence and asks the wrapper's own session record, throwing `StreamLaggedError` for any state that is not `exited`. ⚠️ **Every wrapper call carries a timeout** (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS`): without one a wrapper that accepts the connection and never answers hangs a spawn or a status sweep indefinitely, which is worse than the refusal it replaces. ⚠️ **A refused opening turn reaps its child** — the wrapper has no `prompt` field, so the turn is a second call, and a failure there used to leave a live process holding stdin that looked exactly like a thinking worker. Change set: `server/wrapper-stream.mjs`, `server/wrapper-stream.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **a headless worker is spawned through `claude-wrapper`, so its permission prompt parks with a process that can actually be answered.** The SDK `query()` path parks into this server's own `pending` Map, which is a dead end the moment the park belongs to another manager's server; the wrapper owns the child's stdin, so the same park becomes reachable over REST. The spawn now posts to the wrapper with `cwd`, `env`, `permission_mode`, `mcp_configs` and `resume`, delivers the opening turn as a `POST /message` (the wrapper has no `prompt` field, so a caller that only streams gets a child that initialises and idles forever), and records `wrapper_session_id` on the agent. ⚠️ **Four options the SDK path carried are NOT carried here, and the loss is reported rather than hidden**: the policy tier (`decide()`, the manager allowlist, the carried decision and the 15-minute deny) has no CLI equivalent because the wrapper owns the single `--permission-prompt-tool`, so a spawn replies with a `policy_gap` naming all four as not-run, the shipping settings it would have applied, and the REST route that answers instead. A green spawn is therefore not a spawn under policy — it is a spawn whose policy lives in the wrapper. `settingSources` is deliberately absent: the CLI loads user/project/local by default, so passing it would be a no-op dressed as a decision.

- feat: **`answer_permission` reaches a wrapper-backed park by request id.** The id stays authoritative and the wrapper's LIVE session record is read on each call, so a worker that unparked and re-parked between a status read and the answer gets the wrapper's own `409` and nothing lands. ⚠️ **The refusal now names both sources it consulted** (`pending`, `wrapper`): a wrapper outage and a genuinely foreign park used to produce the same sentence, and the difference is the one a manager needs.

- fix: **`SUPERVISOR_WRAPPER_URL` is refused unless it points at loopback.** The wrapper's API is unauthenticated by design, and a spawn posts the whole inherited environment — `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` and any credential riding in it — to that URL in cleartext on every spawn. A non-loopback value therefore made the spawn/message/answer control plane an unauthenticated endpoint that receives the fleet's credentials. The refusal is **returned** on `config.wrapperUrlRefusal` rather than thrown at import — one mistyped variable must not make the module unimportable and take tab workers and the attention surface down with it — and the spawn refuses and says why. The accept/reject matrix is pinned by tests, including the userinfo (`localhost@evil.com`) and out-of-range-octet (`127.0.0.256`) shapes a naive string check would accept. It returns the URL's **origin**, not the trimmed string: a value carrying a path, query or fragment passed the host check and was then used verbatim, so `baseOf()` built `/base/api/1.0/...` and every wrapper call 404'd silently. The refusal text names the host and never echoes the raw value, so a userinfo credential cannot reach the spawn reply or the log.

- feat: **`pending_question` and `wrapper_session_id` on `agent_status` and `list_agents`.** Without the first a manager cannot obtain the request id `answer_permission` takes, so the answer path above was unreachable from outside. It is read from the wrapper's own live record — ONE `GET /api/1.0/sessions` per roster, skipped entirely when no row is wrapper-backed, never one read per worker. ⚠️ **Three states, not two**: `null` for a worker that is not wrapper-backed, the question object when parked, and `{unreadable: true}` when a wrapper-backed worker's wrapper could not be read — collapsing the last into `null` would report "not parked" during exactly the outage that matters, which is why `wrapper_session_id` ships beside it to name which null a caller holds. ⚠️ **It is a status field, never a gate**: the gate the fleet polls stays `awaiting_input` from the session registry, and a test fails if `pending_question` ever grows a branch, a comparison or an answer path, so one park cannot read as two. `wrapper_session_id` is also **persisted to the spawn ledger** (unlike `shipping`): the wrapper is the only holder of the park, so a server that restarted without it could neither report nor answer one, and a genuinely parked worker would read as "not parked" for the rest of its life.

## v0.213.2

- fix: **a headless worker's gate stops rendering as `unroutable`, so the operator can see it is a question rather than an exited session.** A headless worker owns no pane by construction (`owned_pane()` refuses an unprovable pane, so the hook records `pane: ""`), so every such row took the no-pane branch and was labelled `unroutable — no pane recorded for this item` — **while already carrying a working `answer:` handover**. Measured 2026-10-24: four operator-bound gates surfaced in one sweep, all four rendered that way, and the operator acted on none of them; each was cleared only by a manager reading the store directly. Both halves of that reading are fixed: the jump line now says `headless — no pane to jump to; answer with the command below` when the row is answerable, and the pane column renders `hst` instead of `?`. ⚠️ **The relabel is scoped to rows that ARE answerable.** Without a store id there is no handover and no route, so the row stays `unroutable — no pane recorded for this item` — otherwise this change would dress up a dead end as actionable, which is what the original wording existed to prevent. ⚠️ **A stale pane is untouched**: a row carrying a pane that does not resolve to its session still reads `unroutable — pane N does not resolve to this session`, because that is a real ambiguity and not a headless worker. ⚠️ **The pane-column marker is scoped with the jump line, not applied to every pane-less row**: a row with no pane *and* nothing to answer keeps `?` and `unroutable — no pane recorded for this item`, because it may be an exited session and `hst` would claim a live headless worker there — the same overstatement this change removes one branch up. `commands/who-needs-me.md` now documents all three shapes. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`, `commands/who-needs-me.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.213.1

- fix: **the share's denominator now counts the digest's stalled and parked rows, which it had been silently dropping.** `drive-share.py`'s `digest_classes()` read the class from `parts[3]`, which is the class in the test fixture (`· idle · 12m · parked · …` — it carries an age column) but is the **task file** in the digest the fleet actually renders (`· idle · parked · task · boxes · link`). Every live digest therefore parsed to classes like `{idle, shell}` and `{—, 'Executors Claim Agent Tasks Wi'}`, detected **zero** stalled/parked rows, and the printed denominator counted only the ledger's `unverifiable` entries. Measured 2026-10-22 on two live rounds: round `18-35-05Z` printed `1/120` where the honest figure was `1/125` — 5 parked rows, zero overlap with the 120 ledger sids; round `18-19-06Z` printed `0/124` and was right only because that digest had `parked=0`. The class column is now **located** by scanning for a known class token (`DIGEST_CLASSES`) rather than indexed, so both shapes parse. ⚠️ **The tests could not catch this** — the fixture carried the same age column the parser assumed, so it mirrored the bug instead of the render; `test_drive_share.py` gains a fixture in the real shape and two cases that fail against the old parser. ⚠️ **The first cut of this fix kept the same fail-quiet shape it was replacing** — an unrecognised class token was skipped by a bare `continue`, so the next class the render added would vanish from the denominator exactly as stalled/parked had. The vocabulary now carries `deferred` and `orphan`, a cell is matched on its **leading word** so the render's inline qualifiers (`parked (closer pick)`, `finished (shell child held)`, `⏸️ HELD — <reason>`) still count, and rows whose token is genuinely unknown are **counted and printed** under the SHARE header rather than dropped. Measured across four consecutive live rounds the detected counts go 0, 0, 0, 1 with a fixed index and 0, 5, 7, 2 with the located class — the `2` is a `parked (closer pick)` row the exact-match version still missed. ⚠️ **The second cut was still wrong in the other direction.** Scanning forward for the first known token walked into the task column, so `brandnewclass · Parked Ideas.md` was read as a **parked** row (denominator inflated, nothing reported unparsed) and `brandnewclass · Finished Migration.md` vanished — the same fail-quiet shape again, one layer down. The class position is now **decided structurally**: the class cell is never a task basename, so a class word found in a cell ending `.md` is the collision case and is rejected, and the position falls out of that one fact. ⚠️ **Enumerating the age format does not converge** — across nine live digests the age cell has read `11m`, `75s`, `42m`, `25m`, `0.3h`, `0.1h`, `18:04Z` and `stall 2`, and every enumeration missed the next form: a `\d+[smhdw]` test dropped two real **stalled** rows on round `19-39-16Z`, and the decimal form dropped **13 stalled/parked rows** on 2026-10-28. Detected counts across the eight live rounds measured are 0, 5, 7, 2, 6 and 7, 9, 10 — the last three are today's, and they went from 7, 7, 0 before the structural rule. ⚠️ **`agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md:142` still documents `<age>` as mandatory** and could not be corrected here: that file sits **7 bytes** under its 30 720-byte body ratchet, so the one-line fix is refused by `check-budget` outright. The divergence is recorded in the PR instead of silently left. A digest with **no `CLASSIFICATION` heading at all** now refuses rather than reporting a share over the ledger alone — a renamed or truncated section is a render the parser cannot read, not a fleet with no problems; an explicitly empty section (`UNKNOWN (pass not run)`) is a readable statement and keeps `0/0 (n/a)`. Change set: `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.213.0

- feat: **a manager's escalations can now carry `producer_kind: manager`, so the board can separate a manager's ask from a worker's.** The store has declared `manager` since `attention-controller/pkg/producer-kind.go:34`, but `attention-ask.py`'s client-side `PRODUCER_KINDS` pinned four values and omitted it, so no producer could emit it and the field read `session` on every card the fleet raised. The tuple now carries `manager` in the store's own order, and every manager-loop call site passes it: `scripts/open-items.py` (its ledger is the manager loop's, per its own module docstring), `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/incident-ledger.py`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `commands/open.md`, and `commands/steps/{covered-subject-invariant,drive,goal-gates,goal-under-target,needs-input,readings,round-tail}.md`. ⚠️ **The flag defaults to `session`**, so a call site that omits it keeps emitting `session` silently — the omission is the failure mode, not a neutral default, which is why each site is now pinned by a test rather than left to the next reader. `scripts/pod-attention.py` stays `session` deliberately: a pod is a long-lived Claude Code session and there is no `pod` member. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/open-items.py`, `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/incident-ledger.py`, `agents/goal-drive.md`, `commands/open.md`, `commands/steps/covered-subject-invariant.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `commands/steps/goal-under-target.md`, `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_pod_attention.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.212.0

- fix: **a reaped `permission` gate stops reading as open, and the arm stops naming a cause the store cannot establish.** Two surfaces misreported the same close. `poll` printed `OPEN` for any record carrying neither an answer nor an act — a branch deliberately *not* keyed on `state` — so a card closed by a dead producer's sweep read exactly like a live gate; measured 2026-10-27, `poll` and `answer` disagreed about one card seconds apart. That branch is now keyed on `state == "open"`, and a reaped close falls through to `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:` — where an acknowledgement also lands, since a close that carries an actor is deliberately not read as a release; the two remain distinguishable by `ANSWERED_BY:`, which an acknowledgement carries and a reap cannot, rather than by the terminal word. ⚠️ **What the old branch bought was a word that also meant "a live gate is waiting"**, and that overload is what this change gives up. ⚠️ **"No actor" is not a synonym for "producer exited"**: the watcher's dead-session sweep, the compact-crossing clear, a forwarded `SessionEnd` and `close_session_approval` all post an empty body — and the last is not an exit at all. The `ITEM_CLOSED:` refusal therefore now says the store does not record *why* a card left, instead of offering "the card was cleared, or its producer exited" as if the record had settled which; the `LOST:` fallback stops merging *answered* with *no longer open* for the same reason, at strictly less evidence. `commands/attention-next.md` was still repeating the two-cause claim, so it is corrected too. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/attention-answer.py`, `scripts/answered-attribution.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_answer.py`, `commands/attention-next.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.211.1

- docs: state the subject-level phase precondition beside the spawn readiness rules in `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker
- fix: a phase-refused loop stands down rather than re-arming — re-arming records liveness and hides the subject from the armed-set check, and a bare stop is restarted uncapped by the topic keep-alive

## v0.211.0

- feat: a goal or topic loop arms only a subject at `phase: execution`; it otherwise refuses and names the level's gate command — the round re-arms so the re-check is automatic, the arm asks the operator to re-run it

## v0.210.0

- feat: **a Claude session that dies now leaves a record of how it ended, instead of leaving nothing anywhere.** Until now a killed session and a session that was never started were indistinguishable from every surface the fleet reads: the task file keeps `claude_session_id`, the session registry keeps a pid, and both still read as "running" long after the process is gone — nothing recorded *that* it ended, *when*, or *how*. `scripts/session-exit-record.py` is the writer: one tab-separated line per event at `~/.claude/state/session-exit/pane-<pane>.log` (`SUPERVISOR_SESSION_EXIT_DIR` overrides), carrying `timestamp · kind · pane · session · pid · rc · signal · sender · writer`, with `128+n` classified as `signalled kill` and the signal **named** (`137 → SIGKILL`) rather than folded into `exited`. ⚠️ **Two writers, layered by what each survives, and the second is why a group kill still leaves a line.** The pane shell's `EXIT` trap covers a normal exit, a non-zero exit and a signal delivered to the launcher child; it is the only writer that can name a signal, because a signal number is a fact only the parent's `waitpid` holds. The second is a detached observer the `observe` call starts: it writes a heartbeat every interval and, when the watched pid disappears, writes `unknown-end` — an **inference**, never `signalled kill`. It starts **before** the trap and the launcher, self-daemonizes by double fork plus `os.setsid()` (macOS ships no `setsid(1)`), and waits on a pipe handshake so the shell does not resume until the grandchild is in its own session — a process-group kill landing immediately after the spawn therefore cannot take it along. It watches `$$`, not the launcher's pid: the shell outlives the launcher and dies exactly when the group is killed, which is the case the trap cannot record. Two consequences are load-bearing rather than incidental. The heartbeat lives in the observer, not a pane-side loop, because a pane-side heartbeat dies with the thing it describes — and the move removes ~960 appends per 8-hour session. And the observer re-reads the record before writing its end line, so a parent report already on disk always wins; that is sound because the shell cannot exit until its `EXIT` trap returns, so by the time the observer sees the pid gone, any trap line is already written. ⚠️ **`sender` is honest in three directions, and the reason is quoted from the failing probe, never asserted from documentation.** It reads `-` on a marker, `none` on a self-inflicted exit (a fact: the process ended itself), and `unavailable(<the probe's own output>)` where a signal was delivered. On this host `/usr/bin/eslogger exec` refuses a non-root caller with rc=1 and `ES_NEW_CLIENT_RESULT_ERR_NOT_PRIVILEGED`, so that symbolic code — read out of the probe's stderr at run time — is what the record carries; a test asserts the composed words a doc claim would have produced are absent. ⚠️ **`writer` separates fact from inference** (`trap` vs `observer`), so a reader can tell a reported end from a deduced one without re-deriving it. The design's original ten fields dropped two deliberately: `tty` is derivable from the recorded pid (`ps -o tty= -p <pid>`) and `event` duplicated `kind`; the deviation is recorded in the module docstring because that docstring *is* the design record and must not contradict the code. ⚠️ **`exec` was the defect, and it was measured, not argued.** `exec "<launcher>"` replaces the pane's shell with Claude, so no process is left to `waitpid` and the exit status stops existing by construction — `bash -lc 'exec bash -c "kill -9 $$"'` reports `137` to its own caller and leaves nothing to run a trap; the same child run without `exec`, behind `trap _sx EXIT`, records `rc=137`. Every spawn that used `exec` therefore ran a launcher whose death could not be observed at all, which is why this shape drops it rather than adding a writer beside it. Six call sites carried the shape and all six now install the trap: `commands/open.md` § 3.1's resume spawn and the goal-manager and topic-manager spawns (lines ~636, ~502, ~547), `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker — the shape's stated single home, which would otherwise re-propagate `exec` to every future reader — `scripts/restart-worker.py`'s `resume_command()`, a deliberate executable copy of Step 3.1, and `server/supervisor.mjs`'s `spawnInteractiveAgent()`, the `spawn_agent(interactive: true)` tab path. That last one is the sixth, and it was missed until a review of this change found it: the MCP server built its own `cd … && exec <launcher>` string, so every tab worker this server spawns still left no record, which is precisely the gap this change exists to close. It now passes the writer path as an `env` assignment before `bash` and runs the launcher as a child behind the trap, with `--session` omitted because the claude session id does not exist until the tab registers one. That shape moved into `server/tab-spawn.mjs`, because a string built inline in `supervisor.mjs` cannot be asserted on — importing that module starts an MCP server — which is exactly how the sixth site survived five fixes. ⚠️ **The guard covers the launcher, not just the first statement after it.** Written as `cd X && <trap>…; launcher`, bash parses `cd X && _sx() {…}; marker; observe; trap _sx EXIT; launcher` — a failed `cd` skips only the function DEFINITION, and the `;`-separated marker, observer, trap and launcher all still run, so the worker starts in wezterm's mux-server cwd while the tab title and the ledger name the requested one. Measured 2026-10-27: that string with `cd /nonexistent-dir` exits 0, writes the marker, and reaches the launcher (`bash: _sx: command not found`). The shape it replaced, `cd X && exec <launcher>`, did abort. A brace group keeps the `&&` covering everything it was written to cover. The writer path also resolves through `fileURLToPath`, not `URL.pathname`: a `.pathname` is percent-encoded, so a plugin under a path with a space or a non-ASCII character names a file that does not exist and every write fails open behind `|| true` — a pane that silently records nothing, indistinguishable from one still alive. `server/tab-spawn.test.mjs` (12 cases) now pins `exec` absent from the whole command, the trap installed before the launcher, the start marker written before the launcher, the observer started before both and watching `$$`, `--pid $$` on all three calls, `--session` never invented, `$?` captured on the function's entry rather than at definition time, per-call `env` exported before the `cd`, the writer path left to the caller — and, **driven through a real shell rather than asserted as string order**, that a failed `cd` launches nothing and writes no record, with a good-`cd` positive control in the same file so a probe that can never fire cannot read as a pass. An ordering assertion on the string passes on the broken form; that is why the first version of this file missed the regression the review then caught as critical. The trap's shell syntax was settled by measurement because the recipe's inner string is single-quoted by its caller: `trap '_sx $?' EXIT` is impossible and `trap "_sx \$?"` would expand `$?` at definition time, so `$?` is captured as the function's first statement (`_sx() { rc=$?; … }`) and `trap _sx EXIT` invokes it bare. ⚠️ **The writer path rides as an `env VAR=value` assignment, never inline in the quoted string.** A spawned pane inherits wezterm's *mux server's* environment, not the caller's, so `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` does not resolve inside the new pane; `restart-worker.py` passes the absolute path beside itself, since it already knows the location. ⚠️ **`--kind spawn` is a start marker, and it is what makes an empty record readable.** Without it, a record with no end line is ambiguous between a live session and a writer that was never wired — the exact ambiguity that made a previous attempt at this look successful while proving nothing. A record beginning `kind=spawn` with no end line is a session that was still running (or whose writer died with it); no record at all is a spawn that never installed the writer. ⚠️ **`closed-tab` is in the vocabulary and is never emitted.** WezTerm's own event surface has no pane/tab-close event (15 events, 13 window + 2 mux, enumerated 2026-10-27), and `SIGHUP` is not specific to a tab close — a terminal quit, a wezterm crash and an SSH drop deliver the same signal — so a trap stamping `closed-tab` on `HUP` would assert a cause it cannot distinguish. ⚠️ **A new store, not one of the three ledgers.** `asked-ledger.py` records who was asked and who resolved it, `drive-ledger.py` records what a drive round nudged, `incident-ledger.py` records a *blocker's* lifecycle keyed on a dedup key; none records a process ending, and folding this into the last would either pollute its permanent dedup-key namespace with non-blockers or make every ended session look like an incident. Checked 2026-10-27 and recorded in the script's docstring, because the check is the reason. Records are keyed on the **pane**, not the session id: the pane is the process container that dies, a resumed session is a new process in a new pane, and two panes are two ends that must not overwrite each other — the session id rides as a field because a fresh manager spawn does not know it yet, and the registry maps pane → session for the rest. `scripts/tests/test_session_exit_record.py` (32 cases) pins the field order, `rc=1`/`rc=128` as exits rather than signals, `137 → SIGKILL` with 129/130/143 named and an unnamed signal printing its number, an unknown session and an unknown pid each rendering `-` rather than an empty field, `kind=spawn` and `kind=heartbeat` both being markers and neither being an end kind, `sender` reading `-`/`none`/`unavailable(…)` in its three directions with the reason matching `^unavailable\(eslogger ` and the composed words `root-only`/`documentation` absent, a pane id that could escape the store being refused with nothing written, a second append not truncating the first, the appended line being echoed to stdout (the pre-kill control a test can capture without reading the record path, which would make "never wired" indistinguishable from "wrote nothing"), `read` of an absent record returning empty at exit 0, `list --json` on a missing store yielding `records: []`, and `$WEZTERM_PANE` being used when `--pane` is omitted; the observer's own six cases pin that a live pid yields a heartbeat and no end, a vanished pid yields `unknown-end`, the observer never claims a signal (`rc=unavailable`/`signal=unavailable`), its end carries an `unavailable(` sender, the parent's report wins over its inference, and a non-positive pid is refused with nothing written. `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py` gains six cases pinning the recipe's own invariants — `exec` absent from the whole argv, the writer path present exactly once as an env assignment, the spawn marker written before the launcher runs, the observer watching `$$`, the observer starting before the launcher, and `--pid $$` appearing on all three of the marker, the trap and the observer. `commands/open.md` budget entry 70128 → 73488: the three spawn lines each carry the writer path, the trap, the start marker and the observer, and the ⚠️ paragraphs that stop a future reader re-adding `exec` or a pane-side heartbeat are what the raise pays for — the paragraphs were tightened to their load-bearing core, and the entry is a deliberate hand raise because `--tighten` cannot raise by design. `docs/fleet-surface.md` keeps the spawn shape and defers the end-kind vocabulary and each writer's survival properties to the `scripts/session-exit-record.py` module docstring rather than restating them. Change set: `scripts/session-exit-record.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_exit_record.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/tab-spawn.mjs`, `server/tab-spawn.test.mjs`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.209.3

- fix: **`scripts/propose-managers.py` no longer offers a manager for a goal that already has one, when the live manager's session label abbreviates the goal title.** The label rule (`_MANAGER_SUFFIX` stripped, `_norm_session_name`, `_matches_one_title`) needs the goal title or a ≥20-character prefix of it, and an abbreviated label is neither: `Vault API Manager` does not name *Typed Vault REST API for Agents and Tools*. Measured 2026-10-25 — **three of five live managers were mis-matched, and one card had actually printed**: `Agent Namespace Manager` → *Agent Workloads Run in Dedicated Agent Namespaces* (**rank 3, posted**), `Vault API Manager` → *Typed Vault REST API for Agents and Tools* (rank 6), `PR Review Agent Fast Manager` → *PR Reviews Return a Verdict Within Minutes, Not Hours* (rank 10); the other two registrations read `STALE` and their goals do not qualify. The fix is **in the caller**, as decided at plan time: the session's own registration at `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<session-id>.json` carries the `subject` the tier resolved at spawn, so `managed()` gains a second path that matches that subject **through the same imported `_matches_one_title`** — one answer to "does this name that subject", never a second matcher, which is the rule `fleet-board.py:212` states for the first caller. No suffix is stripped on this path: the subject is a page title, not a session label, and removing a trailing ` manager` from a goal name would corrupt it. ⚠️ **The pairing with `manager-liveness.py` is not optional.** That directory is documented as never cleaned (`CHANGELOG.md:942`), so a registration outlives its session; suppressing on one would hide a genuinely manager-less goal, which is the silent direction. `_liveness_ok` therefore suppresses **only** on state `OK` — `STALE`, `STOPPED`, `UNARMED`, `INVALID`, an unreadable module and an unknown branch all leave the goal qualifying, per this file's own `TOPIC_DIRS` doctrine that failing to suppress is the loud direction and a wrong card is one the operator can decline. The classifier is **imported, not copied** (lazily and cached), the same rule as `_board()`. ⚠️ **A registration suppresses only for its own vault.** Goal titles are not namespaced across vaults, so a title that matches in another vault proves nothing here; the comparison lowercases and strips both sides, and an absent or non-string vault reads `""` and never matches. ⚠️ **One board read, deliberately.** `fleet-board.py --json` measures at ~5.7 s, so `live_manager_count()` became `live_managers()` returning `(labels, registrations)` from a **single** snapshot and `count = len(labels)` in `main()` — two reads would let the cap arithmetic and the suppression disagree about the same fleet, and would double the card's cost. The registration directory resolves through the repo's established override chain — `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MANAGER_DIR` → `WORKER_MANAGER_STATE_DIR` → `~/.claude/state/worker-manager` — matching `scripts/answer-path-check.py:55`, because a reader honouring a different name than the writer is how a relocated tree reads as "no registration" and silently restores this defect. The reader carries the sibling's guards verbatim: the session id must be a bare filename (it is joined into a path), the parsed shape is checked **inside** the guard, and `subject`/`branch` must be **strings**, not merely truthy — `{"subject": 123}` passes a truthiness test and then raises inside the matcher, turning a suppression check into a traceback. `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py` grows 70 → 96 cases: the reproduced pair asserted **in both directions** (the label alone must not match — that is the defect — and the registration must), all three measured pairs as a subTest table, a non-`OK` registration leaving the goal qualifying, a cross-vault registration and a vault-less candidate both refusing to suppress, the vault comparison's case/space tolerance, `qualify()` end-to-end for both the suppress and the stale direction, the registration reader's path-escape and non-object/non-string shapes, `_liveness_ok` against the **real** `manager-liveness.py` classifier over a temp state dir (`OK` fresh, `UNARMED` absent, `STALE` backdated, `STOPPED` marker, unknown branch), and `live_registrations()` skipping the Fleet Manager row, a worker row, a manager with no registration and a non-object row. `MainTest.run_main` now stubs `live_managers()`; `count=` still names how many live managers a case wants, with placeholder labels filling the count, so the cap arithmetic reads exactly as before the read was widened. Change set: `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.209.2

- fix: **the attention board's open-card count no longer matches a class attribute the board stopped emitting.** `scripts/attention-board.py` counted the literal `<li class="item" data-item-id="`, but every row now carries a risk class (`class="item risk-risk"`, `item risk-decision`, `item risk-routine`), so the literal matched nothing and every round printed `Board: 0 open` — indistinguishable from an empty board, and printed beside the round's own gate list (measured 2026-10-24: five live operator gates under a `Board: 0 open` line). The count is now a predicate over rendered `<li>` rows carrying a `data-item-id` whose class list lacks `dimmed` — never an exact-string match on the class attribute, which reads zero rather than erroring. ⚠️ **Scoped to rendered markup:** `<script>`/`<style>` bodies are stripped first. A raw-text count of the served page is inflated by 12% (92 `data-item-id` occurrences against 82 rows on 2026-10-24); the predicate scans `<li>` tags and so is not inflated by the board's own attribute reads, which makes the strip the guard that keeps it correct if a template change ever has the script construct row markup. `scripts/tests/test_attention_board.py` re-pins `BOARD` to the live shape — two open cards of **different** risk classes plus one dimmed — so a predicate hardcoded to a single class, or still matching the old bare literal, fails (0 and 1 against an expected 2), and adds a script/style-carrying fixture whose bodies hold **both** the measured attribute-read form and a row-shaped decoy string, so the assertion reads 1 against 6 raw occurrences and **3** if the stripping is removed — the decoy is what makes the test able to fail.

## v0.209.1

- fix: **three hygiene findings the fleet-registration approval left open.** `scripts/fleet-register.py` writes its temp file with `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` at `0o600` and removes it on a failed dump — the bare `open()` it used landed at the umask with a pid-predictable, followable name, against the convention `scripts/session-identity.py:222-238` states for a state file under `~/.claude`, and this registration is read as an identity assertion. `commands/steps/re-arm.md` gains the missing failure branch for the **arm** leg: a non-zero `--arm` leaves the session registered but `UNARMED`, which `--state` refuses exactly like no registration, and the usual cause is an interval given as `15m`/`900s` instead of bare seconds. `scripts/tests/test_fleet_register.py` snapshots and restores the four env vars its tests mutate, so the suite is no longer order-dependent for any later module that resolves a path at import.

## v0.209.0

- feat: **the fleet loop records its liveness, so the Fleet Manager has an answer path the spawn guard can see.** `scripts/manager-liveness.py` gains a third branch, `fleet`; new `scripts/fleet-register.py` writes the Fleet Manager's `worker-manager` registration; and `commands/steps/re-arm.md` runs **both** at every round's head — the registration first, then `--arm --subject "Fleet Manager" --branch fleet`. ⚠️ **The registration is the half that actually matters, and a cadence-only first cut of this change was blocked in review for missing it:** `scripts/answer-path-check.py` refuses at the registration gate before it ever asks `manager-liveness.py` anything, so arming a cadence for a session with no registration changed nothing. ⚠️ **And the arm belongs in the ROUND, not the one-time arm:** `fleet-loop.md` runs once, while `--state` calls a record STALE past `interval + SLACK`, so an arm written only at the first round expires ~18 minutes later and every later spawn is refused again. ⚠️ **The record is the point, not bookkeeping.** A fleet round genuinely answers headless workers' parked prompts (it runs the wrangler and watches the queue), but it left no evidence of that, and `scripts/answer-path-check.py` reads a session with no `worker-manager` registration as having no answer path — so the refusal added in v0.208.0 refused **every** headless spawn the Fleet Manager made. Measured 2026-10-23: the refusal was correct for the probe and wrong for that session, and the fix is the record, never a bypass. ⚠️ **`fleet` is its own tier, not a synonym for `goal` or `topic`** — the Fleet Manager manages no subject page, and registering it under either would put a non-subject into that tier's namespace; the slug is `fleet--fleet-manager`, so it cannot collide with a goal or topic of the same name. `commands/fleet-loop.md` budget entry 4019 → 4402 (the commands themselves live in `commands/steps/re-arm.md`, which is not baselined, so the arm file carries a pointer rather than a copy). Tests pin the behaviours the fix actually claims: `fleet-register.py` writes the exact payload the probe reads (subject `Fleet Manager`, branch `fleet`), refuses a non-bare session id and writes nothing on refusal; the liveness branch is its own slug and an unknown branch is still refused; and — the pair the review demanded — a fleet registration **plus** a fresh arm returns `ok: true` from the real `answer-path-check.py`, while the registration **alone** still refuses as `UNARMED`. That last pair is what a cadence-only fix could not have passed. ⚠️ **A second review round tightened the command block itself, and it needed it:** the two lines are now `&&`-joined with quoted placeholders and a stated vault source (`$(basename "$PWD")`) — unquoted, `--vault <vault>` parsed as a `<` redirection and `--interval <clamped seconds>` split at the space, so the block would not have run at all — and a **refused registration now stops the arm**, rather than leaving exactly the registered-nowhere-but-armed state this step exists to prevent. The registration write is temp-file + `os.replace` like every sibling state writer, the producer→consumer seam is tested end to end (the real writer's file fed to the real probe, not two assertions on the same literals), and `server/answer-path.mjs`'s known-gap note retires with the gap it described.

## v0.208.1

- fix: **the four findings the spawn-mode approval left open.** (1) **`docs/fleet-surface.md` item 6's worked example did not map onto the closed list it illustrates** — *"an inbox sweep judging what is actionable"* was offered as carrying signal (1) or (5), but judging what is actionable is the worker's own work, not a decision the body names as the operator's, so the conclusion did not follow from a list the same section declares exhaustive. The example now separates the two cases by *who the body reserves the judgement to*: a sweep told to flag ambiguity for the operator carries signal (5); one that judges on its own carries none and is `headless`. (2) **`scripts/answer-path-check.py` refuses a non-string `subject`/`branch`** — `{"subject": 123}` passed a bare truthiness test and then raised `TypeError` inside `subprocess.run`, outside the classes `liveness_state` catches, breaching `check()`'s "never raises" and handing the caller a traceback where a quotable refusal is promised. (3) **The same script refuses a session id that is not a bare filename** — the id is joined into a path, and `--session-id` is caller-supplied, so `../` or an absolute value read an arbitrary `*.json` outside the registration dir; refused rather than sanitised, because a stripped id addresses a registration the caller did not name. (4) **`commands/open.md` stops promising a remedy the probe does not carry** — the line told the reader the probe's own output names "the reason and the remedy", but no probe branch carries one; the command now appends it. **Three python tests added — 13 cases:** three non-string field shapes with their reported **type names** asserted, seven non-bare-filename ids (including a null byte), and three absent-id forms, which must say "no session id" and **must not** claim a path escape there is none of. `commands/open.md` budget entry 69904 → 70128.

## v0.208.0

- feat: **a `role: agent` task with no watch-need signal now defaults to `headless`, flipping the spawn-mode floor.** `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 6 is the single home and now reads *"a `role: agent` task defaults to `headless`; the five watch-need signals below are the only thing that keeps it `interactive`"*. The five signals are closed and exhaustive — an approval step in the body, an "ask if unsure" instruction, a live-output dependency, a GUI-only surface, a judgement reserved to the operator — and `shipping: true` and `flag: true` are explicitly NOT among them (`shipping` decides permission mode, never mode; `flag` opens a `todo` row and says nothing about mid-flight watching). `role: human` and `role: manager` stay forced `interactive`. ⚠️ **The old asymmetry is reconciled rather than deleted**: it still explains why the five signals exist, and now says so — *"it now buys the five signals, not a floor for every agent task"* — with the superseded *"floor, not a tie-break"* wording recorded in place rather than quietly overwritten. Four restatements reconciled: `commands/open.md` (the *unclear → interactive* fallback, the *safe floor* clause, and the *headless should be rare* paragraph, which becomes the two standing constraints plus a single-home pointer) and `commands/goal-spawn.md` (both floor clauses, and its *"the default is a wezterm tab"* opening, which the flip falsified). ⚠️ **`scripts/check-spawn-mode.py`'s `HOME_DEFINES` pinned the deleted phrases** and would have failed on the correct tree while passing on the stale one — the one direction a guard must never fail; it now pins the closed list and its exhaustiveness, and a new test proves the second pin fires on its own. `scripts/budget-baseline.json` records `commands/open.md` at **69904** bytes in the landed tree — master's 68342 plus this change's additions, raised by hand because `check-budget.py` accepts a deliberate growth only that way.

- feat: **`scripts/answer-path-check.py` — the observable a headless spawn's refusal needs.** A headless worker's gates park with the session that spawned it, so a spawner with no answer path strands it; until now nothing could answer *"does this session have one?"*. The script resolves the session's own `subject` and `branch` from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<session-id>.json` and asks `scripts/manager-liveness.py --state` — ⚠️ **reusing the instrument the fleet already runs rather than adding a second lock file**, which would be the two-counters defect the repo refuses everywhere else. Every branch refuses (no registration, unreadable registration, broken liveness, any state but `OK`), so the check cannot fail open. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-10-23 across all 106 registrations: 52 STOPPED, 36 STALE, 18 UNARMED, 0 OK** — the refuse direction is exercised on live data, the pass direction has no live positive yet (arming one is a manager act, not a worker's), and wiring the refusal before managers arm their loops would refuse every headless spawn and hold the headless share at its baseline. ⚠️ **Re-measured 2026-10-23 while wiring the refusal below: two live managers DO read `OK`** (`01261c71` *Operator Oversees only Managers, not Workers*, `b9a2ea68` *PR Reviews Return a Verdict Within Minutes, Not Hours*), so the pass direction has a live positive and the "0 OK" reading above was a snapshot of a lapsed fleet, not a structural zero.

- feat: **a headless spawn is now REFUSED when the spawning session has no answer path — at both spawn sites, and the refusal names the probe it used.** New `server/answer-path.mjs` builds the refusal and runs `scripts/answer-path-check.py`; it is wired into `spawnAgent` beside the other guards and **before a worker, a tab or a ledger record exists** — SC2's evidence is the *absent* ledger row, so a refusal that leaves one behind is not a refusal. `commands/open.md` Step 0.6 and `commands/goal-spawn.md` run the same probe before opening a `headless` row, so the refusal is seen before the row opens rather than as a spawn error after it; the server check is the enforcement, and a spawn that skips the command step is still refused. ⚠️ **`commands/goal-spawn.md`'s `allowed-tools` gains `Bash(python3:*)` in the same change** — without it the instruction named a tool that command did not hold, so the site would have read as checked while running nothing. ⚠️ **Every branch fails closed** — no session id, a probe that cannot run, output that does not parse, and an `ok` that is truthy but not `true` all refuse; a check that failed open would open exactly the worker this rule exists to prevent. ⚠️ **It refuses more than unarmed managers, and that is the point:** the probe proves a *manager loop*, so the Fleet Manager (`commands/fleet-loop.md` deliberately writes no liveness record) and a plain operator session are refused too. The remedy is in the refusal text — open the worker `interactive`, or arm the session's manager loop and re-run — and the fleet-tier registration gap is recorded in `answer-path.mjs`'s header rather than papered over with a bypass. 8 unit tests in `server/answer-path.test.mjs`, each refusal direction pinned.

- feat: **a permission park that times out now writes a `decided_by: 'timeout'` row — the observable the 7-day criterion had none of.** `server/supervisor.mjs`'s `PERMISSION_TIMEOUT_MS` settle path resolved the promise with a deny and returned, so `permissions.jsonl` recorded **no** row for a timeout: the log's `manager deny` rows were all explicit manager denies and could not proxy one. `server/attention-poll.mjs` gains `timeoutDecisionRecord({ requestId, agentId, park, now })`, shaped key-for-key like `storeDecisionRecord` and the `answer_permission` record so all three are comparable in one log with only `decided_by` separating them, and `decision` is **fixed to `deny`** rather than parameterised — a builder that could be handed `allow` is a way to write a timeout that never happened. The record is written **before** the settle, so the row exists even if settling throws. ⚠️ **Built in `attention-poll.mjs` because `supervisor.mjs` cannot be imported in-process** (importing it starts the MCP server), so the builder is behaviourally tested and only the call site is pinned — by a source-text assertion that there is exactly **one** such site and that it sits inside the timeout timer, since a second site would double-count the window.

- fix: **`agents/goal-wrangler.md` resolves its allowlist under the plugin root, so it can read it from a vault cwd.** Both references named `docs/permission-allowlist.md` relative, and a wrangler runs inside the manager's session — whose cwd is a vault — where that path does not exist. The read therefore failed and **every** park escalated to the operator, which is the exact cost the written allowlist exists to remove (measured 2026-10-23, reported by PR Review Fast Manager). Both sites now read `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/permission-allowlist.md`, the same resolution `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` and `agents/gate-relay-send.md` already use for their own scripts.

- fix: **three review findings on the probe, and one stale restatement the flip left behind.** `scripts/answer-path-check.py` now honours `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MANAGER_DIR` — the repo's established override for that directory (`scripts/adopt-orphans.py:70`, `scripts/inbox.py:119`) — with the fixture knob kept one step below it, so a relocated tree no longer reads as "no registration"; and it type-checks the parsed registration **inside** the guard, because valid JSON that is not an object (`null`, `[]`, a bare string) parsed cleanly and then raised `AttributeError` on `.get`, a traceback where the caller is promised a quotable refusal. Its header also stops claiming the pass direction has no live positive, which the re-measurement below disproved. ⚠️ **`commands/open.md:195` still described item 6 as carrying *"the asymmetry that makes `interactive` a floor rather than a tie-break"*** — the rule this change flips — at the one section that decides `mode`; the line now names the five-signal rule and records the superseded wording in place.

## v0.207.0

- feat: opt a task row into a `/goal` loop at spawn with `goal_loop: true`, so a worker keeps working until a disk-verified done or an operator gate instead of stopping to be nudged. The condition is the spawn prompt and the work command follows as a second message — measured, not stylistic: one submitted message is one command, and a `/goal` with the work command behind it parses as a single goal while the command never runs (the pane showed `Goal set: …` with the command folded into the condition, then `Goal achieved`, and no work done). `scripts/goal-condition.py` emits the condition, inlining the task's Success Criteria so the tool-less evaluator never needs a file read, and stepping full → compact → probe to stay under the command's 4,000-character cap; every form keeps the disk probe (`Status: completed`, open-box count 0) and states that an inlined criterion is never proof, so a criterion's text drifting out from under the goal cannot manufacture a false "met". Opt-in only: a row without the field opens exactly as before. Wired at `commands/open.md` § Step 3 and § 3.2 and in the goal tier's spawn mechanics; `mode: headless` and `docker` / `k8s` are excluded — the emitter refuses them with exit 4, since neither has a pane to send the work command into or to show the `Goal set:` readiness signal — and it refuses (exit 3) a task carrying no Success Criteria rather than arming a condition that cannot fail. Its stdout carries the `/goal ` prefix, so it is the spawn prompt verbatim. `scripts/spawn-goal.py` then arms it in the pane and fails closed: it exits non-zero when the emitter refuses the row or the `Goal set:` marker never appears, so the work command is never sent behind a goal that did not arm — the idle-under-an-armed-goal failure the feature exists to remove. Rule: `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 10.

## v0.206.0

- fix: **a manager no longer mints a second attention card for a worker gate it relays.** Every surface that documents `--closer` now says the same thing — `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, `commands/steps/goal-gates.md`, `docs/voice-asks.md`, `agents/goal-drive.md` and `attention-ask.py`'s own docstring: the flag is **required, not opt-in**, whenever the closer repeats the card's ask, and omitting it is correct only when the closer asks something *else*. The `Stop` hook mints one card per closer verb — its condition is `board_opt_in or approve_closer or waiting_closer` — so a round that posted the board card and then ended on a `you run:` / `review:` line naming the same gate produced two or three cards for one decision; measured 2026-10-23, one worker gate was re-rendered across three managers' closers and the visible queue read roughly twice its real length. The rule change is the fix — no hook change, because a verb-free handover (`jump-link.py <pane>` with no `👤 You:` verb) already mints nothing and needs no `--closer`.

## v0.205.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:topic-verify` now carries its own topic-page checks instead of calling a vault-local command.** `agents/topic-verify.md` grew from the seven gated steps to a **sixteen-row** check: the eleven disk-backed page-consistency checks (status valid, member links resolve, member must not outrank its topic, task must not outrank its goal, necessity, excess, `# Completion Gate` checkability, `# Status Summary` reconciliation, no double declaration, `## Goals` entry format, `## Goals` entry closes a gate item) now run in the agent, and the five review steps (coverage, fill, startability, slots, route) are retained behind them. The old rule forbidding the agent from restating the checks is gone, because the checks are now its own. Two `## Goals` parse widths are stated deliberately: rows 1–8 read the member set leniently so a malformed entry cannot silently move three other rows, while rows 9 and 11 match `^- \[\[X\]\]` at line start, since a section-wide wikilink scan reads a recorded *removal* as a declaration. `commands/topic-verify.md` resolves `topics_dir` / `goals_dir` / `tasks_dir` once through the new `scripts/resolve-folders.py` and passes them in, so the checks work in a renumbered vault. That script matches the session's cwd **or its nearest ancestor**, so a session started inside `25 Tasks` still resolves its vault, and it reports a missing folder key rather than defaulting one — a literal fallback would be the hardcoded-path defect the resolver exists to remove, applied quietly. An unresolvable vault prints `UNKNOWN <reason>` and the command stops rather than running eleven checks over three empty paths. The goal tier follows: `commands/goal-verify.md` and `agents/goal-verify.md` repoint their topic branch at `supervisor:topic-verify`, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` names it as the necessity instrument, now reading a member goal's SC or DoD item rather than its one-line sentence, and `llms.txt` stops describing the topic tier as seven checks. The vault-local `verify-topic` command this replaced is archived in the operator's vault; nothing in the plugin references it any more.

## v0.204.0

- docs: **the fleet manager opens topic managers, goal managers and workers — and the tier table now says so.** Operator correction (2026-10-23): *"fleet manager never starts a goal manager itself; => this is wrong … fleet manager open tabs for topic-manager, goal-manager + worker if needed this is intended."* `docs/session-tiers.md` § The four tiers states the hierarchy (fleet manager at the top of everything; a topic manager opens goal managers **and** workers; a goal manager opens workers; a worker opens nothing) and its Fleet manager and Topic manager "May start" cells follow. The `-spawn` verbs stay narrower than their tier, and that split is now stated rather than implied: `topic-spawn` starts a goal manager, `goal-spawn` a worker, and a worker opened from the topic tier goes through `/supervisor:open --task`. Reconciled everywhere the old strict-tree rule was restated — `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session roles and § Which manager owns a subject, `commands/topic-loop.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `commands/topic-spawn.md`, `commands/read-guides.md` Step 3, and this repo's `CLAUDE.md` § Session tiers. ⚠️ **"Starting a *topic* manager is a human act" becomes "the operator's decision, the fleet manager performs the open"** — a topic manager is never invented by a manager, but the fleet manager opens it like any other rung below it; every retained "human act" sentence would otherwise contradict the new hierarchy on the same page. **Opening a worker and parenting one stay separate rights**: a topic manager may open a worker, but it does not adopt the worker of a goal it does not own, so `manager_on_chain`'s parenting is unchanged.
- feat: **`/supervisor:open --goal` opens the goal's manager instead of leaving it to the operator.** `commands/open.md` § Step 2B gains the manager branch Step 2C already had: resolve `<goal> Manager` from the session registry (`name` and `formerNames`), JUMP a live one, RESUME an `ABSENT` id, and otherwise spawn it through `manager-launcher.py` into the `Managers` window as `orange`, then tell the operator to run `/vault-cli:work-on-goal` and `/supervisor:goal-loop`. The goal's task listing moves to a fallback that runs only when the manager branch cannot (`manager-launcher.py` non-zero, or the spawn failed) — a live manager owns the goal's tasks.
- fix: **`topic-spawn`'s `--worker` refusal is about the VERB, not the tier.** The token reads `workers are not this verb's to start` (was `… this tier's to start`), because the topic tier may open a worker — just not through this verb. `scripts/topic-spawn.py`'s docstring, `--worker` help and refusal detail follow, and `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py` asserts the new token.

## v0.203.2

- fix: **the incident leg no longer opens a stall incident for a session that is gone, and can no longer open one per eligible session in a single round.** `incident-ledger.py stall` opened an incident for every drive-ledger row at `unverifiable_streak >= 2` with no liveness check and no cap — measured 2026-10-22: **117 incidents in one round**, every one for a session absent from the heartbeat store, escalating into 116 open board cards in 41 seconds. The per-round cap that was supposed to prevent exactly this (`drive-unverifiable.py`'s `MAX_ACTIONS`) governs the nudge/escalation leg only, so the flood arrived through the one door it did not cover. `cmd_stall` now resolves the live fleet once per run through `session-liveness.py --list --json` and skips a session it does not list — a stall is a running session that is not moving, while a session that no longer exists has nothing to investigate and no card the operator could act on — and caps new incidents at `SUPERVISOR_INCIDENT_STALL_BUDGET` (default 3), ordered longest-streak-first so a backlog drains oldest-first. ⚠️ **A failed liveness probe fails OPEN, not closed:** an unreachable store must never read as "every session is dead", which would suppress the whole incident class on an outage — the same false-empty `read_drive_ledger` refuses for a corrupt drive ledger, one store over — so the run keeps its old behaviour and says so on stderr. Both the skip and each held-back row are printed, because a cap that reported nothing would be indistinguishable from a threshold that stopped firing. ⚠️ **The two sides do not share a key space, and the first version of this gate got it wrong.** `session-liveness.py --list --json` returns full registry UUIDs while the drive ledger keys `session_id` on the **8-char display handle** the sweep digest carries (`agents/fleet-drive.md:44`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py:108`) — every other consumer is prefix-tolerant (`--check`, `--pane-for`, `transcript_for`'s `session_id[:8]`), which is what made the mismatch invisible. A set comparison of full ids against 8-char keys classifies EVERY row as gone: the incident class silently disabled, with a plausible skip line per row — the mirror of the bug being fixed. `live_sessions()` therefore returns both forms (lower-cased), and the suite pins the prefix join with a fixture whose drive row is keyed `SESSION[:8]` while the live set holds the full id. ⚠️ **The card is posted by `escalate`, not by `stall`**, so `cmd_escalate` gates stall incidents for gone sessions too — otherwise everything already in the ledger still reaches the board, which is exactly the harm measured above; a skipped incident stays `pending` rather than being stamped `timed-out`, because a skip is not an escalation. Tests stub the probe (an unstubbed run would answer from the host's real fleet) and cover the absent row, a live row still opening beside it, 117 eligible sessions opening only the budget, and the probe-failure fallback — plus a second suite over `live_sessions()`'s own parse, which every stall test stubs past. ⚠️ **The `rows=… registry_live=… degraded=…` header goes to STDERR, not stdout** (measured: with stderr discarded, stdout begins at `[`), so the contract pinned there is "stdout is the array, stderr carries the human line" — and the stdout-header case is kept as a separate tolerance test, because asserting the tolerance as if it were the shape would pass for the wrong reason. The parse is line-based rather than `stdout.find("[")` so a bracket in any future header cannot be read as the payload start, and a non-zero exit, an unparseable body, or a body with no array all read as `unavailable`, which the caller treats as fail-open. `incident_budget()`'s env parsing is pinned too, where a `0` or non-numeric override falling back to 3 is the property that keeps a typo from disabling the cap outright. The round's output collapses the dead rows into one summary line naming all of them — the drive ledger keeps a row for every session that has ever been unverifiable, so a line per row grows without bound — and the trailing "no session has stalled" line is withheld when every eligible row was skipped, because it reads as "nothing stalled" rather than "everything eligible had already exited". Change set: `scripts/incident-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_incident_ledger.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.203.1

- fix: read the controller's Agent Card **with the bearer**, so the control-plane version negotiation actually finds a version. The controller guards its whole surface, `/.well-known/agent-card.json` included — an unauthenticated GET answers **401** `{"error":{"code":"UNAUTHORIZED"…}}` (measured 2026-10-22). The card read added in the previous change sent no `Authorization`, took the `!res.ok` branch, returned no version, sent no `A2A-Version`, and the controller defaulted the **absent** header to `"0.3"` and refused — reproducing the exact `-32009 … requested "0.3", served "1.0"` that change existed to remove. The worker's card is public, which is why the turn plane never needed this; the control plane does. Two tests pin it: a read carrying the bearer yields the version, and a 401 yields none — the failing path, asserted rather than left to be rediscovered.
- fix: **a re-registered park card now matches the park it is about — a native permission modal gets an `ack` card that offers no answer, instead of a `message` card whose free-text field could not release anything.** The tracked-set watcher re-posted every lost park as `attention-ask.py post`'s default `message` item, which the board renders with an input field and Submit. For a tab worker parked on an `Ask` rule (`Bash(git push:*)`, measured 2026-10-22, pane 1543) that field was a lie in the operator's hand: a typed answer reaches the store and stops there, because the modal is answered by a keystroke in its own pane. Posting it as a `permission` item would not have fixed it either — the release path reads `resolved_by`, which only an arm writes and the board never sends, so the card would have offered Allow/Deny that release nothing (the same defect one affordance further out). So `attention-ask.py post` gains `--mechanism`, offering `message` (default) and `ack` and deliberately **not** `permission`; `ack` refuses `--option`/`--recommend` because the schema gives that mechanism no choices. `manager-attention-watch.py`'s `post_repost` takes the park's `is_gated` reason and posts an `ack` carrying "Answer it in the pane" plus a jump target for a permission-class reason (`registry:waiting`, `busy+parked-modal`), and keeps the `message` card unchanged for a question-class one (`headless+closer`) — narrowing that too would remove the board's only way to answer a parked closer line. The jump target resolves the pane by title (`pane_for_session_name`, `None` on a failed query, never `""`) and shells to `jump-link.py`, so a card still posts when the pane cannot be resolved. ⚠️ **The title join goes through the session's REGISTRY name, not the tracked task's label** — a manager-spawned tab is named for its session (`⚙ MDM split command`) while the re-post is handed the task name (`Register an Unmerge Command on …`), so a label-based join finds no pane and every real card silently loses the target it advertises. `registry_name_for_session` reads it from `~/.claude/sessions/*.json` by `sessionId`, with the label kept only as a fallback, and `_norm_title` strips any leading decoration rather than a fixed glyph list — the `⚙` marker is why an enumerated-glyph normaliser misses — while keeping a trailing ` (N)` intact, because `uniqueTabName` adds it as a uniqueness guard and collapsing it would point a suffixed session's card at its sibling's pane. `pane_for_session_name` therefore requires **exactly one** normalised match and returns `None` on zero or several, the same rule `who-needs-me.py:pane_for` applies. `_mechanism_or_default` refuses a real string outside the postable set rather than downgrading it to `message`, so a programmatic `"ack "` typo cannot silently post the question card this change removes. Six regression tests pin both directions and fail on the pre-fix code; a further twenty-eight cover the surfaces the bot reviews found untested — `pane_for_session_name`'s parse (glyph stripping, a failed query, malformed JSON, a non-list payload, a non-dict element, a missing or null `pane_id`, duplicate titles), the registry-name join that decides which pane a card points at, `_norm_title`'s marker handling, and `--mechanism` at the `attention-ask.py` boundary (an `ack` body carrying no `options`, the `--option`/`--recommend` refusals, a real string outside the set refused while a placeholder defaults, `permission` absent from the postable set) — plus one pinning `main`'s `state[sid8][2]` reason extraction, which every other test bypasses by supplying `reasons=` directly. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.203.0

- fix: **the watcher tests no longer write incidents into the operator's live ledger.** `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py` never set `SUPERVISOR_INCIDENTS_LEDGER`, and `open_incident` shells out to `incident-ledger.py` inheriting this process's environment — so every test that drove the real loop opened a REAL incident. Measured 2026-10-22: two fixture records (`aaaa1111`, `15fce333`) had landed in `~/.claude/state/incidents/ledger.json`. The redirect is set at **module** scope (`setUpModule`/`tearDownModule`): a per-`Fixture` version was tried first and did not hold, because `IncidentReplayTest` and the transition tests drive the loop without constructing a `Fixture`, and the records reappeared on the next `make precommit`.
- docs: add the manager permission allowlist — the closed list of commands a manager may answer, the deny list that overrides it, and the clean-tree confinement that admits test runs
- feat: managers answer permission prompts from the written allowlist — the server matches each parked prompt against `server/manager-allowlist.json` and records the matched entry on the answer record, and `goal-wrangler` answers only what the list covers, escalating everything else
- fix: negotiate the A2A protocol version on the **control plane** too, so `create`, `list` and `stop` reach a controller running a2a-go v2.7.0 or later. From that version the controller's handler defaults an **absent** `A2A-Version` header to `"0.3"` and refuses it, and the control-call builder sent no header at all — deliberately, because the controller used to pin v2.6.0, which has no version check; the code carried a note saying whoever bumped the controller past v2.7.0 must add a card read here. That is what happened: controller `v0.17.0` refused every control call with `-32009 … requested "0.3", served "1.0"`, so a `k8s` spawn failed before reaching any backend. `fetchControllerProtocolVersion` now reads the controller's `/.well-known/agent-card.json` and every control call sends what it advertises, through the same `a2aVersionHeader` the worker turns already use. ⚠️ A failed or versionless card read returns `undefined`, which sends no header — the previous behaviour, and what a controller with no version check still accepts — so this degrades rather than breaks. A new test pins every `a2aSendRequestFor` call site to the version, mirroring the existing worker-facing guard, because a site that drops it silently restores the refusal.

- fix: **the cap fix's own reporting now tells the truth about which cap ran, how long a row waited, and whether an action left the building.** Review findings on the previous commit, all of them numbers that would have misreported: `render()` printed the module-level `MAX_ACTIONS` in the `HELD BACK` header while `plan()` ran with `--max-actions`, so a `--max-actions 2` round claimed "cap of 3"; the acted-row line and both message builders printed the constant `THRESHOLD` while the trigger is now `>=`, so a row held back and acted at streak 3 was reported as "streak 2" and told it had waited 2 rounds; `drive-share.py`'s `sent is None` fallback still tested `unverifiable_streak == 2`, so a row acted at streak 3 rendered "no action taken" although a nudge or escalation was taken — it reads the ledger's `unverifiable_action` marker now, which is the field that actually records an action. `commands/steps/drive.md` also now **truncates `/tmp/drive-sent.txt` before writing it**: the path is fixed, so a round that sent nothing and did not truncate left the previous round's ids in place and `--sent` credited sends that never happened — the exact overstatement that flag exists to remove. Dedupe merges the two copies of a session rather than keeping the first, so step 0's `reprobe_verdict`/`reprobe_probe` pair survives when the digest copy comes first and the row does not re-signal `PARKED`. Two comments that stated the opposite of the code (`exactly 2`) are corrected. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the 2-round unverifiable rule acts on at most three sessions a round, and an action now fires once per silence rather than being pinned to the count.** The first `v0.201.0` round put **112 sessions** at `unverifiable_streak: 1` in one tick, because the field was new and every retained row started from nothing — so the next round would have opened up to 112 pane reads and emitted up to 112 nudges or attention cards at once. Most were rows with no task file or long-dead sessions, whose only possible action is a card saying "no pane resolves": nothing the operator can DO, which is the test every card has to pass. `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py` now caps actions at `MAX_ACTIONS = 3` per round (`--max-actions`), ordered by longest streak first then session id so a backlog drains oldest-first and nothing starves, and `render()` names every held-back row under `UNVERIFIABLE HELD BACK` — a cap that printed nothing would be indistinguishable from a threshold that stopped firing. ⚠️ **The cap exposed a latent defect in the trigger and the fix is not the obvious one.** A held-back row keeps advancing its streak, so the `streak == 2` equality test could never match it again and it would have been acted on *never*; the trigger is now `streak >= 2` and what stops a repeat is a new ledger field, `unverifiable_action` (`"" | nudge | escalation`), validated by `drive-ledger.py`.
