The marker also carries the ordinary case — a session silent for six rounds gets one action, not five — and a verdict that is not `unverifiable` clears it with the streak, so a session that recovers and later goes silent is acted on again. ⚠️ **Two more defects surfaced in the same round and are fixed here.** (1) A session can arrive in the entries **twice** — it is in step 0's retained set *and* in the digest's candidate set — which cost two pane reads and two identical nudge lines, and, worse, made `drive-ledger.py merge` **refuse** the whole round's ledger write; `plan()` now dedupes by session id and rewrites the list so the merge payload is clean. (2) `drive-share.py` counted a **drafted** action as taken, but the drive leg only drafts and the round sends — so a round with every send deliberately held still printed `share: 2/112 (2%)`, crediting two messages that never left. The round now writes the ids it actually sent to or posted a card for and passes them as `--sent`; that set is the only authority, an unsent draft renders `drafted, not sent`, and a missing `--sent` prints `send state unknown` in the block header rather than letting the numerator overstate. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.202.0

- feat: **Incident ledger — a blocker closes only on a verified resume.** `scripts/incident-ledger.py` records every worker blocker (permission prompt, question, stall) with owner, opened-at, deadline and outcome, and closes it only on the worker's next tool call or task change after the answer — never on a card close, which the attention store records as CLEARED rather than answered. `share --days <n>` prints the manager-resolved share, with an answered-but-never-resumed incident counted in the denominator only. `escalate` posts a board card for an incident past its deadline and stamps its caller (`round:<id>` from the periodic round, `manual:<session>` from a hand-run), so "escalated automatically" is an observation rather than an assertion.

## v0.201.0

- feat: **a session unverifiable for two consecutive rounds now has its pane read and gets a nudge or an escalation, and every round prints the share of detected problems that ended in a verified action.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` classified a row `unverifiable` when a probe could not run, no task resolved, or no `CLOSERS` row existed — and its verdict table then said "observation only, never nudged". That is right for one round and wrong for a run of them: the row leaves the digest's candidate set, `drive-retained.py` keeps re-probing it from the task file, and nothing ever looks at the session. Measured 2026-10-21: one drive leg marked 99 of 101 candidates unverifiable and did nothing. `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py` advances a per-session `unverifiable_streak` carried on the drive ledger, and at **exactly 2** resolves the pane (`who-needs-me.py --pane-for`), reads it (`wezterm cli get-text`), and acts once: a closer line on the pane is quoted back to the session verbatim as read-only context that authorises nothing, and a pane that resolves no closer — gone, unreadable, or rendering none — raises an escalation naming the session and why. ⚠️ **Exactly 2, never `>=`:** acting on every later round re-nags the same silent session, which is the suppression rule the ledger already exists for. ⚠️ **The pane read lives in the script, not the agent** — `agents/fleet-drive.md` forbids the agent looking for a pane itself, and a markdown file has no callable seam, so the acceptance replay could not have discriminated a build that reads the pane from one that reprints a stored string; `decide()` is that seam and `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py` stubs it and counts. The streak is consecutive only when the entry's `unverifiable_round` equals the ledger's own `round`, so a gap resets it with nothing having to notice — and a verdict that is not `unverifiable` writes 0, which is what makes "unverifiable in round 1, verified in round 2" start a fresh run at 1 rather than continue an old count. Second half: `scripts/drive-share.py` prints `share: <verified>/<detected> (<pct>%)`, `0/0 (n/a)` when nothing was detected. Detected = digest rows classed `stalled`/`parked` (or naming an error) plus this round's `unverifiable` entries; verified = a detected row where an action was taken **and** the session's transcript moved past the round start or its task file changed. ⚠️ **The exclusion is the whole content of the number** — a hardcoded ratio satisfies every shape-only check of it, so `test_action_without_movement_counts_as_unverified` drives a row that took an action and did not move and asserts it counts as unverified. The round start is a floor rather than the action's own timestamp, deliberately: a `stalled` row has no movement by definition and a `parked` row is idle, so movement after the round start is attributable to the round for exactly the rows measured, and a row already progressing is not in the detected set at all. `drive-ledger.py` gains `unverifiable_streak`/`unverifiable_round` as validated fields (a non-int count is refused, not silently zeroed, because the count gates an action). Wiring rides the existing step files, not `commands/fleet-drive.md` — that is the loop's wakeup target and sits 33 bytes under its 10 240-byte tick cap. Change set: `scripts/drive-unverifiable.py`, `scripts/drive-share.py`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_unverifiable.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_share.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/drive.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.200.1

- fix: **the A2A client now negotiates the protocol version from the worker's Agent Card, so a worker serving 1.0 can be driven again.** Every turn, push registration and push read-back is a fresh request to the worker's own JSONRPC handler, and that handler runs a version gate: a request carrying no `A2A-Version` header has its client version defaulted to `"0.3"` and is refused with `-32009 ... requested "0.3", served "1.0"` by any service serving 1.0 — inside a well-formed HTTP 200 envelope, so every transport check passed while nothing ran. Measured 2026-10-22 against a worker on agent-claude v0.23.0 (a2a-go v2.7.0): the identical request without the header is refused, and with `A2A-Version: 1.0` it reaches parameter validation instead. `resolveA2AEndpoint` now returns the card's `supportedInterfaces[0].protocolVersion` beside its `url`; the worker record carries it so a follow-up turn and both push-config calls speak it without re-reading the card; and a card naming no version sends no header rather than a default. ⚠️ **A hardcoded `"1.0"` was rejected for the same reason a hardcoded `"0.3"` was the defect:** `a2a.Version` is a constant on the SERVER side, so a client that pins it breaks against the first peer serving a different one — and the assertion lives at the card→builder path in `a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, not at the builders, so a hardcoded default in the resolver fails the suite. The controller plane (`a2aSendRequestFor`) is deliberately untouched: a2a-worker-controller is a different service serving its own version, and it needs its own card read when it upgrades. Change set: `server/a2a-client.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/message-channel.mjs`, `server/a2a-client.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/message-channel.test.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.200.0

- fix: **a worker no longer inherits its goal's launcher.** Launcher selection is keyed on ROLE: a manager takes the vault's configured `manager_launcher` (reported `manager-default`), a worker takes the vault's configured `worker_launcher` (reported `worker-default`), and an operator-set task-level `launcher:` still wins for that task. The goal rung is gone from both the spawn path (`resolveTaskLauncher`) and the resume path (`resolve_launcher` in `scripts/restart-worker.py`) — a goal's `launcher:` describes how its MANAGER runs, and inheriting it put every worker under 12 private-personal goals on Opus. Goal frontmatter is left in place and is now inert for workers. The `-claude` sibling stays as the manager's compatibility rung (`claude-sibling`) for a vault-cli too old to emit `manager_launcher`, so the two sources never look alike in a spawn response. Docs: `docs/fleet-surface.md` items 8 and § Which launcher a manager opens on, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `commands/open.md`, `commands/open-a2a.md` (which records that the a2a controller still applies the older goal-inheriting rule). Change set: `server/spawn-cwd.mjs`, `server/spawn-cwd.test.mjs`, `server/task-launcher.mjs`, `server/task-launcher.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `scripts/manager-launcher.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `docs/`, `commands/`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **a fleet round below its worker target now ranks the next focus from the operator's own periodic plans.** The round swept live sessions and classified them; it never read the pages the operator writes to rank work, so a free slot went to whatever was filed last and today's most important item could wait behind a lower one. `scripts/next-focus.py` reads the current weekly, monthly, quarterly and yearly plans plus the task board and emits one ranked list — due/flagged today first, then weekly, monthly, quarterly, yearly — each planned row citing the plan line it came from (a due-today row cites its own task file, since no plan line names it). A `phase: todo` row renders `needs your approval` and carries no action at all: `todo` is the operator's approval inbox, and the key's absence is the rule rather than an empty value. Important items in no current plan (`priority: 1` tasks, a firing prod alert, a goal at `status: hold` with `blocked_by:`) are proposed for the week, at most three per round, each with its own stable dedup key — **distinct even for names that slug alike**, since `A B` and `A-B` both collapse to `a-b` and the store suppresses on that key, so a digest of the raw name is appended — and a **shell-quoted** `payload` — that text is an alert line or a worker-chosen filename, so it must never reach the round's command line unquoted; 38 open P1 rows existed on 2026-10-21, so an unbounded rule would have put 38 cards on the board at once. Every read is three-valued — an unreadable plan, an `unread` alert fetch and an unreadable capacity store each render as themselves, never as "nothing to do", because a suppressed round and a round that found nothing must never render the same. **Any non-zero exit is a refusal the round renders**, and an unexpected failure is mapped to that same exit 2 rather than escaping as a traceback — a traceback is a state the render contract defines no output for. ⚠️ **Two reads were hardened against the vault's real shapes:** a non-UTF-8 page raises `UnicodeDecodeError`, which is a `ValueError` and **not** an `OSError`, so it escaped the guard and killed the tick — it now reports `unread`; and `blocked_by` is written as a **block** even for a single entry (`agents/goal-sweep-reader.md`), so the frontmatter reader collects block-style list entries instead of returning the empty scalar that made the hold-goal class never fire. ⚠️ **The alert read executes a vault-resident script**, and the vault is worker-writable — an accepted boundary because that script is the operator's own declared alert source, with `--alerts-script <path>` as the way to point the reading outside the vault. It rides the round's existing every-tick readings step (`commands/steps/readings.md`, now four readings) rather than adding a router row: `commands/fleet-drive.md` is the fleet loop's wakeup target under a strict 10240-byte tick budget, and the block belongs with the other readings anyway. `commands/steps/output-shape.md` gains item 2 so the block is rendered by the round rather than described by it. Change set: `scripts/next-focus.py`, `scripts/tests/test_next_focus.py`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `commands/steps/voice-notify.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.199.0

- chore: **the three `manager-*` aliases are retired.** `/supervisor:manager-loop`, `/supervisor:manager-drive` and `/supervisor:manager-status` existed for exactly one reason — a pre-split loop's armed `ScheduleWakeup` prompt names them, so deleting them outright would have stopped every live loop silently on the first `/reload-plugins` after the release. `docs/session-tiers.md` carried the end condition ("deleted in a later release once no armed loop carries the old prompt"), and this release meets it: checked over every live session's transcript before deleting, 37 live sessions hold an armed wakeup and **0** carry an old alias — all name `goal-drive`, `topic-drive` or `fleet-drive`. `llms.txt` still advertised the retired commands and their agents, in both the slash form and a slashless `Agent: supervisor:manager-drive` form, plus `manager-verify`, which named a command file that never existed (its successors are `topic-verify` / `goal-verify`); the worker/agent block now names the tier commands. `scripts/check-budget.py`'s two wakeup-target comments and `scripts/restart-worker.py`'s re-arm message also named the old prompt and now name the tier loops. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-status.md` (all deleted), `docs/session-tiers.md`, `llms.txt`, `scripts/check-budget.py`, `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.198.0

- fix: **a shipping TAB worker now starts in `acceptEdits` instead of parking on the operator's mode switch.** `shipping: true` was headless-only by construction: the tab path builds a shell command line and never reaches the SDK `settings` option, so `shippingSupportError` refused the combination outright. The tab path now carries the same mode through the launcher's own seam — `shippingTabEnv` exports `PERMISSION_MODE=acceptEdits`, which `shellEnvExports` writes onto the tab's command line and the launcher passes to `--permission-mode`. ⚠️ **The launcher is part of the contract, and that is why the refusal was rewritten rather than deleted:** every `cc-*` launcher used to `export PERMISSION_MODE="auto"` unconditionally, overwriting whatever the spawn set — accepted and quietly ignored, so the worker would still park on the gate `shipping` exists to remove while the reply said it was granted. Every launcher now defaults it (`${PERMISSION_MODE:-auto}`, in `bborbe/scripts`), and `shippingSupportError` REFUSES a tab spawn whose launcher does not, rather than trusting one. `acceptEdits` is chosen because it is not in `POLICY_UNREACHABLE_MODES`, so `permissions.allow` and every `PreToolUse` hook — including `deny-prod-deploy.sh` — still apply; the cluster and `docker` / `k8s` refusals are unchanged. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker gains item 9, which is the rule's single home, and `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6 gains the read-and-pass half — without it a row carrying `shipping: true` would be spawned without the argument, so the field would be recorded and never consumed. ⚠️ One budget entry rises deliberately: `commands/open.md`, a new rule rather than padding. ⚠️ **The PR review found four defects, all fixed here.** (a) **`PERMISSION_MODE` is now a RESERVED env name** (`server/spawn-mode.mjs`): defaulting it in the launchers made a caller-supplied value reach `--permission-mode` on *any* tab, so `spawn_agent(interactive: true, env: {PERMISSION_MODE: "bypassPermissions"})` would have started a worker with every approval gate disabled — the mode this whole design refuses. Only `shippingTabEnv` may set it now. (b) **`launcherHonoursModeEnv` no longer matches commented-out text** and additionally requires the launcher to actually forward `--permission-mode`, so a commented default beside a live unconditional export can no longer pass as honoured. (c) **`commands/steps/goal-spawn.md` now reads `shipping:` too**, and Step 3's call shape in `commands/open.md` carries it — otherwise a shipping row opened through the goal path was spawned without the argument, the same recorded-never-consumed failure. (d) **The docs no longer claim the tab path carries `SHIPPING_ALLOW`**: it carries the mode only, so a shipping tab worker's unattended set is the *ambient* `permissions.allow`, which is the real blast radius and is stated as such. ⚠️ **A second review round found two more, also fixed.** (e) **The `spawn_agent` schema's `shipping` description had become a second, drifting source** — it still advertised headless-only and promised the three verbs on the tab path, contradicting both the docs and the spawn sites; it now states tab support and the mode-only carrier. (f) **`launcherHonoursModeEnv` is now order-aware**: the LAST assignment must be the defaulting form and must precede the `--permission-mode` use, so a defaulting export followed by a later unconditional one can no longer pass while silently overwriting the spawn's value. The reserved-name refusal also branches by name, since `PERMISSION_MODE` fails for a different reason than the spawn-mode keys. Change set: `server/shipping-settings.mjs`, `server/shipping-settings.test.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/open.md`, `commands/steps/goal-spawn.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.197.0

- feat: **a round's readings are computed, not described — and a round that skips them can no longer render a clean table.** On 2026-10-21 the BRO-21389 manager's round receipt read `"fields": {}`: step 2 never ran, the sweep reader received no liveness / in-flight / feed / external-wait verdicts, and a worker denied `gh pr merge` twice bucketed `progressing` on liveness alone. `scripts/round-readings.py` now computes all four verdict families for the tracked set and writes `<slug>.readings`, and `manager-receipt.py` gains a `readings` step with a shape predicate over the four vocabularies, so round-tail step 8 prints `skipped: readings` for a round that skipped it. `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` gains input 16: it verifies the file with `round-readings.py --verify` first and, on a `REFUSED:` line, renders **no table** rather than degrading to UNVERIFIED — an UNVERIFIED cell still renders a table, which is what made the incident read as a complete sweep. The same input makes `stuck: denied|frozen` win over the feed's `gated` branch, so a worker that declared a denial renders `⚠️ problem` rather than `⌛ waiting-on-human`. `commands/worker-drive.md` gains the worker half: a denial writes `<sid>.denial.json`, messages the manager and posts an operator card under a stable `denial:<hash>` dedup key, because a classifier denial raises no `permission` gate and writes no `pending_permissions` entry — the attention event log has no `answer`/`decision` field at all, so nothing else can see it. Change set: `scripts/round-readings.py`, `scripts/tests/test_round_readings.py`, `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `commands/steps/round-readings.md`, `commands/steps/round-tail.md`, `commands/worker-drive.md`, `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ Two budget entries rise deliberately: `agents/goal-sweep-reader.md` by 2174 bytes and `commands/worker-drive.md` by 1847 — both are new rules, not padding.

## v0.196.2
- fix: **the drive leg's `RETAINED` and `PARKED` blocks are rendered by the script, not summarised by the agent.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` has required one line per row since `v0.194.0`, and the round of `2026-10-21T05-07-35Z` collapsed 99 retained rows into a single summary line anyway — carrying none of the three things the section exists to show: the round tag, the command run that round, and that round's result. A prohibition the agent must remember loses to an agent under load, so `drive-retained.py` gains a `render` subcommand that prints both blocks with one line per row, each carrying `round <tag>`, the probe command and that round's result; step 0 now pastes its output verbatim. Measured on a copy of the live ledger: 99 `RETAINED` rows rendered from the same data that produced the one-line summary, the ledger's `shasum` unchanged across the run. Change set: `scripts/drive-retained.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.196.1
- fix: **the drive leg's `PARKED` signal is deduplicated again.** `drive-retained.py` compared step 0's own observation against the entry's `verdict` — the agent's step-4 conclusion — but the two vocabularies never coincide: the re-probe emits `needs-step-4`, which `VERDICTS` deliberately omits because step 4 resolves it, so a live row re-signalled on every round. Measured 2026-10-21 on the deployed `v0.195.1` artifact: two consecutive sandbox rounds over one digest produced byte-identical `PARKED` blocks for the same 6 rows. Step 0's observation now lives in its own `reprobe_verdict`/`reprobe_probe` fields and the dedup key is those; entries written before this change read as a first observation, so the first round after deploy re-signals each retained row once. Change set: `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/drive-retained.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.196.0
- feat: **`TOKEN_KEYS` gains `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_OCTOPUS_DEV`.** The supervisor now resolves the octopus-dev controller token from TeamVault (`ROnzvO`, the personal instance `mcp-start.py` already reads) the same way it resolves the nuke and docker ones, so `k8s:octopus-dev` can authenticate once `a2a.url.octopus-dev` is present. Change set: `scripts/mcp-start.py`, `scripts/tests/test_mcp_start.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.195.1

- fix: **`RETAINED` and `PARKED` render one line per row, never a summary.** The first `v0.194.0` round (`~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/2026-10-20T21-16-09Z.drive`) collapsed 74 retained rows into a single line — `74 rows, all unverifiable: … Full list: python3 drive-retained.py reprobe` — with zero per-row lines, and that line carried none of the three things the section exists to show: the round tag, the command run that round, and that round's result. The template now carries the round tag and says the probe is the one run *this* round, and an explicit rule states that a summary is a failure rather than a style choice, because a summary is exactly the shape a build that never re-probed would also produce. Change set: `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.195.0

- feat: **a clock-bound worker now parks its task and closes instead of holding a fleet slot.** On 2026-10-20 the fleet held 41 live sessions against `maxConcurrent` 30, with idle sessions waiting hours to days on clocks and watchers, and nothing told such a worker to use the existing `defer_date` resurface. `commands/worker-drive.md` § *Park a clock-bound session* is the rule's single home. It applies when every open item is clock- or event-bound and more than 2 h out. The worker then sets `defer_date` (an event wait gets a bounded re-check date at most one day out), records `later (on <trigger>)`, and runs sync-progress → session-close → `/exit`. One doable item voids the park. `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` classifies an idle session on an `in_progress` task with a future `defer_date` as `deferred`, ranked above the closer row so the park's own closer cannot shadow it. `agents/fleet-drive.md` verifies those two facts plus the park's `later (on <trigger>)` Progress line and lists the row under `FREEABLE TABS` as `deferred <date>`, for the round to close through `/supervisor:close`, which already accepts a deferred task. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ The `commands/worker-drive.md` budget entry rises by 1951 bytes, deliberately: the park rule is a new rule, not padding.

- fix: **an ANSWERED propose-a-manager card no longer re-asks every round.** The store's dedup suppression is open-scoped, so once the card was answered the next tick wrote a fresh row under the same `fleet-propose-manager:<goal>` key — measured 2026-10-19 on 0.180.0: eight cards posted, all eight answered `start manager` within 64 s, eight posted again next round (6 open keys returned identical ids, 2 answered keys wrote new rows), repeated in round 3. `scripts/propose-managers.py` now owns a declined-ask state (`~/.claude/state/manager-propose/<key-slug>.json`), mirroring `scripts/under-target-state.py` rather than deriving a second policy: one file per **card key** (the two cards ask different questions of the same goal, so a goal-keyed file would let an answered approve card suppress the start-manager card the approval makes legitimate), `row_set` = the goal's approved non-terminal titles (broader than the card's live-phase list, so a parked row can carry its bit), and the sibling's four `compare` arms (new row · unknown · became unstartable · became startable) as the single derivation — arms 3 and 4 as TRANSITIONS, because this branch's `row_set` includes parked rows and a state test would re-post every round for any goal holding one. `--post` writes the snapshot and compares before posting; `--compare` is the standalone verdict (0 suppress · 10 re-post · 3 no snapshot · 2 refused) the step file runs first; `--clear --goal` removes a spent snapshot on `ANSWERED: start manager`, so a goal that later loses its manager is askable again. The policy's one home stays `commands/steps/goal-under-target.md`'s declined-ask bullet, cited not restated. Change set: `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py`, `commands/steps/propose-manager.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.194.0

- feat: **`fleet-digest.py` reports a fleet round that stopped being produced.** The fleet drive leg acts only on a digest round — no round, no `CLASSIFICATION` set, so no `parked` candidate, no `revive` verdict and no nudge — and nothing watched digest production: `grep -rln 'fleet-handback'` returned only `CHANGELOG.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` and `commands/steps/sweep.md`. Measured 2026-10-20: the retained digests run `2026-10-18T12:52:46Z` then nothing until `2026-10-19T21:58:31Z` — a **33h05m gap** spanning the day-end census, in which four of the six `idle-with-open-work` rows were never dispatched to at all. `--check` ages the newest round's own `snapshot written:` line against a 1080 s limit and exits 10 once per lapse, with the following healthy round clearing it; `--state` prints one line and always exits 0; `--stale` reports the watcher's own liveness, since a dead watcher prints nothing and silence is not evidence. Three traps are pinned by tests: the newest round is ordered by its parsed timestamp rather than its filename (`2026-10-20T08-43-00-02-00.digest` holds `06:43:35Z`), the nine non-timestamp snapshot forms resolve to parsed/unknown and never to "fresh" (a silent miss) nor "parse failure" (a false report), and a gap the watcher itself did not run across is `UNOBSERVED` rather than a lapse, so a sleeping host is not reported as a lapsed loop. Change set: `scripts/fleet-digest.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_digest.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **the drive leg re-probes a retained row instead of dropping it.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 1 rebuilds its candidates from the digest every round, so a row that landed `blocked` or `unverifiable` and then left the digest was never looked at again. Measured 2026-10-20 on round `round14-2026-10-20T13-10Z`: `9bcd20f1` and `f61ca42d` are absent from that round's digest (`grep -c` returns 0 for both) yet still present in the ledger, which is the only record that either was ever parked. New step 0 re-probes every `blocked`/`unverifiable` ledger entry from its task file before the candidate scan; the payload gains a `RETAINED` section (every retained row, every round — the re-probe evidence) and a `PARKED` section (one line per row whose parking verdict or probe changed, deduplicated through the entry's `suppression` field so an unchanged row does not re-post every round). The ledger entry gains `task_file` — the only field that can resolve a task, because `session_id` is an 8-char display handle (prefix-matching it against `claude_session_id:` is the anti-pattern `fleet-sweep-reader` step 8 forbids) and `name` is a session name that need not equal the filename (measured: `Row Whose Body Demands…` against `A Row Whose Body Demands…`). The re-probe itself lives in `scripts/drive-retained.py`, not in the agent's prose: a stubbed-probe test can only discriminate against a copy if there is a callable probe to stub, and `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py` counts the invocations. Liveness delegates to `scripts/session-liveness.py` rather than scanning the session registry — that instrument accepts the 8-char prefix the ledger carries and separates ABSENT from UNKNOWN, so an unreachable store can never read as a dead session and invent an orphan. Change set: `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/drive-retained.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_retained.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.193.0

- feat: **a goal that a live session anchors on but that no manager was ever armed for is now a reported state, not a probe failure.** `manager-liveness.py --state` answered `INVALID — unreadable cadence file` for such a goal — a *probe failure* — so "needs a manager started" was indistinguishable from "cannot measure". Measured 2026-10-20 that was every goal named by the six idle sessions of the 2026-10-19 census, so a correct `revive` nudge had no manager loop to land on. `classify()` gains `UNARMED` (no record under either name) and resolves the **pre-split** bare `<slug>` record as well as the prefixed one: `migrate_legacy` deletes a legacy record only once its twin is written, so a subject that never re-armed kept one, and three of the five census subjects read `INVALID` despite having a record — one had armed the same day, one was deliberately stood down (32 legacy `.stopped` files sat against 2 prefixed). `scripts/unarmed-goals.py` is the reverse index the sweep structurally cannot be — live sessions → non-terminal tasks → `goals:` → `--state` — reporting a goal only when it has **both** a live session and no record, so a goal with no live session is never reported and a lapsed or stood-down one stays in its own class. A `themes:`-only task is listed as `NO-SUBJECT` rather than dropped. `commands/steps/readings.md` carries the per-tick line, `docs/sweep-output.md` the `unarmed` disposition, and `topic-keepalive.py` no longer folds `UNARMED` into restart-worthy — a restart resumes a session that never armed a loop. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/unarmed-goals.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_unarmed_goals.py`, `commands/steps/readings.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/topic-drive.md`, `docs/sweep-output.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.192.0

- feat: `mcp-start.py` resolves `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_SUN` from TeamVault, so `k8s:sun` (sun-k3s) authenticates against its own controller bearer

## v0.191.6

- fix: **the goal-tier reap predicate no longer reaps a session parked on the operator's own `approve:`/`pick` gate.** `scripts/reap-closer.py` gains a fourth verdict, `PARKED` (exit 3), for a last closer carrying a standing `approve:` or `pick`; `agents/goal-drive.md`'s fourth read renders such a row under `Parked on operator` and sends no notice. Operator ruling **2026-10-20** (attention card `92f0e524bdd5c9c0b09c063b06363575`, `ANSWERED: option: stop reaping`): the gate's own attention card already reaches the operator, so a reap notice is redundant, the session can only refuse it, and the refusal trains sessions to discount the reap channel. `scripts/close-line.py` accepts `--closer parked` and renders the candidate with its own reason — it must not be folded into `closed`, because the session is live and has not closed. Any other OPEN closer (`review`, `you run`, `later`, or anything unrecognised) is still reaped exactly as before, and UNREADABLE still never suppresses. Change set: `agents/goal-drive.md`, `scripts/reap-closer.py`, `scripts/close-line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_closer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_line.py`, `commands/steps/goal-sweep.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ The `agents/goal-drive.md` budget entry rises by 1527 bytes — deliberate: a fourth verdict branch is a new rule, not padding.

## v0.191.5

- fix: **`spawn_agent`'s `task` description names the retired `cluster` target where the requirement actually bites on the A2A targets.** The clause read `REQUIRED with \`target: "cluster"\``, but `cluster` was retired on 2026-10-15 and the requirement now lives in `a2aSpawnArgumentRefusal` (`server/a2a-spawn.mjs`), which refuses every A2A target — `docker`, `k8s`, `k8s:<cluster>` — without a `task`; a caller reading the schema passed `target: "k8s:<cluster>"` and lost the call to `target "k8s:octopus-dev" requires \`task\``. The clause now names those targets and keeps the retired one, matching both enforcement sites (`server/a2a-spawn.mjs`; `server/supervisor.mjs` § `spawnClusterWorker`). Change set: `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § the three-way exclusion states that a probe which temporarily weakens a live control announces the weakening and its revert in the same message as the edit.** Measured 2026-10-20: `private-personal`'s allowlist in `~/.config/claude-supervisor/vaults.json` was widened to include `octopus-dev` for a mutation probe with no announcement, and the operator stopped it on sight of the diff. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.191.4

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py` reports a lapsed manager after one cadence, not two.** `classify()` built its limit as `2 x PERIOD + SLACK`, so a stopped loop stayed unreported for two cadence periods — measured live on 2026-10-20, every production report landed at 1860–2040 s against a 900–960 s cadence, and a planted-lapse probe took 681 s on a 300 s cadence. The limit is now `PERIOD + SLACK`. Halving the multiplier alone is not enough: PERIOD was the median arm-to-arm gap, and the median is what a *healthy* tick costs, so a limit built from it fires on every ordinary slow tick — replayed over the retained `.arms` history (41 subjects, 374 gaps, 12.56 subject-days) a plain 1x rule gives 5.02 events/manager-day against the old rule's 1.35, 33 of the 46 extra events being near-misses inside 1.5x the median. PERIOD is therefore the **p90** of the observed gaps, **capped at 2.5x their median** — the p90 absorbs jitter, the cap stops one historic stop from inflating a subject's limit for ever (uncapped, the worst subject sat at 13.4x its own median). On the same snapshot that rule gives 1.91 events/manager-day, a median limit/period ratio of 1.22, and a worst subject at 2.69x. `SLACK` moves 60 → 180 s. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a message the TUI accepts into its queue now reports `delivered: true` instead of `unverified`, which restores the `delivered: false` state that v0.191.2 made unreachable.** `confirmSubmission` treated any composer that did not hold its own text as "text this send did not write" and returned `unverified` — but a queued message leaves exactly that, a `Press up to edit queued messages` notice. Every accepted message therefore read `unverified`, and a genuinely swallowed send landed in the same bucket, so the state the three-state contract exists to distinguish never fired. A composer matching one of `QUEUED_HINTS` is now positive evidence of acceptance and returns `delivered: true` with `via: 'queued'`; `unverified` is reserved for a composer that is neither empty, nor ours, nor a recognised acceptance notice; `delivered: false` again means our own text survived the Enter retries. `send_agent_message` surfaces `via` alongside `delivered`. The cost is a coupling to the queue notice's wording, accepted deliberately: it is the only positive signal that exists, and the alternative is the misclassification this replaces. Change set: `server/tab.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/tab.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.191.3

- fix: **a worker whose goal has no live manager is no longer parented to the topic manager.** `scripts/fleet-board.py`'s `manager_on_chain` walked `[goal] + topics_of(goal)`, so a task declaring a goal with no live goal manager resolved to the **topic** manager that lists the goal — a topic→worker edge the tier contract forbids (`docs/session-tiers.md` § The four tiers; `commands/topic-loop.md`: "A topic manager never manages a worker"). It now parents a worker only to the goal manager of a declared goal, falling to the fleet manager (the root) as a holding row when none is live; the two documented exceptions stand (`managed_by` naming a topic, and a goal-less task directly under a topic). `docs/session-tiers.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Which manager owns a subject now state that tier 4 owns the unmanaged **goal** for starting, not the worker's parent. Change set: `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_board.py`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.191.2

- fix: **`send_agent_message` reports delivery from a post-send read, not from keystroke delivery, so a swallowed Enter can no longer read as a sent message.** `sendToPane` returned `{sent: true}` whenever `send-text` reached the pty — true whether or not the Enter submitted — so a pane that was mid-turn (its composer is empty, which `waitUntilReady` reads as ready, and the TUI discards the `\r` while it is still busy) produced a success return for a message that never arrived. It now returns `delivered: true` when the composer was watched clearing, `delivered: false` with a reason when the text is still there after the Enter is re-pressed, or `delivered: 'unverified'` when the pane could not be read or its composer holds text this send did not write; `sent` is kept as an alias meaning only "keystrokes reached the pty", and `send_agent_message` surfaces the state instead of a bare `sent: true`. `scripts/send-delivery-drill.py` drives the busy/idle pair against a live pane and records both transcripts. Change set: `server/tab.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/tab.test.mjs`, `scripts/send-delivery-drill.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.191.1

- fix: **`propose-managers.py`'s dry-run line names the dedup key it would actually post, so a goal in the approval inbox no longer reads as a start-manager card.** The printed line called `dedup_key()` while `post()` called `card_key()`, so a `phase: todo` goal rendered `card: fleet-propose-manager:<goal>` in the dry run and was posted under `fleet-approve-manager:<goal>` — the operator would have been sent looking for a card the store never received. Measured 2026-10-20 against a scratch vault while probing the phase gate: the POSTed bodies were correct and only the printed summary disagreed, which is why a suite that asserts on `card_for()` alone never caught it. Pinned by a `MainTest` case that stubs `goal_phase` and asserts the printed key. Change set: `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.191.0

- feat: **a row in `fleet-board.py --json` now carries the task it resolved to, so a label that drifted from its task title no longer reads as an unresolvable session.** `build_rows` already held the resolved title and rendered it into `cells[2]`, but the `sessions` projection dropped it — leaving a consumer with the registry `label` and nothing to match it against. On a session that outlived a retitle the two differ, and `--mismatches` reports exactly that set, so a reader searching the tasks dir by label finds no file: measured 2026-10-20, worker `77f7ff67` was recorded as having no task at all when its `claude_session_id` resolved fine and its `parent` was already the goal manager. Each entry now carries `task_title` and `task_title_reason`, with one wording per distinct absence (manager, unmanaged, unbound worker) rather than one sentence shared across three states; `subject` is documented as manager-scoped, since every worker row carries `null` there by design. The projection moves into a pure `build_sessions(rows)` so it is testable without a live fleet. Change set: `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_board.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.190.0

- feat: **each vault can declare the clusters its A2A workers may run on, and a spawn outside that list is refused with no fallback.** Adds `server/cluster-allowlist.mjs` — a `~/.config/claude-supervisor/vaults.json` reader shaped `{"vaults": {"<vault>": ["<cluster>", …]}}` — with deliberately fail-closed semantics that differ from `config-reload.mjs` on purpose: an unreadable or missing file **refuses** rather than reading as "no restriction", a malformed file refuses rather than keeping the previous permissions (a narrowing edit that failed to parse must not silently widen), and a file that vanishes mid-edit keeps the last good value so a rename race cannot fail open. Adds `clusterAllowlistRefusal({ vault, target, allowlist })` beside `a2aSpawnArgumentRefusal` in `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`: a `k8s:<cluster>` target the vault does not declare is refused naming **both** the vault and the cluster and the clusters that vault may use, an undeclared vault is refused rather than treated as unrestricted, and a `k8s` target naming no cluster is refused rather than skipped — a guard that passes the unnamed case has a hole exactly where the boundary lives. It sits on the resolved target, so a pairing that arrived from a vault's default cluster is refused exactly like an explicit one. The guard runs at **two** points in `spawnA2AWorker`. For an explicit `k8s:<cluster>` it runs BEFORE the controller resolves, because the cluster is already in the caller's string — a forbidden pairing is refused whether or not that cluster is configured, since the resolver's own "unconfigured cluster" error would otherwise mask the boundary on exactly the pairings the rule exists to forbid (on this supervisor octopus has no URL or token, so `private-personal` → `k8s:octopus-dev` would never reach the allowlist). For a bare `k8s` it runs AFTER resolution, on `controller.target`: that target names no cluster until `resolveA2AControllerTarget` turns it into `k8s:<the default cluster>`, so judging the caller's string would refuse a legitimate default spawn while the cluster it will actually run on went unchecked. ⚠️ **A consequence worth naming: an A2A spawn must now name a `vault`**, because the allowlist is per vault and a spawn that cannot be attributed to one is refused rather than run unchecked. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7 records the carve-out: the row-eligibility rule stays ADVISORY at the call site, while the vault↔cluster boundary is a server refusal — eligibility is a judgement about the work, which the server cannot make, whereas the boundary is a statement about who may touch what, which it can decide from the `vault` and resolved `target` it already holds. Change set: `server/cluster-allowlist.mjs`, `server/cluster-allowlist.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.189.1

- fix: **a manager that re-arms and lapses again between two polls is now reported, instead of staying silent.** `check()` suppressed any subject already present in `liveness-notified.json`, and its only way to clear that entry was observing the `RECOVERED` transition — which never runs when the manager re-arms and goes stale inside the tick's one-minute gap. The entry then outlived the lapse it described and every later lapse was silent: measured 2026-10-20, `--state` read `STALE age=4328s` for `topic--development-pipeline-throughput` while `--check` exited 0 and printed nothing, and the topic had no live manager. The entry now records the epoch at which the lapse was reported, and a lapse re-notifies when the subject has armed since; a legacy list-shaped file loads at epoch 0, which is older than any arm, so it upgrades in place. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **a vault can name its own default A2A cluster, so a target-less row lands on its vault's cluster instead of one fleet-wide default.** `vault-cli`'s per-vault config gains `a2a_default_cluster`, a cluster NAME rather than a target string, and `/supervisor:open-a2a` reads it when the row carries no `target:` and passes `target: k8s:<name>` as an ordinary per-call argument. ⚠️ **This does not weaken the per-call-only rule**: the forbidden source is a *fleet-wide* default, where one config edit defaults every vault, and a value read from the row's own vault cannot default another vault's spawn — `resolveSpawnTarget` still has no env source and no config source. An unknown cluster name refuses at the server exactly as an explicit `k8s:<unknown>` does, never falling back to `a2a.defaultCluster` or to `local`; a vault that names no default keeps the existing local-backend path unchanged. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/open-a2a.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.189.0

- feat: **A goal or topic at `phase: todo` now gates manager start, and the proposer asks for the approval instead of the manager.** `phase: todo` is the operator's approval inbox — `vault-cli goal approve` / `topic approve` is the only writer that leaves it while recording `approved_by` / `approved_at` — so `propose-managers.py` emits an approve card for a qualifying goal in the inbox and no start-manager card, and `/supervisor:open`'s goal and topic branches print `⌛ waiting-approval` and spawn nothing. An **absent** `phase` is exempt and keeps today's behaviour: 291 of 312 goals and 16 of 18 topics carry no phase, so reading absent as unapproved would have blocked every one of them — the contract `vault-cli goal approve` states. The two cards carry distinct dedup keys deliberately: one shared key would let the store swallow whichever asked second, so declining a manager would silently cancel the approval ask. Change set: `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py`, `commands/open.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **an A2A spawn target can name its cluster — `k8s:<cluster>` — so one supervisor reaches several controllers, one URL and token each.** Until now `k8s` was a single target read from one `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL_K8S` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_K8S` pair, so every spawn went to nuke dev. A target is now `docker`, `k8s` (the default cluster), or `k8s:<cluster>` (`k8s:nukedev`, `k8s:octopus-dev`, …); each cluster resolves its own `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL_<CLUSTER>` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_<CLUSTER>` (uppercased, non-alphanumerics → `_`) or its own `a2a.url.<cluster>` entry, and an unknown or unconfigured cluster **REFUSES rather than defaulting** — the rule this path has always applied one level up. ⚠️ **A bare `k8s` keeps working**: it resolves to `a2a.defaultCluster` (else `SUPERVISOR_A2A_DEFAULT_CLUSTER`, else `nukedev`) and falls back to the legacy `_K8S` / `a2a.url.k8s` source **for that cluster only**, so a live setup needs no edit. ⚠️ **The roster status is the full target** (`k8s:nukedev`), and every reader of `agent.status` follows it — the ledger mode, rehydrate, the liveness fan-out and `stop_agent` — because each keyed on an exact `A2A_SPAWN_TARGETS` membership that a colon-bearing status would silently miss, leaving a running worker reading as dead and a stop unable to resolve its controller. The `spawn_agent` tool schema publishes a `pattern` instead of an `enum`: the cluster names are the operator's configuration, so a closed list would publish names the server cannot guarantee. `scripts/mcp-start.py` now carries one TeamVault lookup per cluster — `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_NUKEDEV` and `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_NUKEPROD`, each from its own key — in place of the single `_K8S`, which the server still accepts as the default cluster's fallback. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/config.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/message-channel.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `scripts/mcp-start.py`, `scripts/tests/test_mcp_start.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/open-a2a.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.188.2

- fix: **the topic tier's spawn gate stops refusing the goals it exists to start, and the fleet board resolves a task to its topic's manager.** Six defects, all measured against one live topic on 2026-10-20. ⚠️ **The gate refused an ABSENT `phase`**, so 22 of the topic's 25 goals became unstartable the moment the split shipped — the accepted design leaves the unphased goals exempt, and the topic tier is their only start path. ⚠️ **A prose bullet in the topic's `## Goals` parsed as a declared goal** (`- **Binding now (…):** …` → `**Binding now`), inflating the declared count 26 against 25 real; entries must now resolve to a goal page. ⚠️ **The N-slot count read only page-recorded `manager_session_id`s**, so a live goal manager the registry knew about read as unmanaged and the round would start a second one — the count is now the union of the page id (probed through `session-liveness.py --check`) and the fleet registry. ⚠️ **A manager whose registry name the harness truncated resolved to no subject at all**, which is what made the registry half of that union miss; `manager_subject` now falls back to the same exactly-one-prefix rule `has_task_file` already applies. ⚠️ **A task named directly in a topic's `## Goals` fell to the fleet manager**, because the goal chain had no rung for the task's own topic; `task_topics` adds it. ⚠️ **Nothing in the topic tier wrote the topic page's `manager_session_id`**, so the fleet manager's keep-alive read a stale id; `commands/steps/topic-resolve.md` gains the write, under the same writer carve-out the status reconcile carries. Change set: `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `commands/steps/topic-resolve.md`, `scripts/tests/test_topic_spawn.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_board.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.188.1

- fix: **`manager-liveness.py` accepts the deprecated `--topic` flag again, and retires the pre-split records that would have fired a false STALE on every live manager.** Renaming `--topic` to `--subject` in 0.187.0 left **no compatibility path**, and every armed manager loop's `ScheduleWakeup` prompt carries `--topic` — so a manager that reloaded and then followed the arm text already in its context failed with `unrecognized arguments: --topic` on **every tick**, and its liveness record silently stopped updating. `--topic` is now accepted as a deprecated alias for `--subject` (announced on stderr), and `--branch` is auto-detected when omitted: an existing `goal--`/`topic--` record decides it, then the vault's pages, then `goal` as an announced default. ⚠️ **The second half is the sharper one.** The state directory held **55** unprefixed `.cadence` records against **0** prefixed ones — measured 2026-10-20 after the release — so the first `--check` would have reported every pre-reload manager as lapsed while it was alive. `--arm`/`--stop` now retire a subject's flat record once its prefixed twin is written (`migrated legacy <base>`), and `--check` skips a flat record whose prefixed twin exists, for one an older release already wrote and nothing will re-arm. A flat record with no twin still reports STALE, pinned by a control test. Change set: `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: `scripts/close-worker.py` closes a session that was handed off from a task. `find_task` resolves `metrics_sessions` membership when `claude_session_id` names a successor, and that branch skips `task-not-terminal` — the session no longer owns the task, so the successor's progress is not its gate. Every other refusal still applies, and a session id appearing only in a task's body prose is still not a claim.

## v0.188.0

- fix: **an A2A spawn that fails after the controller minted a worker now writes a ledger row, so the worker can be reaped instead of becoming an untouchable orphan.** Two returns ran after a successful `create` while the worker already existed — an unreadable Agent Card and a failed session binding — and each returned its error with the worker id while writing nothing, so `stop_agent` answered `unknown agent` for a worker that was running. Measured twice on 2026-10-20: a pod refused by ResourceQuota (503 card), and a bind failure on a task name with no vault file. ⚠️ **Both paths share one `recordUnreachableWorker` helper, because the defect is one class** — patching only the card path leaves the bind path live, which is exactly what the second measurement showed. A third return (an unbuildable record) deliberately does NOT use the helper: `a2aWorkerRecord` refuses only when the target is not an A2A target, so rebuilding from the same target would fail identically and write nothing while reading as though a row had landed — that branch reports the error instead of pretending to record it. The row carries the error and `sessionId: null`, the latter deliberately: no session serves that worker, and naming the minted id would let `a2aLiveSessions` stamp a phantom session live, whereas a null session id is skipped there by construction. Change set: `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`stop_agent` accepts a bare controller worker id, so a worker with no ledger row can be stopped without the controller token leaving this server.** A spawn that fails after `create` writes no row (previous bullet), and until now the only route to that worker was the controller's own stop run by hand — which meant extracting the controller token into a shell. `stop_agent` now falls back to `stopWorkerByIdOnController`, which tries every configured target (a controller that does not own the id REFUSES it, so a wrong target costs a refusal, never a wrong removal) and uses the token the server already holds. An optional `target` narrows the attempt. The `unknown agent` refusal is kept, and now names each attempt, because "unknown" alone is what made this undiagnosable. Change set: `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`a2a-local-control.py` gains a `stop --worker-id` verb**, the route to a worker the supervisor ledger lost. `stop_agent` resolves from that ledger and answers `unknown agent` when the spawn failed after the controller had already minted the worker, so without this verb such a worker was unreachable from every session on the machine. It is keyed on the worker id, never the task name, because a task can be spawned again after its first worker died and a task-keyed stop would remove the successor. Change set: `scripts/a2a-local-control.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **three bullets moved back out of `## v0.187.1` and under `## Unreleased`, where their merge puts them.** The merge of #447 landed at 11:47 UTC, nine minutes after `v0.187.1` was tagged at 11:38, so GitHub's auto-merge appended those bullets beneath a heading that had already been renamed from `## Unreleased` — announcing v0.187.1 work the tag does not contain, and leaving the releaser no `## Unreleased` to cut. `git tag --contains ed74884e` is empty, so the work is in no tag: per the fold guard's tag rule it goes back under `## Unreleased`, never into the section the merge happened to place it in. Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.187.1

- fix: **`CHANGELOG.md` no longer carries git conflict markers, and two long-folded bullets are back in the releases that shipped them.** The merge of #449 resolved three conflicts and left this file's own in place, so `<<<<<<< HEAD` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> origin/master` sat between the manager-tier block and `## v0.186.0` — and the v0.187.0 cut then renamed that block's heading with the markers still inside it. Both sides were real content, so the repair keeps both in order and drops only the three marker lines. ⚠️ **A malformed changelog is not cosmetic on an `autoRelease` repo:** the release watcher reads this file to decide what to cut, and a marker line is neither a heading nor a bullet. Separately, the `changelog-fold` guard had been red on master for two unrelated bullets, each sitting under a release that does not contain its merge — the liveness-timeout fix (v0.184.8 → v0.184.9, merge `ae93916d`) and the `FREEABLE TABS` feature (v0.184.6 → v0.184.7, merge `a4f64e5f`). Both move INTO the later tag, per `git tag --contains`, never back into `## Unreleased`, which would announce shipped work as pending. Guard now reports `0 folded`. Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.187.0

- feat: **the manager tier is split into a TOPIC tier and a GOAL tier.** One `manager-*` command set branched on goal-vs-topic internally, which blurred two management levels and made one topic manager sweep 23 goals and 186 tracked names every 15 minutes for 150–210k tokens a round and 0–2 rows acted on. The tree is now strict — fleet → **topic** → goal → worker, each level managing only the level below it — and `<tier>-spawn` starts exactly that level: `topic-spawn` starts a **goal manager**, `goal-spawn` a **worker**. The rename: `manager-loop` → `topic-loop`/`goal-loop`; `manager-drive` → `topic-drive`/`goal-drive`; `manager-status` → `topic-status`/`goal-status`; `manager-verify` → `topic-verify`/`goal-verify`; `manager-spawn` → `goal-spawn` (plus a new `topic-spawn`); `manager-answer` → `goal-answer`; `manager-drain` → `goal-drain`. Agents: `manager-sweep-reader` → `topic-sweep-reader`/`goal-sweep-reader`, `manager-drive` → `topic-drive`/`goal-drive`, `manager-verify` → `topic-verify`/`goal-verify`, `manager-wrangler` → `goal-wrangler`. Steps split the same way, and the four both rounds read became `round-tail`/`round-readings`/`round-quiet`/`round-self-improvement`. ⚠️ **Breaking for everything except the three aliases above** — `manager-spawn`, `manager-answer` and `manager-drain` are gone with no alias, and a deployed session keeps the body it loaded until `/reload-plugins`. A `manager-` prefix on a *script* or *server module* still means "manager-tier shared", never a command: `manager-liveness.py`, `manager-predispatch.py`, `manager-receipt.py`, `manager-quiet.py`, `manager-menu-*.py`, `manager-attention-watch.py`, `manager-coverage-gate.py` and `server/manager-sessions.mjs` are unchanged.
- feat: **`topic-spawn` is a real gate, not a convention.** `scripts/topic-spawn.py` refuses with a named token on its **first line** and a non-zero exit: `goal-not-in-scope` (the goal is not in the topic's `## Goals`), `goal-not-startable` (the goal's `phase` is not `planning`/`execution` — the operator's approval boundary), `manager-cap` (whose second line names the bound: `goal-cap` at N = 3 per topic, or `manager-cap` at the fleet-wide `spawn.maxManagers` ceiling), and `workers are not this tier's to start`. **N = 3 is an inner bound and the fleet ceiling always wins** — the cap is never raised to make room. `--rank` prints the § 1 ranking (operator pin → startability → ready work desc → `priority` asc → oldest) and which goals take the N slots. The topic tier holds **no `spawn_agent` tool**: it starts a goal manager through `/supervisor:open --goal`, which already carries the hold check and the liveness guard.
- feat: **`scripts/topic-keepalive.py` keeps a topic's goal managers alive** — two independent signals per goal (the page's `manager_session_id` through `session-liveness.py`, and the loop cadence through `manager-liveness.py`), a resume rather than a second spawn, and a **3-resumes-per-goal-per-30-min** cap that prints `⚠️ MANAGER CRASH-LOOP` on the fourth. `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`MALFORMED` are cannot-tell, never death. A resume is recorded only once the liveness instrument shows the resumed id live, so a resume that never landed cannot arm the cap against its own retry.
- fix: **`manager-liveness.py`'s state slug carries the branch.** A topic and a goal may share a name — `docs/subject-resolution.md` requires exact-basename matching precisely because they do — so the flat slug let one subject's cadence record overwrite the other's in one directory. The slug is now `goal--<base>` / `topic--<base>`, the directory stays flat, and `--topic` became `--subject` with a required `--branch`. `scripts/fleet-board.py`'s second reader of that directory follows.
- feat: **`manager-loop`, `manager-drive` and `manager-status` survive as DEPRECATED aliases.** The rename removes the rest of the `manager-*` set outright, but these three cannot go in the same release: **every armed manager loop wakes by calling `/supervisor:manager-drive <subject>`** — the prompt its own `ScheduleWakeup` carries — so a rename with no alias stops every live loop on the first `/reload-plugins` after the release, **silently**, and nothing reports it until `manager-liveness.py` calls the cadence stale two intervals later. Each alias resolves the subject's tier and invokes `topic-*` or `goal-*` through `Skill`; none reimplements a procedure, and each holds only `Read` and `Skill`. Same shape as the `worker-restart` → `restart` deprecation. Delete them in a later release, once no armed loop carries the old prompt.
- docs: `docs/session-tiers.md` now declares **four** tiers, each naming what it manages, what it may start and its done condition; `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session roles gains the second rung and § Which manager owns a subject gains the tier (a goal with no live manager, under a live topic manager, is that topic manager's); `docs/subject-resolution.md`'s write-scope table enumerates all eight resolving commands. Change set: `commands/`, `agents/`, `commands/steps/`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/subject-resolution.md`, `docs/sweep-output.md`, `docs/handback-contract.md`, `docs/session-progress.md`, `docs/drive-audit-budget.md`, `scripts/topic-spawn.py`, `scripts/topic-keepalive.py`, `scripts/manager-liveness.py`, `scripts/fleet-board.py`, the `scripts/check-*.py` path constants and their tests, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.186.0

- feat: **`attention-ask.py poll` answers with the operator's four states, and neither a decline nor a hidden close releases a gate.** The operator's ruling fixes the vocabulary: *"the rest api is able to tell the session what the state is — accepted, declined, skipped/hidden or not answered — one of these 4"*. So `poll` now prints `DECLINED:` for the bottom Dismiss and `HIDDEN` for the board's corner X (bborbe/attention-controller#151), alongside the existing `OPEN` and `ANSWERED:`. ⚠️ **`DECLINED` is a behaviour change, not a rename:** a dismissed card used to arrive as `ANSWERED: declined (no answer given)`, so every command gating on `ANSWERED:` treated a decline as a release. `answered-attribution.py`'s `classify()` now reads the answer's **kind before** its client evidence — a decline is a decline whoever sent it, and asking "was this client flagged as automated?" first answered `ATTRIBUTED` for a card the operator explicitly dismissed. `answer_kind()` reads a multi-question card as whole-card only when EVERY unit carries the kind, so a card with one tab hidden and one answered is not swallowed. The five command files that name the vocabulary (`worker-drive.md`, `manager-gates.md`, `needs-input.md`, `manager-under-target.md`, `manager-tail.md`) now state that `DECLINED`, `HIDDEN` and `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED` all read as `OPEN`. Change set: `scripts/answered-attribution.py`, `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_answered_attribution.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json` (worker-drive grew by 24 bytes), the five command files, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.185.3

- fix: **a reap is handed over only when its recipient is live.** The drive leg decided `reap` from the roster the caller passed — read from the supervisor registry, which **retains ended sessions** — while the harness surface `SendMessage` addresses carries live sessions only, so a reap whose remedy is "nudge the session to close itself" had no recipient whenever the session had already ended. Measured 2026-10-03 (Manager Layer tick 86): `reaped 2` over two sessions absent from the 43-row peer list, both undeliverable, and the summary line indistinguishable from two nudges. `agents/manager-drive.md` step 1 gains a **fifth read** — probe the full id with `scripts/session-liveness.py --check` and route the verdict through the new `scripts/reap-line.py`, which prints the hand-over line **only** on `live` and `reap moot — no live recipient` otherwise; `reaped <n>` now counts delivered notices only, and the report gains a `Reap moot (<n>)` block. The vocabulary is pinned in the script's docstring: `LIVE`→`live`, `ABSENT`→`absent`, `UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS`/`MALFORMED`→`unknown`, and `parked` only from the sweep's classification — never deliverable, unlike `close-line.py`. Change set: `scripts/reap-line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_wiring.py`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json` (raised by hand for the deliberate fifth-read growth), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.185.2

- fix: **`/supervisor:close`'s `headless-target` guard now fires, instead of being unreachable.** The guard keyed on the registry's `kind` field, which every entry writes as `interactive` — a tab session and an SDK worker spawned with `interactive: false` alike — so the branch could never be taken and a headless target fell through to the task gate. It now keys on `entrypoint`, which does separate them (`cli` for a tab, `sdk-cli` for an SDK worker; measured across the live fleet on 2026-10-20: 24 × `cli`, 1 × `sdk-cli`). An absent `entrypoint` is deliberately NOT read as headless: those are entries written before the field existed, and refusing them would break closes for legitimate tab sessions — absence falls through to the pane join, which is fail-safe (an operational `❌ error:`, never a close). The unit test that made this look covered used `kind="headless"`, a value nothing in the fleet produces, so it read green against a branch no real session could reach; it now uses `sdk-cli`, and a second test pins that `kind` is not the signal. Change set: `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
## v0.185.1

- fix: **a `role: manager` row is never opened as a worker.** `commands/open.md` § Step 0.6 resolved `role:` to `human|agent` only, so a row declaring `role: manager` fell through the classifier and was spawned as an agent — measured 2026-10-19: the two tick-35 rows already carried `role: manager`, and the open path had no branch for it. Step 0.6 now renders `👤 YOURS` and spawns nothing, writes nothing and opens no window, using the vocabulary `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 4 and `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (4) already carry; the documented write values widen to `role <human|agent|manager>` so a filer can declare it. The classifier never derives `manager` — a prose-derived value was measured at ~1.1% false positives against 4/19 recall and rejected. The report line gains `yours Y (manager)`. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.185.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:close <session-id|pid>` closes a finished session's pane** — the fleet manager could open and restart a session but not close one, so every finished tab waited for the operator (nine of them on 2026-10-19). The verb refuses in seven named cases — `busy-target`, `gate-held`, `dirty-worktree`, `held-session`, `headless-target`, `task-not-terminal`, `task-unresolved` — and reuses the existing guards rather than adding a second kill mechanism: the registry read, the hold store and the refusal contract come from `restart-worker.py`, the worktree probe and cause-of-death classifier from `restart-precheck.py`, and the pane→session join from `jump.py`. The two new guards are the task gate: a task that is neither `completed`/`aborted`/`hold` nor deferred to a future `defer_date` refuses, and so does a session no task claims as its `claude_session_id`. Change set: `commands/close.md`, `scripts/close-worker.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_worker.py`, `commands/steps/rules.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/steps/manager-drive.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.11

- fix: **a failed liveness read now falls back to the last successful worker count when it is younger than 60 s, and labels it stale.** `workerSessions()` and `scripts/worker-sessions.py` write `last-worker-count.json` under the supervisor state dir on every successful count and read it when the live read fails; `concurrentLimitRefusal` decides on a fresh cached count and says so (`This decision uses the last successful count, 5 s old — the live read failed: …`). Past 60 s, or with no readable cache, the refusal stands — the fallback is never a fabricated `0`, and `--list` keeps refusing because a count has no rows to list. The cache is a FILE because `worker-sessions.py --count` exits after printing, so an in-memory cache could never outlive it. Change set: `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.10

- fix: **a `finished` row now carries its jump link, so every `FREEABLE TABS` row has a pane to hand over.** `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 7b attempted `--pane-for` only for a `parked` `CLASSIFICATION` row and the `<link>` column admitted only `BLOCKED` / `CLOSERS` / `parked`, while the `FREEABLE TABS` population added in v0.184.6 is `finished` rows — so round 55's two finished rows (`Start Day - 2026-10-19`, `Cleanup Email Inbox (Personal) - 2026-10-19`) rendered `—` and the drive leg printed `no pane — digest carried none` for sessions live in panes 973 and 974. `pane_for()` was healthy throughout (`--pane-for a8c9ba13` → `973`, `88a2dd9a` → `974`, exit 0): the lookup was never attempted. Both clauses now admit `finished`, and two redundant clauses were trimmed in the same file to stay under the 30 KB body cap (30715 → 30674 bytes). Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.9

- fix: **a slow-but-healthy store no longer refuses the spawn: the liveness timeouts are 15 s and a failed read is retried once.** 5 s sat inside the observed tail of a loaded attention store — 12 concurrent `/session-heartbeat` reads each took ~0.95 s on 2026-10-19 (load avg 74–91), and the fleet lost spawns to `UNKNOWN` reads that succeeded 20/20 minutes later. `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS`, `SESSION_LEDGER_TIMEOUT_MS`, `session-liveness.py`'s `_HTTP_TIMEOUT` and `spawn-ledger.py`'s copy are now **15 s, kept equal**, and each reader retries once after ~250 ms before declaring `null`/`None` — the failed attempt is logged with `(attempt 1 of 2, retrying)` so a recovered read is distinguishable from one that never failed. Two failed attempts still refuse: the retry widens the window in which a healthy store can prove itself; it never turns a failure into a count. Change set: `server/read-diagnostics.mjs`, `server/session-heartbeat.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.mjs`, `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/spawn-ledger.py`, `server/read-diagnostics.test.mjs`, `server/session-heartbeat.test.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.test.mjs`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: the `FREEABLE TABS` row no longer repeats the task name when it equals the session name — a worker's session name is usually its task name, so the row printed the same string twice (measured 2026-10-19, round 55: `Start Day - 2026-10-19 · Start Day - 2026-10-19 · finished · …`). The task column is omitted when it is equal to the name. Change set: `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.8

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` no longer reads a `🔵 READY` panel's follow-up line as a machine wait.** The deployed v0.184.7 sweep, checked against pane-bottom reads recorded before it ran (2026-10-19 23:29, 16 of 17 matched, zero `unclassified`), labelled pane 816 `external`: its panel declared `🔵 READY · you decide` with an operator action on the `👤 You:` line, and its `⏰ Next: I re-run … merge` — what the session does after the operator acts — was taken as a machine, because parked rows never received the panel's declaration. `state_of()` now gets `declared` on the parked path too, and a `⏰` line counts as a machine only under a panel that declares no state or a wait — the rule `machine_wait()` already applies to a `nothing` closer. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.7

- feat: the fleet round now renders a `FREEABLE TABS` section — every `finished — reap candidate` row plus every `parked` row idle ≥12 h whose task carries a `claude_session_id` (the resume mechanism) — each with its `jump-link.py` handover, so the operator can free a spawn-cap slot without a diagnosis. The population is a set, not a sample: a row whose pane lookup failed still renders with `no pane — <reason>`. The digest's `CLASSIFICATION` row gains `<age>` to carry the wait. The reap message now names `/exit` as the step that ends the process and frees the slot, and no longer implies that closing the task does it. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a closer followed by more than 200 KB of records is still read.** The deployed v0.184.5 `/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` sweep, checked against pane reads recorded before it ran (2026-10-19 22:58, 10 of 13 matched; the two others were sessions whose last turn ended on prose with no closer), put pane 955 — a `later (on the next organic broker loss)` closer followed by ~260 KB of `attachment` records — on `unclassified — transcript unreadable`: `_read_last_assistant_text()` read a fixed 200 KB tail and found no assistant text in it. It now widens the tail (200 KB → 2 MB → 20 MB) only when a window holds no assistant text, so the common case reads what it read before and a large transcript is never read whole. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.6

- fix: **a failed liveness read now names its cause and elapsed milliseconds, and a refused spawn prints it.** Every reader on the worker-count path — `server/session-heartbeat.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.mjs`, `scripts/session-liveness.py`, and the ledger reader in `scripts/spawn-ledger.py` — answered a bare `null`/`None`, so a timeout, a refused socket and an unparseable body all reached the operator as "the live-worker count could not be taken". Measured 2026-10-19: `worker-sessions.py --count` returned UNKNOWN at 21:49 and succeeded 20/20 at 22:07, and the failure could not be attributed. Each failed read now logs `session-heartbeat read failed after 5019 ms: timeout` (or `ECONNREFUSED`, or `unparseable body: …`) to stderr and reports the same line to an optional `onFailure` observer; `workerSessions()` returns `{ workers, error }`, and `concurrentLimitRefusal` prints `The count failed: <cause>` beside the limit, so the refusal names what failed rather than the limit that did not. `null` is still `null`: a limit that cannot count still does not open. Change set: `server/read-diagnostics.mjs`, `server/session-heartbeat.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/spawn-ledger.py`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `server/read-diagnostics.test.mjs`, `server/session-heartbeat.test.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.test.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `scripts/tests/endpoint_fixture.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the tracked-set gate doorbell re-emits when a gate's question changes.** `manager-attention-watch.py` compared membership only, so a session that stayed in the gated set while its closer changed produced no event — measured twice on 2026-10-06 and a third time on 2026-10-13, where a worker parked five times in twelve minutes and every park after the first was invisible. The transition key is now the gate's content: `content_key()` hashes the exact `reason :: detail` string `NEW GATE` carries (per body hash — `per session` is the defect, and `per session+reason` misses a body change under a stable reason, which is the measured `7f07a37a` case), and `gate_transition()` is the single predicate both the streaming arm and the `--once` diagnostic use. The state file moves from a bare list of session ids to a `{sid8: content_key}` dict; a legacy list reads as membership-known/content-unknown, so an upgrade records the key in place instead of re-emitting every member once. `--once` marks each line `(new)` or `(seen)`. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.5

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` labels an idle session that declares itself `🟢 ACTIVE` as `working`.** The deployed v0.184.4 sweep, checked against pane reads recorded before it ran (2026-10-19 22:38, 15 of 18 matched, 2 stale), still put pane 796 — `idle`, `🟢 ACTIVE · 1 running (… resumed without a tab)`, a `nothing` closer and `⏰ Next: … (ScheduleWakeup)` — on `unclassified — idle with no ask`: `machine_wait()` rightly refuses a running declaration as a wait, and the unparked branch honoured a declared `⚪ DONE` but not its running twin. An unparked row whose panel declares `🟢 ACTIVE` / `State: working` is now `working`; a dead shell stays `bare shell`, since process death outranks a stale claim. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.4

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` reads the state a session's own panel declares.** The deployed v0.184.1 sweep, checked against pane reads recorded before it ran (2026-10-19 22:05), still mislabelled three sessions: pane 816 — `🔵 READY · you decide` with a `nothing` closer and `⏰ Next: a fresh session picks up …` — read `external`, and panes 887 and 890 — `⚪ DONE` panels with no `📌 Task:` link — read `working` (887 holds a stray shell) and `idle with no ask`. `declared_state()` now reads the panel's last state line in either vocabulary (`🟢 ACTIVE` / `🟡 WAITING` / `🔵 READY` / `🔴 BLOCKED` / `⚪ DONE`, or worker-drive's `State: <state>`); `machine_wait()` admits a `nothing` closer only when that declaration is absent or a wait, and an unparked row whose panel declares itself closed is `closed` unless a resolved task file says it is still open — the disk wins over the panel. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
