## v0.184.3

- fix: **`parked-lever.py` tells a headless worker apart from a tab worker whose pane is unresolved.** The first live lever-row sample passed `--pane -` for a tab worker and the row read `no pane (headless)` — telling the operator to stop looking for a window that exists. `--pane ?` now renders `pane unresolved (tab worker — resolve before reporting)`, and an empty `--pane` is refused rather than silently reading as headless. Change set: `scripts/parked-lever.py`, `scripts/tests/test_parked_lever.py`, `commands/steps/manager-parked.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.2

- docs: **a `waiting-external` worker is a parked lever row too.** The first live `/supervisor:manager-drive` round on v0.183.0 rendered a `machine: <watcher>` row for a worker whose turn had ended on a PR watcher, which exposed `commands/steps/manager-parked.md` as too narrow: it listed gate classes only, while the owner taxonomy already carried `machine: <watcher>` for exactly that worker class. The definition now includes `waiting-external`. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-parked.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` no longer reads a running or finished session's `⏰` line as a machine wait.** The deployed v0.183.1 sweep, checked pane by pane on 2026-10-19, labelled two sessions `external` wrongly: pane 888 declared itself in worker-drive's own header (`State: working 🟢`), which the `🟢 ACTIVE` test did not know, and pane 889 was a `⚪ DONE` panel whose `⏰ Next:` named the operator (`you open a new session`). `machine_wait()` now refuses a panel whose state line says it is running or finished in either vocabulary (`🟢 ACTIVE`, `⚪ DONE`, `State: working`, `State: closed`), and a `⏰` line led by `you` names no machine. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.184.0

- feat: **the manager and fleet rounds act on criteria, not only on sessions.** `commands/steps/manager-gates.md` now folds every decision a round holds — its own findings, its `waiting-approval` rows and its relayed pane questions — into ONE batch claimed through `asked-ledger` and rendered under `commands/steps/needs-input.md`, so a round never opens a second modal. `commands/steps/escalate.md` files one task per cause class recurring on two or more distinct days (naming the covering task when one exists) through `Task(vault-cli:task-creator)` — the round holds `Task`, not `Skill` — leaving it at `phase: todo` for approval, instead of re-reporting the same blocker daily. `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md` persists each round's reconciliation line to `~/.claude/state/manager-coverage/<session>.json`, and `commands/steps/output-shape.md` ranks managers by closeness to their SCs from those files (`unranked`, never last, when a manager has no file). Gate letters stay the script's: A–E keep their runbook meaning and nothing in `commands/` carries a second one. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-gates.md`, `commands/steps/manager-coverage.md`, `commands/steps/escalate.md`, `commands/steps/output-shape.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.183.1

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` gives watched waits, held shells and busy-but-ended turns their true state.** A hand read of 23 live panes on 2026-10-19 found 11 mislabelled. A `👤 You: nothing` closer was read as "nothing to report" before its `⏰ Ends:` / `⏰ Next:` line was looked at, so every `🟡 WAITING` panel (a cron, a manager wakeup, a PR watcher, clocks) landed on `unclassified — idle with no ask`; it is now a park whose `⏰` line names a machine, labelled `external` with its watcher and the panel's `ETA:` slot — refused for `⏰ Next: no mechanism is running` (⚪ DONE), a `⏰` line asking the operator, and a closer whose state line says `🟢 ACTIVE`. A live `status: shell` session (a held background child) is now `working` rather than `idle with no ask`; a dead one is still `bare shell`, and an unprovable one stays where it was. A `busy` session whose foreground turn ended while a background agent runs is read from its closer — a `pick —` is a `decision`, a machine `⏰` line `external` — but only on positive evidence the turn ended: `who-needs-me.py` gains `closer_turn_state()`, the three-state form of `turn_after_closer()` (unchanged for its callers), and an unreadable tail keeps the row `working`. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.183.0

- feat: **worker-drive names its session state and mints exactly one card per ask.** The §8 report now opens with a mandatory `State:` line (`working` 🟢 / `external` 🟡 / `decision` 🔵 / `closed` ⚪, else `unclassified — <reason>`) whose closer icon must match. An external wait arms its own watcher and never asks the operator to "reply watch". A decision run produces exactly one card — the closing modal's, or the waiting verb's, never a second `📣 Board:` line or `attention-ask.py post` — and `answered-watch.py` is armed only after the card is confirmed. §8's card paragraph now points at the mint rule's home instead of restating the mechanism. The rule is carried in `docs/worker-session-states.md`; `scripts/tests/test_worker_drive_state.py` lints the shape with a negative control. `commands/worker-drive.md` stays within its size ratchet (45114 bytes). Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `docs/worker-session-states.md`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_drive_state.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`/supervisor:manager-drive` names one unblock lever per parked worker.** A worker parked on a gate used to get a reap/nudge/resume line and no statement of what would clear it, so the operator read each pane to find out. Each round now prints one row per parked tracked worker — jump link · waits · the one lever · owner (`manager` / `operator` / `machine: <watcher>`) — rendered through `scripts/parked-lever.py`, which refuses `the fleet` / `CI` / `wait` as levers because they read exactly like an answer and leave the pane unopened anyway. Manager-owned levers are executed in the same round and reported as done, never offered back; operator-owned levers feed the round's existing single card, so no second modal is added. The rule has one home, `commands/steps/manager-parked.md`, referenced from the router and restated nowhere. Change set: `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/steps/manager-parked.md`, `scripts/parked-lever.py`, `scripts/tests/test_parked_lever.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.182.1

- fix: **the closer lint can see a closer that is not in the transcript's last 256 KB.** `reap-closer.py`'s `last_closer` read one fixed tail window, so on a long session the new `Stop` hook recorded `no-closer` 44 ms after the closer was written: the harness writes large `api-request-blob` entries after the assistant message, and the closer sat ~758 KB from EOF (measured 2026-10-19; a single 400 KB entry after the closer reproduces it). It now scans backwards in `WINDOW` chunks and stops at the first assistant message carrying a closer, bounded at 4 MB so a pathological transcript cannot turn a hook into a full-file read. Change set: `scripts/reap-closer.py`, `scripts/tests/test_reap_closer.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.182.0

- feat: **a worker can no longer end its turn on an operator gate for a step the rules already authorise.** A new `Stop` hook, `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, reads the session's last `👤 You:` closer (reusing `reap-closer.py`'s `last_closer`) and exits 2 when an `approve:`/`review:` line names an After Opening a PR verb — `/github-pr-watch`, `/coding:local-review` (including the observed prose form "I run the local review"), `/coding:pr-review`, `gh pr ready`, `dark-factory prompt|spec approve` — so the turn continues and the model does the work instead of asking. `gh pr merge` and every production-touching action stay gated. Fail-open on every path, a `stop_hook_active` loop guard, kill switch `SUPERVISOR_CLOSER_GATE_LINT=off`, and one JSONL record per invocation written on allow and block alike. Change set: `hooks/hooks.json`, `scripts/closer-gate-lint.py`, `scripts/tests/test_closer_gate_lint.py`, `scripts/tests/fixtures/closer-gate-lint/`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.181.0

- feat: **the heartbeat sender can now emit a `location: pod` row, so a pod worker declares itself live to the attention store.** Both pod-side liveness paths stamp `location: 'pod'`: the A2A controller poll (`pollA2A`, whose stamp callback is now the exported `a2aStamp`, so the test drives the shipped callback rather than supplying its own) and the cluster ConfigMap mirror (`pollCluster`). `location` is a property of the path, not of the record — an A2A worker is created by the controller on `docker`/`k8s`, and every cluster-store entry was published by a pod. Local sessions are unchanged: `startSelfStamp` still writes `location: 'local'`.

## v0.180.1

- fix: **the changelog-fold guard is green again: a v0.176.0 bullet that shipped in v0.177.0 now sits in its own release section.** The guard failed on every push since 2026-10-18T18:38:46Z because an earlier fold repair left a v0.177.0 fix under `## v0.176.0`; the tag containing that merge proves the destination, so the bullet is relocated there as a pure move — no text change. A guard that is always red cannot signal a new fold; this clears the standing backlog, and a red run on master now blocks the next merge (`CLAUDE.md` § Changelog fold guard). Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`, `CLAUDE.md`.

## v0.180.0

- feat: Manager sessions now open on the vault's Claude launcher by default. This covers topic managers from `/supervisor:open`, `spawn_agent` with `role: manager`, and manager restarts. The launcher is chosen in this order:
  1. the subject's `manager_launcher:` frontmatter;
  2. the `-claude` sibling of the vault's `claude_script` (`cc-private-deepseek` becomes `cc-private-claude`), if that script exists;
  3. the vault's `claude_script`.

  Workers are unchanged. The rule is documented in `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Which launcher a manager opens on. The new `scripts/manager-launcher.py` resolves the launcher for `/supervisor:open`. `scripts/budget-baseline.json`'s `commands/open.md` entry was raised by hand from 61974 to 62115 for the call site.

## v0.179.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:open-a2a`'s local-backend create now matches `/supervisor:open`'s spawn.** `scripts/a2a-local-control.py create` gains `--vault` and `--first-step`, sent as the controller's new `vault` / `first_step` create fields, and prints the reply's `session_id` and a `bind:` line (`stamped` / `appended` / `error <msg>` / `none`); token redaction unchanged. `commands/open-a2a.md` passes the resolved vault and first step, reads `bind` the way `/supervisor:open` § 4.1 reads `spawn_agent`'s, falls back to `list` on `none`, and reports the bind. Requires bborbe/a2a-worker-controller#24. Change set: `scripts/a2a-local-control.py`, `scripts/tests/test_a2a_local_control.py`, `commands/open-a2a.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.178.4

- fix: **`/supervisor:open`'s CREATE branch dispatches `spawn_agent` again instead of routing through the a2a-worker-controller.** The controller's `local` backend has a single vault directory, so a cross-vault create got the private-personal launcher and cwd, never reported or bound its session id (vault-ui showed Start, not Live), and bypassed the start-rate limit. Reverts the route-through from #367; `/supervisor:open-a2a` stays a separate, explicitly invoked command until it handles every vault.

## v0.178.3

- fix: **`sweep_shape()` accepted only one of the two payload shapes its producer writes, so the round receipt flapped between green and red on rounds identical in substance.** The tick's output leads with a liveness marker and hangs the rendered frame two spaces under it — `docs/sweep-output.md` § Print the full table on every tick calls the marker *"always the first line of the tick's output"* and *"part of the frame, not decoration"* — so a payload captured from a tick starts with the marker, while one captured from the frame alone does not. `manager-predispatch.py --write-payload` stores whatever it is handed, so both shapes are real: measured 2026-10-18/19 on consecutive rounds twenty minutes apart, same code and same install, one payload of each shape, and the frame-at-line-0 predicate returned False on the marker shape and True on the other. The predicate now accepts a leading marker line when a real frame starts on the next line, and nothing else: the marker must carry the `✓` the spec names as its distinguishing glyph, and the frame's own contract is unchanged — `┌…┐` top rule, `│`-framed rows, `└…┘` bottom rule — so a marker with no frame, a marker-prefixed frame truncated before its bottom rule, arbitrary prose ahead of a frame, the old JSON payload and an empty file all still fail. Stripping the marker in the producer was rejected as the alternative: it would make the stored payload disagree with what the tick actually prints, which is the opposite of what the receipt exists to guarantee. The regression test now runs the marker-prefixed shape through the same producer chain as the frame-only one (`box-table.py` → `--write-payload` → the stored `.table`), and both new cases fail against the pre-fix predicate. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.178.2

- fix: **the round receipt reported `skipped: sweep` and `skipped: watcher` on every round, because both shape predicates could only ever return False against the artifacts their own producers write.** `sweep_shape()` required `"header" in json.loads(text)`, but `manager-predispatch.py --write-payload` stores the *rendered* box-table `box-table.py` printed — the text `--save` reads back and the operator sees, never JSON. `watcher_shape()` accepted only `isinstance(ts, (int, float))`, while `manager-attention-watch.py:1445` writes `time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S%z")` — an ISO-8601 string, matching **0** of the 526 records in the measured log. So the receipt certified at most one of its three steps, and a genuine skip of either was indistinguishable from the predicate's own type mismatch (measured 2026-10-18, goal manager session `beac5fd0`, round 19:49–20:05). Both predicates now match their producer rather than an imagined one: `sweep_shape()` requires the frame's own contract — a `┌…┐` top rule, `│`-framed rows and a `└…┘` bottom rule, so a truncated frame still fails — and `watcher_shape()` parses the record's `ts` as ISO-8601 (a bare number stays accepted), with the round's lower bound floored to the producer's one-second resolution so a record appended in the same second the round opened is not read as *before* it. The **producer side is deliberately unchanged**: the text payload is consumed by `--save` and the operator, and the ISO timestamp is the format the whole `events.jsonl` log and its readers use — the predicate was the only side that disagreed with both. Both rejection halves are pinned, because an always-True predicate would blind the receipt in the opposite direction: `sweep_shape()` returns False for the old JSON payload, an empty file and a frame truncated before its bottom rule, and `watcher_shape()` returns False when every record's `ts` lies outside the round window. The regression test now runs each predicate against an artifact its own producer wrote — `box-table.py` → `--write-payload` → the stored `.table`, and `manager-attention-watch.py`'s own `log_event` — instead of the JSON and numeric fixtures that re-encoded the defect; those tests fail 8-of-21 against the pre-fix predicates and pass against the fix. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.178.1

- fix: **`read_push_config` never worked — it read the wrong key, and its error named the opposite of the truth.** The tool shipped in `v0.178.0` issues `GetTaskPushNotificationConfig` with `params: {taskId}`, but the SDK's handler is `h.pushConfigStore.Get(ctx, req.TaskID, req.ID)` (`a2asrv/handler.go:419`) — the store is keyed by task id **and config id** — and the registration never sends a config `id`, so every call looked up an empty one and returned `failed to get push config: push config not found`. ⚠️ **That message is worse than an absence**: it reads as "the worker did not store the config", which is the opposite of the truth, and sends a debugger at the registration instead of the reader. Measured against a live worker whose pushes were arriving for the same task id, and re-measured mid-turn to rule out cleanup on terminal state. It now uses `ListTaskPushNotificationConfigs`, keyed by `taskId` alone (`a2a/push.go:45`), returning `{configs: […]}` — where an empty list is a RESULT meaning "the worker holds none", distinct from a refused call. ⚠️ The unit test passed because the stub accepted whatever params it was sent: a stub that echoes your own request shape cannot validate it. Change set: `server/a2a-client.mjs`, `server/a2a-client.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.178.0

- feat: **`read_push_config` reads a worker's registered A2A push config back from the worker itself.** `push_registration.state === "attempted-ok"` proves only that the worker ACCEPTED a registration, not that it HOLDS one — an accepted-then-dropped config is indistinguishable from a working one until something reads it back. The tool issues the A2A `GetTaskPushNotificationConfig` JSON-RPC call against the worker, addressed by task id only (never re-sending `url`/`token`, which would make a get into a second registration), gated on an `attempted-ok` registration already existing and refusing rather than reading an unregistered task. It returns the CONFIG — the url and notification token the worker stores — and never the worker's bearer token, which authenticates the call and is never projected; that keeps "the token is memory-only" true for everything except this one deliberate, named, read-only call. Change set: `server/a2a-client.mjs`, `server/a2a-client.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.8

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` no longer bounds how long an operator reply's lead-in may be, because the live fleet exceeded the bound on the first run after it shipped.** The previous release classified a watcher line as an operator reply only when the verb came within 24 characters of `your`; pane 483 read `external` with `⏰ Ends: your \`nukeprod-threshold-applied\` reply` — a 29-character lead-in. The pattern is now unbounded and non-greedy, so it stops at the first verb wherever it falls. A bound here is a bet on how long a phrase written by another session will be, and the fleet settled that bet against the code within minutes. A fixture pins the live shape. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.7

- fix: **the A2A push registration never ran at all: `a2a-spawn.mjs` called `a2aPushConfigCallFor` without importing it, so every registration threw `ReferenceError` and the collector swallowed it.** `registerPushCallbackOnWorker` is the only place a spawner registers its push config against a worker's task, and its first statement referenced a symbol that is exported by `a2a-client.mjs` but absent from `a2a-spawn.mjs`'s import list. The resulting `ReferenceError` was caught by `collectTurnIncrementally`'s `try/catch` around `await onTaskId(taskId)` — a catch that exists so a failing registration cannot fail a healthy turn — so every turn completed normally, `push_registration` kept its creation seed *"the turn has not yet named a task"*, and the channel was never armed. No test called the function, which is why it shipped. Found by instrumenting the served copy and reading three file markers: `S spawn … push=yes`, `D onDiagnostic {"path":"incremental"}`, `T onTaskId taskId=… push=yes` — and then nothing, with the seed still in the record 90 s after the turn completed. Four earlier probes were spent reading that seed as a missing registration. The fix imports the missing function, **bounds the call with `AbortSignal.timeout` (10 s)** so a worker that never answers records `attempted-error` naming the timeout instead of stranding the write, and **records `{state:'attempting', taskId}` before the await** so the field can never read as "not started" while the call is in flight — applied at the spawn site and at the follow-up site alike. It also **stops the swallow from being silent**: `collectTurnIncrementally` still refuses to fail a healthy turn, but now reports the thrown message through `onDiagnostic`, which `pushDiagnosticPatch` turns into `attempted-error` — the report that would have made this a one-probe diagnosis instead of five, and the reason a `ReferenceError` is no longer indistinguishable from a quiet channel. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.6

- fix: **`progress-window.py` reads a window with a Moved box as `PROGRESSING` even while a tool call is in flight.** `TOOL-RUNNING` ranked above `PROGRESSING`, and a productive session is nearly always mid-call, so the short path was close to unreachable: measured 2026-10-18 on the SC4 exercise, both progressing windows read `TOOL-RUNNING` — one of them with a grade-A `SC4` tick and a `backed` claim. `TOOL-RUNNING` now ranks below `DONE-CANDIDATE` and `PROGRESSING`, so it shields only the no-move verdicts it exists for (never read an in-flight call as stuck). On that window the fixed script reads `PROGRESSING`, `short_path: true`. One fixture pins a measured tick plus a young trailing call; it fails on the old order. Change set: `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `docs/session-progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.5

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` no longer labels a live session a `bare shell`.** The shell limb of `state_of()` keyed on the registry's `status: shell` alone — stale metadata, since the harness stamps `shell` when a shell child is spawned and never resets it — while the same registry record already carried the `alive` identity verdict. Measured 2026-10-18: nine panes with `status: shell, alive: True` were handed to the operator as `bare shell`, the label a close-the-exited-tabs action reads as safe. `alive` is now threaded from `build()` into `state_of()` at both call sites, and the limb fires only on `alive is False`; `alive: None` (pid occupied, identity unprovable) falls through like any other live-but-quiet session rather than being read as death. Three fixtures pin it: a differential (the same pane under `status: shell` and `status: idle` must classify identically, so renaming the reason cannot satisfy the suite), the dead-shell arm (deleting the branch would otherwise pass), and the unknown arm. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.4

- fix: **an A2A follow-up turn now registers its own push config, and a spawn with no push config says so instead of naming the wrong cause.** A follow-up is sent with a `contextId` and **no `taskId`**, so the worker creates a NEW task and the config held for the first turn's task does not apply to it — the spawn-side comment claiming "a follow-up continues an existing task, so its config is already held" was false, and every turn after the first therefore had no callback at all. `send_agent_message` now passes `onTaskId` / `onDiagnostic` to `collectWorkerTurn`, reusing the token the registry already bound (`a2aPushRegistry.pushFor`, which never hands back an unbound token), and resets `pushRegistration` to the seed first so the field describes the latest turn exactly as `result` does. Separately, the spawn path's `onTaskId` used to `return` silently when `push` was undefined, leaving the seed reason *"the turn has not yet named a task"* — the opposite of what happened, because that callback only runs once a task id HAS been found. Measured 2026-10-18 on a fresh docker spawn: the record showed exactly that seed. It now records `not-attempted` carrying the bind error `beginCreate` returned. Change set: `server/a2a-push-registry.mjs`, `server/a2a-push-registry.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.3

- fix: **`progress-window.py` rates a coverage row labelled by box id.** The vault-cli coverage mode labels a row either by its checkbox (`[x] …`) or by its box id (`[DoD1] …`); `load_coverage` stripped only the checkbox form, so a bracketed id matched neither the id token nor the box-text prefix and every rating read as unavailable. Measured on the SC4 exercise (2026-10-18, session `6fbbd4f6`): `coverage.rows: 0` from an 11-row coverage output, now `11`. One fixture pins the bracketed form. Change set: `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.2

- fix: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` no longer promotes a session to `external` on a watcher line that asks the operator.** `watcher_of()` reads the last assistant text, so a session that wrote its ask into the `⏰ Ends:` slot instead of the `👤 You:` closer carried a non-empty watcher and was labelled `external` while it was waiting on the operator — the forced-bucket failure the fifth state exists to prevent. Measured on the first live run (2026-10-18, pane 449): `⏰ Ends: \` your reply — I run the three commands…` classified `external`. A watcher is now evidence only when it names a machine — the closer is a `later (on …)` park or opens with the panel's `⏰ Ends:` / `⏰ Next:` marker, or the watcher text is not an operator reply — and the row otherwise prints `unclassified · watcher asks the operator, not a machine`. A pane whose closer genuinely names a background agent (`⏰ Ends: orientation assistant returns`) stays `external`; two fixtures pin both directions. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.1

- fix: **the A2A collection diagnostic no longer mislabels an incremental run as whole-body.** The previous release branched on `d.taskIdFound === false`, but the incremental branch announces itself with `{path: 'incremental'}` and **no `taskIdFound` key** — so the announcement fell to the whole-body arm and reported *"the turn was collected whole-body"* for every incremental run that found no task id, which is precisely the failure the field exists to name. The decision now lives in a pure `pushDiagnosticPatch` that branches on `path`: the announcement returns `null` and writes nothing, `{path: 'whole-body'}` names the fallback, and `{path: 'incremental', taskIdFound: false, frames: N}` names the frame count. ⚠️ **Measured consequence, and why it mattered**: the first instrumented probe reported "whole-body" and was read as the fallback branch. A local probe then showed `fetch` returns `getReader = function` for JSON, SSE **and** a 500-with-body under node — only a 204 no-body yields `undefined` — so the fallback is unreachable for any turn that produced a reply, and the real state was incremental-with-no-id. The bad label inverted the diagnosis. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.177.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:who-needs-my-help` labels every live session `decision`, `external`, `working` or `closed`, or prints `unclassified` with the reason.** The label is a projection over what the sweep already computes — `parked()`, the registry status, `cause_class()`, `gate_class()`, `watcher_of()` and `eta_of()` — so no pane or transcript is read twice; `state_of()` is a pure function, and each bucket is pinned by two fixtures whose input signals differ, because one fixture cannot falsify a constant. The fifth label is the operator's own "or analyse why not": a permission prompt raised by a settings ask rule (`Bash(make build:*)`) carries no closer verb, so `is_operator_ask()` is False and `gate_class()` is None — it prints `unclassified · permission prompt (ask rule)` rather than being forced into `decision`, and a bare shell prints its own reason. ⚠️ **The population was the defect the labels exposed.** `sweep_live()` built its fleet from the attention store, which holds a row only for a session with something to report, so every session that was simply working or finished was invisible — the two states most of a fleet is in. One synthetic record per registry session the store does not cover restores it, and `is_live()`'s pane filter still drops a row nothing can be jumped to. The Sweep section now prints a state tally and one row per session: not-parked rows previously rendered as a comma-joined name list carrying neither a label nor a jump link. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-my-help.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_my_help.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the A2A turn collector reports which read path it took, so a spawner can tell "the task-id callback was never wired" from "the stream named no task".** The previous release recorded `pushRegistration` in three states; its first instrumented run returned `not-attempted`, which correctly established that `onTaskId` never ran but could not say which of two gates stopped it — a whole-body collection structurally never calls the callback (`collectWorkerTurn` falls through to `.text()` when `settled.body.getReader` is absent), while an incremental one calls it unless no frame carries an id. The seed reason string collapses both into one sentence. `collectWorkerTurn` now takes an `onDiagnostic` callback: it fires `{path: 'whole-body'}` on the fallback, and `{path: 'incremental', taskIdFound: false, frames: <n>}` when the stream ends without the callback having run — the latter reported only after the stream ends, where "no frame named a task" is a fact rather than a not-yet, and keyed on the same `announced` flag the callback sets so the two cannot disagree. ⚠️ The spawner records it **only while the state is still `not-attempted`**, so a diagnostic arriving after `onTaskId` can never overwrite an `attempted-ok` or `attempted-error`. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.176.1

- fix: **`scripts/progress-window.py` now rates a box whose coverage row carries a pipe or a leading backtick, and matches a claim to its own box among several uncommitted ticks.** Found by the first live `/supervisor:worker-drive` run on v0.175.0: the coverage agent rated a DoD box `measured`, yet the tick read `rating unavailable` — the box's own text carried `| head -1`, shifting the pipe-split columns, and the cell strip cut a leading backtick so the text never matched; and because every uncommitted tick shares the ref `worktree`, a claim on DoD2 matched DoD1's tick and came back `rejected:asserted`. Columns are now read from the right (`proposal`, then `evidence`), the box text keeps its backticks and matches by prefix, and the claimed box ranks the match. Both errors fell on the safe side — an A read as B, a backable claim rejected — so the one live lever never fired wrongly. A fixture each, both failing on v0.175.0. Change set: `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.176.0

- feat: **`attention-ask.py post` refuses a card whose every option names a manager-owned action.** Class 2 of the board-noise work: when nothing on the card asks the operator for something a manager cannot do itself, the card spends an operator click ratifying a decision already made. The check **loads** `manager-menu-guard.py`'s canonical `names_manager_owned_action` rather than mirroring the match, and it **fails OPEN** — a missing or raising guard posts the card, because refusing a real question strands the asking session, which is strictly worse than one low-value card. A card carrying even one operator-owned option is a real question and is never refused; `--force` keeps a deliberate card postable, and the refusal names that escape. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.175.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:worker-drive` now measures the window before it diagnoses, and no longer takes the session's own account of its progress.** §0b dispatches the coverage mode once, lets the session file at most one structured claim (`{box, kind, ref}` — never prose), runs `scripts/progress-window.py --json`, and passes that JSON to `worker-drive-reader` as `window:` in place of the self-reported `session_digest`; the reader's diagnosis copies the measured verdict, its loop check reads the script's `LOOP` / `READ-LOOP` / `OSCILLATE` subtype, and its drift check reads the caller's `drift_note` until `DRIFT` ships. Every report now opens with the script's ⏱ window block, verbatim. One live lever: `short_path: true` — `PROGRESSING` with a claim backed by a `measured` tick — skips the reader and the inventory and keeps driving the next open box; every other verdict only adds the block. The JSON gains a `block` field so one run serves both. `commands/worker-drive.md`'s budget entry is raised by hand 43583 → 45137: the step's rationale lives in `docs/session-progress.md`, and the remaining +1554 bytes are the step itself. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `agents/worker-drive-reader.md`, `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **add `scripts/progress-window.py`, which measures a session's last window from disk and names its verdict.** It reads the transcript by session id (one file per inode, `tool_use` from `assistant` records only), the anchor task file's history in its vault repo (plus the working tree when `--until` is now), the commits and uncommitted diffs in every git root the session wrote to (only those touching a file it wrote, because `~/.claude` and main checkouts are shared), the registry and the coverage agent's rows. Each observation is graded A/B/C; the verdict is one of `UNKNOWN` (exit 2), `TOOL-RUNNING`, `DONE-CANDIDATE`, `PROGRESSING`, `ORIENTING`, `BUSY-NO-MOVE` (`LOOP` / `READ-LOOP` / `OSCILLATE`) or `IDLE`; a structured `--claim` comes back `backed` or `rejected:<class>`, and `short_path` is true only for `PROGRESSING` with a claim backed by grade A. `--json` echoes the window, the coverage path + hash and the claim so a run replays. `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py` pins one fixture per class (44 cases); ten single-line mutations of the script each fail it. Change set: `scripts/progress-window.py`, `scripts/tests/test_progress_window.py`, `docs/session-progress.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- docs: **add `docs/session-progress.md`, the single home for what a drive must answer first — did this session move its anchor's closure in the last window, on what evidence, and if not, what blocks it.** A session's own account becomes a claim checked against disk, never an input: progress is defined as closure movement up the Task → Goal → Topic chain, every observation is graded A (closure moved), B (state moved toward an open box) or C (activity), and nine ordered verdicts — from `UNKNOWN` and `TOOL-RUNNING` through `PROGRESSING` to `IDLE` — say what the drive does with each. Also covers the window block every drive report will open with, the structured claim a session may file, the blocker table with an owner and a lever per row (reusing gate classes A–E), and what a drive never does. Measured while writing it: one session id can match two transcript paths through a project-dir symlink (same inode), and a record's `cwd` is the launch dir, never the edited worktree — both rules are stated so the script built on this page does not count events twice or probe the wrong tree. Change set: `docs/session-progress.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.174.4

- fix: **a refused or never-attempted A2A push registration is recorded on the worker, so an empty `status_messages` is diagnosable instead of ambiguous.** Measured 2026-10-18 on the first real docker e2e: the worker ran its turn to `TASK_STATE_COMPLETED` in 43.9 s, answered `DOCKER-E2E-OK`, and `status_messages` stayed `[]` / `last_status` stayed `null` across the whole mid-turn window — with nothing anywhere saying whether a registration had even been sent. ⚠️ **The cause was three-way indistinguishable by construction.** `registerPushCallbackOnWorker` returns `{registered}` or `{error}` precisely so the caller can record it, and the `onTaskId` call site dropped the value on the floor; `onTaskId` also returns immediately when `push` is undefined (a receiver that never bound), and never fires at all when the turn's frames carry no task id. All three leave the same evidence — an empty channel and no explanation. The worker record now carries `pushRegistration`: `{state: 'not-attempted', reason}` (the receiver's own bind error, or "the turn has not yet named a task" when the callback never ran), `{state: 'attempted-ok', taskId, at}`, or `{state: 'attempted-error', taskId, error, at}` — and `agent_status` projects it as `push_registration`. ⚠️ **Three states, not two**: a boolean would collapse "never sent" into "sent and refused", which is exactly the ambiguity this field removes. The refusal is filed as a property of the CHANNEL, never as a turn failure, so a healthy turn still files a healthy outcome and a roster read never reports a working worker as broken. Change set: `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.174.3

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --list` degrades per row instead of refusing the whole fleet, so a fleet carrying unstamped sessions is still listable.** Both `--list` paths returned `UNKNOWN` (exit 2) whenever `unstamped()` found a ledger worker with no heartbeat row — which made the whole list unavailable *and* blocked `plugin-version-census.py`, the one tool that names those sessions, behind the same exit 2. Measured live 2026-10-18: `unstamped=12 ledger_workers=23 registry_live=43`, the twelve being interactive sessions running a supervisor older than v0.171.1 — a population that recurs at every plugin release, so a guard that fails the whole list on them is an outage rather than a precondition. The finding now rides ON the row (`degraded: true`, `state: "unstamped"`) and every registry-live session is carried, so `commands/open.md` Step 2C cannot mistake a dropped session for "no such session" — the 2026-09-18 duplicate-spawn shape the guard was added for. ⚠️ **UNKNOWN still refuses**: "could not measure" is neither "measured complete" nor "degraded", and a store that could not be reached must never read as a listed fleet. ⚠️ **A `rows=<n> registry_live=<n> degraded=<n>` summary now prints to stderr**, which is what makes the acceptance criterion mechanical — `rows` must equal `registry_live`, since a smaller `rows` means a session was dropped rather than marked. ⚠️ **The two callers named as blocked need no code change, and their docstrings said otherwise** — `approved-not-started.py` and `plugin-version-census.py` both guard on `returncode != 0`, which is *false* on the degraded path, so the rows now arrive where exit 2 previously returned `None` for the whole fleet; measured after the change, `plugin-version-census.py` prints `live: 39 · stale: 29` where it previously printed `exited 2: UNKNOWN`. Their docstrings claimed exit 0 meant "both halves were read" and now name the degraded state. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/approved-not-started.py`, `scripts/plugin-version-census.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.174.2

- fix: **the receive path now marks its own entries, so a reader can tell a pushed status from one the spawner synthesised for itself.** Every stored entry gains `source: 'push'`, written only here — which is the whole point of it: `a2aTurnPatch`, which files the turn the spawner collected on its own, never sets that field, so an entry carrying it can only have arrived through the receive endpoint. The entry also carries `messageId`, the worker's own message id read from `status.message.messageId` — the same message the entry's text is read from, so the two cannot disagree about which message they describe. It is `null` rather than absent when the event carries no message at all, which the terminal completion currently does (`NewStatusUpdateEvent(ctx, a2a.TaskStateCompleted, nil)`), so `null` reads as "this event had no message" rather than "the receive path dropped it" — the same distinction `last_status` draws between `null` and an empty object. Change set: `server/a2a-receive.mjs`, `server/a2a-receive.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.174.1

- docs: **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § the cause-of-death classifier now names `/supervisor:restart` itself, not only its command file.** The v0.173.0 rename updated the *path* on that line (`commands/worker-restart.md` → `commands/restart.md`) but left the prose naming a file, so the one page that owns the restart contract described the mechanism without ever naming the verb — and a reader grepping the docs for `/supervisor:restart` got no hit there, while `docs/session-tiers.md` had four. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.174.0

- feat: **a manager round writes a per-round receipt and the tail re-arm checks it, so a round that skipped its steps is visible on the tick it happened instead of rendering like a full one.** `commands/manager-drive.md` is a router map — it names the steps and the condition under which each is read, and nothing verified that any of them ran. Measured 2026-10-17 (goal manager session `956f6f82`): 18:01–21:50 local made 15 `ScheduleWakeup` calls with **0** `Agent` dispatches and **0** `Monitor` arms — the sweep leg never ran and neither watcher was armed — while a worker held an in-tab question 34+ minutes unrelayed and every round read as complete. `manager-liveness.py --check` structurally cannot catch it: it proves the *wakeup* was armed, never that the round did its work. `scripts/manager-receipt.py` is the missing check — `--start` opens the round's window at the tick's head and **clears** the fields, each load-bearing step `--record`s the artifact it produced (`needs-you` = the file the readings step persisted `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you` to; `sweep` = the payload `--write-payload` wrote; `watcher` = the tracked-set arm's `events.jsonl`), and `--check` at the tail prints `skipped: <step>` per unresolved field and exits 11. ⚠️ **A field resolves only when its artifact proves the step RAN, not that a path exists** — the file must sit inside the round window *and* carry the producer's own shape, so a `touch`ed empty file, an arbitrary non-empty string and a round-written stub all fail. A probe keyed on mtime alone is satisfied by the very shortcut it exists to catch; the first cut of this criterion did exactly that and was caught in audit 2026-10-18, when it passed against a round that never ran the read. ⚠️ **Staleness uses `manager-liveness.py`'s own period basis** — the observed median arm-to-arm gap, the reported interval as fallback — so the two checks agree and this one cannot re-introduce the false-positive that basis was built to remove (measured 2026-09-23: reported 300 s, real arm-to-arm 17 min, five false pushes in 32 min). ⚠️ **The watcher field reads the newest `events.jsonl` RECORD's `ts`, never the file's mtime** — the arm appends on every poll, so a fresh mtime over a stopped arm is a touched file, not a live watcher. ⚠️ **The check flags and never refuses: the re-arm proceeds whatever the verdict**, because a manager that declined to re-arm on a skipped round would turn a visible defect into a dead loop. ⚠️ **The honest bound, stated because the receipt is written by the round it judges**: no probe here can tell a fabricated receipt from a real one, only raise what fabrication costs. What it does guarantee is that a round cannot skip a step *silently* — omitting it becomes a visible act. Rule and commands live in one place, `commands/steps/manager-tail.md` step 8. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py` (13), covering the skip-everything shortcut (red without the check, green with it) and four fabricated-receipt cases. Change set: `scripts/manager-receipt.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_receipt.py`, `commands/steps/manager-tail.md`, `commands/steps/manager-readings.md`, `commands/steps/manager-sweep.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- feat: **`attention-ask.py post` and `post-batch` take `--risk` (`routine`/`decision`/`risk`), the producer's declaration of how much care a card deserves.** ⚠️ Declared on both subcommands, not just `post`: the two `add_parser` blocks drift independently while both reach the same `_post_and_report` and therefore the same `post_question`, so a flag on one alone leaves that shared helper reading an attribute half its callers never set. The value is sent only when declared: an omitted flag posts no `risk` field at all, so the store reads it as "declared nothing" and the board renders `decision` — the same absent-not-empty distinction the poster already draws for `--context` and `--expires-at`. ⚠️ The declaration is a convenience, not the safety rule: the board derives the rendered colour at render time and may escalate **above** it, never below, so a card whose payload names a `delete` renders red whatever the poster passed. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.173.0

- feat: **`/supervisor:restart` restarts a manager as well as a worker, so one verb covers both.** The `manager-target` refusal is gone: the role predicate still runs, but it now selects the resume **colour** (a manager comes back in `MANAGER_COLOUR`) and the **re-arm note** rather than stopping the run, and `role-undetermined` is the only role-based refusal left. ⚠️ **A restarted manager must be told to re-arm its loop** — a killed session's `CronCreate` jobs die with the process, so a manager that comes back without that note sits idle while its tab looks healthy — and `commands/restart.md` Step 4 states the trade that follows: a manager holding a gate gets **nothing** typed, so its loop stays down until the operator answers rather than having the note read as an answer to the gate. ⚠️ **`/supervisor:worker-restart` is kept as a DEPRECATED ALIAS**, which is what keeps the `worker-` prefix exception in `docs/session-tiers.md` alive — the exception belongs to the alias's name, so removing the alias retires that paragraph and its table row in the same change. ⚠️ **Step 0's load-path one-liner is `…/supervisor/[0-9]*/`, not `*/`** — `*/` also matches the empty `builtin/` directory and `sort -V` orders a non-numeric name after every version, so `tail -1` selected it and the script path resolved to nothing, failing before it reached any refusal token. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `commands/restart.md`, `commands/worker-restart.md`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `scripts/restart-precheck.py`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **the restart reports which tree the session's supervisor MCP server LAUNCHES from, and warns when that tree's version differs from the installed plugin's.** A restart refreshes the server only if the launched tree is current: `~/.claude/mcp-<vault>.json` may point `supervisor` at a workspace checkout, and a checkout lags the release until someone pulls it — so a restart onto a stale checkout reloads the same old server code and reads as a success. Measured 2026-10-17: `spawn_agent` still lacked `mounts`/`secrets` after a fresh process, because the launched checkout was behind. ⚠️ **Both versions are read live** — the checkout's `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` against the newest installed copy under the load path, a plain file read rather than `git -C`, since the question is what the tree on disk says it is — and the warning fires only when both are known AND unequal, so a hardcoded warning cannot pass: equal versions print the source line alone, and that is the control case. An unreadable version is reported `unreadable` and never as current, because the failure this report exists to catch is a restart that reloads stale code while reading as a success. Change set: `scripts/restart-worker.py`, `docs/restart-worker.md`, `scripts/tests/test_restart_worker.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.172.0
- docs: the `local` arm of `/supervisor:open-a2a` is no longer documented as launcher-blind. `commands/open-a2a.md` § 2.2 now states that the controller resolves the task's launcher itself — the task's `launcher:`, else its goal's, else the vault `claude_script` (item 8) — and returns `launcher` and `launcher_source`, so both are readable from a local create; it also records that a non-executable launcher is refused before any tab opens, and that the refusal reason reaches the log rather than the reply. `commands/open.md` carried the matching stale claim ("on § 2.2's `local` arm none of this is readable"), which was true when the create was launcher-blind and stopped being true when `bborbe/a2a-worker-controller` v0.13.0 taught that backend to resolve the launcher; that clause now scopes the unreadable half to `vault`, `cwd` and `model`, which genuinely are still absent because the create carries `task` alone.

- feat: **the supervisor now RECEIVES worker status pushes, so a `docker` / `k8s` worker can report its own progress to the session that spawned it.** Two new modules. `server/a2a-receive.mjs` is the receive endpoint — the first network surface this process has ever had — binding loopback only (a container reaches a host listener bound to `127.0.0.1` through `host.docker.internal`, measured rather than assumed). It authenticates with the SDK's own `A2A-Notification-Token`, and resolves the worker from that **token, never from the body**: the body is worker-supplied, so a `worker_id` field in it would let any worker holding any valid token write into another's record. It reads the SDK's `StreamResponse` union envelope — exactly one discriminator key, and deliberately **no** `event` key — and stamps the **receive** instant rather than a body-supplied one, because the SDK sends no timestamp and a worker-asserted `ts` is not evidence. `server/a2a-push-registry.mjs` owns the lifetime: it mints the per-worker token and the callback URL **together** (a token without a URL registers nothing and reads downstream as a channel that is merely quiet), binds the token to the controller's worker id once `create` answers, **buffers** a push that lands while the create is still in flight and re-keys it at the bind rather than dropping it, releases the token when a create fails so a failed spawn leaves no dead credential behind, and bounds the store per worker.
- feat: **the callback is registered MID-TURN, which is the whole difficulty of this change.** The A2A push config can only be registered against a task the worker already holds, and the mid-turn status it exists to catch fires a fixed ~10 s into the turn — while `collectWorkerTurn` buffered the entire SSE body through `.text()` and so learned the task id only at `turn end`, minutes too late. `collectWorkerTurn` now accepts an `onTaskId` callback and consumes the stream **chunk by chunk** (`createSseFrameReader`), firing the registration the instant the first frame names the task; the whole-body path stays the reference for every other caller. A chunk boundary inside a frame is handled by parsing only up to the last newline, and a trailing frame with no newline is flushed, because that is how the terminal completion usually arrives. ⚠️ The registration goes to the **worker**, never the controller: the controller creates the worker but never sees a task — this server's first turn creates it, on the worker's own A2A server.
- feat: **`agent_status` carries `status_messages` and `list_agents` carries `last_status`** (`{worker_id, state, text, ts}` and the same plus `age_s`). Both fields are always present and empty for every worker that is not an A2A one, so a caller can tell "the channel exists and is quiet" from "this field was never wired" — and both carry the `ts` the **worker** sent, never one synthesised from the reply this server already collected.

## v0.171.1

- fix: **the three sites prescribing the ownership-scoped attention arm now warn never to append `2>&1`, which turns the arm's own heartbeat into a wake per poll.** `gate-owner-filter.py --interval` writes a `LIVENESS  <counts>  new: <n>  change|no change` line to **stderr** on every poll by design — it is what separates a quiet arm from a dead one — while `Monitor`'s generic guidance (*"merge stderr with `2>&1` so its failures reach your filter"*) is written for a one-shot command, so a manager following it arms a watcher that fires on that heartbeat. Measured 2026-10-17 (attention-controller goal manager `956f6f82`): an arm carrying `2>&1` produced **28 notifications in one 30-minute arm**, every one `no change`, each a full manager turn over the session's cached context. ⚠️ **Stated at all three prescribing sites, not only the rule's home** — `commands/steps/manager-tail.md` (beside the arm command it is copied from), `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md` (the arm's rule home) and `commands/fleet-drive.md` § 13 — because the defect is a reader copying the arm from whichever file they are in, and a pointer does not travel with a copy. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-tail.md`, `commands/steps/attention-watcher.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.171.0

- fix: **the compaction gate's token bound is a PRECONDITION, not an alternative to the percentage, so nothing below the floor is offered on any path.** The two bounds were ORed, so adding a bound could only move a compaction earlier — never retire one the percentage already offered. That was deliberate, and it is what let the percentage fire on its own at 70% of a small window: **70% of 128k is 90k**, far below the 500k floor the operator asked to hold everywhere a compaction is suggested or fired. `bound_verdict()` now returns `False` for any record under the token bound whatever its window, and consults the percentage only when no token bound is given. ⚠️ **This REVERSES a decision v0.140.0 recorded as intentional** — its note read *"The percentage stays as the floor for a small window (75% of 128k is 96k, under a 200k bound, and must still fire)"* — so the reversal is stated rather than left for a reader to find. ⚠️ **The per-tier bounds are retired for one floor**: `commands/steps/manager-tail.md`, `commands/steps/manager-readings.md` and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` all now pass `--threshold-tokens 500000`, where they carried 200k (a topic manager) and 350k (the fleet manager). ⚠️ **`Bound in force:` now names only the bound that can fire** — it printed `70% or 500k tokens`, which would describe a gate this code no longer has, and the label exists precisely so a gate's own output cannot be read as naming a bound it did not apply. ⚠️ **`Window UNKNOWN` is unchanged and still its own answer** — a record with no `context_window_size` cannot be judged against a token bound, returns `None`, and is never read as under it. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_context_usage.py` **29 -> 31**, with the small-window case inverted from "the percentage must still fire" to "a 96k session is not offered". `commands/steps/auto-compaction.md` also says plainly that the section lists sessions over the **500k** bound — its `CONTEXT ≥70%` section header keeps its name, because that is a hand-back contract pinned by `scripts/check-handback-keys.py` and renaming it is a separate change. ⚠️ **Out-of-repo half, edited in the same change**: the Personal-vault runbook `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Auto-compaction still prescribed the retired rule on five sites — it passed `--threshold-tokens 200000` and stated *"The two bounds are ORed … A topic manager carries 200k tokens; the fleet manager carries 350k"*, while three further sites invoked `context-usage.py --compactable --threshold 70` with no token bound at all, which under the precondition rule is percentage-only and would offer a 128k window at ~90k. All four live sites now pass `--threshold-tokens 500000` and state the precondition rule; the one dated `Measured 2026-09-20` example is left as history. Without that edit the CHANGELOG's "on any path" claim would be false in the one place a manager actually reads. Change set: `scripts/context-usage.py`, `commands/steps/auto-compaction.md`, `commands/steps/manager-tail.md`, `commands/steps/manager-readings.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_context_usage.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.170.0

- feat: **`spawn_agent` accepts declared `mounts` and `secrets` on a `docker` / `k8s` row, so a remote worker can be given a FILE or a CREDENTIAL without the raw `env` argument that stays refused.** `env` remains the one refusal of the three, and deliberately: a raw map is an ungoverned surface, nothing on this path can tell a harmless variable from a credential, and the value would travel from this server to the controller in plaintext. The two new arguments are the governed route to the same need — a **mount** is a host path the CONTROLLER checks against its own allowlist (`MOUNT_ALLOW` on the local docker controller) and refuses with `mount_not_allowed: <path>` when it is not covered, and a **secret** is a TeamVault KEY the controller resolves itself, so the caller never holds, transmits or receives the value and a secret's value is not expressible in the argument at all. ⚠️ **The `env` refusal now NAMES them** — a caller told only "not supported" reaches for a local spawn to get what a declared secret already gives. ⚠️ **The shape is validated in `declaredCreateArguments` (`server/a2a-client.mjs`), which is what puts bytes on the wire**, rather than only at the tool boundary: a schema is what a caller reads, and a shape only the schema rejected would be one the request builder would happily send. ⚠️ **A declared `volume` is refused BY NAME, matching the controller's own `mount_not_allowed: volume <name>`** — the allowlist governs host paths only, so a volume would be an ungoverned mount surface, and the two refusals must agree about what a volume is. ⚠️ **`list` and `stop` refuse a declaration rather than dropping it**, and that guard sits BEFORE the per-operation branches: placed after `list`'s early return it was unreachable for `list`, and a guard that can never fire reads exactly like one that passed. ⚠️ **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7 carried a claim this change makes WRONG, and it is corrected rather than left standing** — its eligibility bullet read *"a remote worker has no vault mount"* and grounded that in the create call carrying **only `task`**; a vault *is* now reachable on `docker` when the caller declares a mount the allowlist permits, while on `k8s` a declared mount is refused outright by the backend and only `secrets` is available. The bullet's answer therefore depends on the target and on the allowlist, which the caller cannot read from the target alone. Change set: `server/a2a-client.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `server/a2a-client.test.mjs`, `server/a2a-spawn.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.169.0

- feat: **`scripts/check-spawn-target.py` — the third spawn gate, and the one half of the routing pair that could silently rot.** `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py` has guarded the `mode:` decision since it shipped; the `target:` decision had no equivalent, so the eligibility rule in `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7 was **prose only** — nothing failed if a citation, the binding, or a whole call site disappeared. The gate asserts each registered site cites item 7 rather than restating it, carries both halves of the advisory binding (the warning naming the disqualifier, and the `Advisory, never a refusal` clause that stops a site refusing the spawn), and **discovers citing sites rather than trusting its own list** — so a fourth site fails until it is registered. Wired into `precommit` beside its sibling.
- fix: **item 7 said "the two call sites that read `target:`" while three did.** `commands/open-a2a.md` landed 2026-10-17 reading the field itself in its § 2.1, and the rule's own closing sentence was stale from that day — the doc and the tree disagreed with no error on either side. The sentence now names three and points at the gate that keeps the two in step.

- feat: **`/supervisor:open`'s CREATE branch now routes through the a2a-worker-controller, so one command opens every row and `open-a2a` becomes the parallel name rather than a second path.** The create half dispatched `spawn_agent` directly, which left the controller reachable only from `/supervisor:open-a2a` — so goal SC8 (*"after `open-a2a` is verified on real runs it replaces `/supervisor:open`"*) had no carrier. ⚠️ **The dispatch is referenced, never copied**: the create branch reads `commands/open-a2a.md` § Step 2 and executes it, so the two commands cannot drift and the controller's contract keeps one home. ⚠️ **An absent `target:` now means the controller's `local` backend, which is NOT `spawn_agent`'s local path** — that single substitution is what makes four things inert on that arm, and each now says so where it is defined rather than silently going dead: the `mode:` carve-out (neither arm can carry `interactive` — `docker`/`k8s` refuses the argument whenever it is present, and `local` carries `task` alone), `role` (§ 3.0 resolves it for the tab-opening paths only), the `bind` field (no `spawn_agent` response exists to read it from), and Gate 3 — a `spawn_agent`-side check, so it binds the `docker` / `k8s` arm alone. ⚠️ **Gate 3's exemption on the `local` arm is stated, not fixed**: the controller opens a tab directly and never passes through the supervisor's cap, and closing that asymmetry is out of scope for this change. ⚠️ **The start-rate limit is bypassed on that arm for the same reason, and it is the sharper half** — item 5 enforces the rate on *both* existing spawn paths (the server, and § 3.1's wezterm `--resume` branch via `scripts/start-rate.mjs`), but a controller `local` create reaches neither, so a `--flagged` batch of local rows now opens its tabs unpaced. The incident that key exists for was 22 sessions in 2.8 minutes, which a population cap cannot see. Recorded at `commands/open.md` § Step 1.5 Gate 3 rather than silently inherited; **closing it belongs in a follow-up**. ⚠️ **The wezterm fallback is removed rather than re-scoped.** It existed to open a tab when `mcp__supervisor__*` was unavailable, and the create branch is now defined by *not* opening one — so on a vault whose launcher config omits `supervisor` a create is unavailable rather than downgraded, and the report names the arm that could not be reached. JUMP and RESUME keep their tab behaviour unchanged: only the create half moved. ⚠️ **`allowed-tools` gained `mcp__supervisor__spawn_agent`**, which the create branch had been calling without declaring. ⚠️ **`check-spawn-mode` no longer lists `commands/open.md` as a spawn site, and that is the change rather than a bypass**: the file carries no `spawn_agent(` call at all any more and neither arm can carry `interactive`, so the both-ways binding it demanded could not honestly be written — keeping it listed would have demanded a line that cannot exist, which is the shape that gets a check disabled instead of satisfied. It stays covered by the checker's own grant dimension (it grants the tool for the arm `open-a2a.md` executes, so it must carry the anchor to the rule's home, and it does), and the mode decision did not move out of reach — it moved to `commands/open-a2a.md`, which executes the spawn and is checked both ways. ⚠️ **`scripts/budget-baseline.json`'s `commands/open.md` entry is raised by hand, 61974 → 66982** — the documented remedy for a deliberate growth, since `--tighten` cannot raise an entry by design. Change set: `commands/open.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.168.2

- docs: **orphan recovery gains the `stop_agent` FAILED-reply reconcile — and why it matters more than it looks.** `docs/orphan-recovery.md` told a reader *"A stop the controller refused is not a stop"*, which is true of a genuine refusal and misleading about the case the live exercise actually hit: `stop_agent` can return the controller's `TASK_STATE_FAILED` while the stop **succeeded**. Measured 2026-10-17 — the reply was the controller's `UNKNOWN`/`FAILED` and the controller's own `list` came back **empty**, so the worker was gone. ⚠️ The consequence is the load-bearing half: the ledger's `stopped_at` marker is written on `stop_agent`'s **success** path only, so a stop that succeeded behind a FAILED reply leaves **no stop record**, and the row then reads as an **`ORPHAN`** — the one verdict that authorises another stop + recreate. The doc now states the one-call reconcile against `list`, the missing-marker consequence, and how a true refusal differs (the reconcile still shows the worker). Found by this task's own exercise rather than by review, which is the point of running it.

## v0.168.1

- docs: **orphan recovery gets its own home — `docs/orphan-recovery.md`.** The recovery procedure for a remote worker whose container or pod is gone was a single clause inside `docs/fleet-surface.md` item 7's eligibility list, which is a statement about *why a row is ineligible* and cannot also carry the steps. The new file is the single home for the procedure: the `resume` refusal on both A2A targets (`server/a2a-spawn.mjs:81`), `stop_agent` keyed on the **worker id** and never the task name, the repeat-create refusal and the remedy its own message names (`pkg/docker.go:256`), the 120 s card-ready window and why a create inside it must not be retried, and the finished-vs-orphan split with the 60 s heartbeat lag that delays an `ORPHAN` verdict after a kill. Item 7's bullet keeps the eligibility statement and points at the new file rather than restating it — a second copy of the procedure is the defect the single-home rule forbids. Registered in `README.md` § Documentation and `CLAUDE.md` § Layout.

## v0.168.0

- feat: **the session-ledger write MERGES, so a supervisor that did not spawn a row can record a stop on it.** `writeLedger` refused every write on an agent carrying `rehydrated`, so a stop on a worker this server did not spawn reached no durable store at all. That is not an edge case: `stop_agent` resolves its agent from the request-time roster *precisely because* the worker a manager stops is usually the one it did not spawn, so the stop was recorded nowhere and the sweep then read a deliberately stopped worker exactly as it reads a dead one — an orphan candidate the drive leg would stop+recreate. ⚠️ **The refusal is now scoped to the CREATE arm, because the guarantee is a property of the WRITE rather than of the caller**: the endpoint's `Post` REPLACES a whole row, so a second server would rebuild it from identity alone and blank the owner's fields, while its `PATCH` MERGES, so the same server can name one field and leave every other exactly as the owner wrote it. `server/session-ledger.mjs` gains `patchSessionLedgerRow`, and `publishLedgerPatch` sends an adopted row's patch to the per-id route instead of `publishLedgerRow`'s replacing one — a separate function rather than a flag, because routing a partial record to the replacing route is the destructive write the guard exists to prevent. Both share `inFlightPublishes`, so the shutdown path still waits for a stop issued moments before the process exits. ⚠️ **An adopted row deliberately does NOT gain a local `ledgerRecord`** — this process holds no copy of one, so a record built from the patch alone would be PARTIAL, and the next wholesale publish of it would blank the owner's fields: the same damage deferred by one write instead of prevented. ⚠️ **The decision is now a pure function, `ledgerWriteRoute`, because it could not be tested where it lived.** `writeLedger` is module-local in `supervisor.mjs`, which cannot be imported (it starts an MCP server), so the guarantee was pinned by matching the guard's SOURCE TEXT — a shape a refactor can change while leaving the behaviour wrong. `ledger.test.mjs` now asserts the routing directly: an adopted row with a stop patch answers `patch` and never `post`, an adopted row with no patch still refuses, an owned row answers `post`, and a session id that never resolved answers `no-session-id`. Change set: `server/session-ledger.mjs`, `server/session-ledger.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/ledger.mjs`, `server/ledger.test.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **the interim machine-wide A2A stop file is retired — the ledger row is now the only record of a deliberate stop.** It existed for exactly one case: `writeLedger` refused to patch a rehydrated row, so a stop on a worker the stopping server did not spawn had nowhere else to go. That case is closed by the merge above, so the file's reason is gone with it. ⚠️ **Deleted, not deprecated** — `server/stop-file.mjs` and its test, the `config.stopFile` key (and its entry in the config-surface test), the `appendStop` call in `stop_agent`, and the reader half of `scripts/a2a-liveness.py`, which loses `read_stops`, `stop_file_path` and the `A2A_STOP_FILE` override. `stop_state(row, stops)` becomes `stopped_state(row)`, reading the row's own `stopped_at` and nothing else, and `verdict` loses its `stops` parameter — so the resolver has one stop source where it had two, which is the simplification the change buys. ⚠️ **The property the file also carried survives without it.** It was keyed on `worker_id` so that a task respawned after a stop would not read its SUCCESSOR as stopped; this resolver matches rows by `session_id` against the task's CURRENT ids, and a respawn is a new session with a new row, so a stale stop sits on a row nothing here selects — the same outcome by a different route, which is why this is a removal rather than a lost guarantee. ⚠️ **`stopped_state` keeps reading by PRESENCE, not truthiness** — `True`, `0` and `{}` are all truthy-or-present, and reading any of them as "deliberately stopped" is the direction that reports a dead worker as finished and suppresses its recovery; a new fixture pins the negative half too, that a row carrying only a `worker_id` answers `none`. ⚠️ **The sweep reader's own input text was stale and is corrected** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` input 15 still told the reader the resolver consults TWO sources and that the deleted file "is the load-bearing one", which would have sent a manager hunting for a record that no longer exists; it now states the single source and why the second one went. That file sits outside the grep probe's `server/ scripts/` scope, which is exactly why the probe alone did not catch it — the probe proves the code is clean, not that the prose describing it is. `grep -rnE 'stop-file|a2a-stops|appendStop|stop_state' server/ scripts/` returns 0 lines. Change set: `server/stop-file.mjs` (deleted), `server/stop-file.test.mjs` (deleted), `server/config.mjs`, `server/config.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `scripts/a2a-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_a2a_liveness.py`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/budget-baseline.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.167.1

- fix: **the local backend's control call failed on every row — the default endpoint was the controller's origin, not its JSON-RPC path.** The controller mounts its handler at `pkg/a2a.go`'s `A2APath` (`/a2a`), so `ENDPOINT_DEFAULT` = `http://localhost:8092` answered `404 page not found` to every `create` and `list` that did not supply its own `--endpoint`. Measured 2026-10-17 on the first live SC3 probe, and it is worth recording *why* it shipped: the checks that preceded the release — an override in both flag positions, an empty `--task` refused — each supplied an endpoint of their own, so **none of them ever exercised the default**, and the one path that was broken was the only path never run. ⚠️ **The repair is the default, not an appended path.** Every sibling spells the endpoint in full (`server/a2a-spawn.mjs` documents `http://127.0.0.1:8080/a2a`; `worker-resource-builder.go` returns `"https://" + host + "/a2a"`), so appending one here would turn a correct `…/a2a` into `…/a2a/a2a` — the old defect traded for a new one, on the callers who had it right. The shape is the caller's contract; only the default was wrong. ⚠️ **A guard now exists where the file previously had no test at all**: `scripts/tests/test_a2a_local_control.py` asserts the default carries the path *and* that the URL actually sent is that value — a constant-only assertion would pass again the moment something downstream rewrote it, which is the same failure one layer along. Verified to discriminate rather than merely pass: it fails on the shipped value and prints the origin URL the broken build sent. The suite also pins the reply parsing against the shape `pkg/a2a-executor.go` really produces (the `ControlResponse` as a data part inside the task's `artifacts[]`, reached through the `result.task` ONEOF — not a flat `result.create.*`), the endpoint precedence, and the recursive redaction of token-shaped keys. Change set: `scripts/a2a-local-control.py`, `scripts/tests/test_a2a_local_control.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **`commands/open-a2a.md` no longer claims a controller-created worker has no pane.** It stated the worker "holds no local pane" and forbade reporting a `pane_id` at all — correct when only `docker` / `k8s` existed, and false from the moment the `local` backend landed, because `pkg/types.go` populates `PaneID` precisely for a worker that runs in a WezTerm tab and leaves it empty for a backend whose workers answer at their own `a2a_url`. The live probe's create reply carried `"pane_id": "81"`, which is what exposed it. The prohibition is now scoped to the backends it actually describes, and the local report line carries the pane — for a local worker that is the operator's way into the tab, so suppressing it hid the one handle that backend has. Change set: `commands/open-a2a.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.167.0

- feat: **`scripts/a2a-local-control.py` gives a slash command the local backend's control call, mirroring the wire shape rather than re-deriving it.** The supervisor MCP server cannot route `local` to the controller — `server/config.mjs:218` hardcodes `a2aUrls` to `{ docker, k8s }` and `server/a2a-spawn.mjs:33` sets `A2A_SPAWN_TARGETS = ['docker','k8s']`, so `supervisor.mjs:1666` sends only those to `spawnA2AWorker` and an omitted target falls through to the in-process WezTerm path — so a command that must send *every* spawn through the controller needs its own client for the local backend, which its launchd wrapper serves at `http://localhost:8092`. The four wire rules are mirrored from `server/a2a-client.mjs` and stated in the file, because each yields a request that looks right and is not: the part is `{"data": …}` and never `{"text": …}` (the controller answers `ErrInvalidParams` to a message carrying no data part, so no operation runs at all); the data payload must be a non-null object (a JSON `null` hydrates to no discriminator); the call is one-shot, with the bare `SendMessage` method and no `accept: text/event-stream`; and exactly one operation-specific field is set. ⚠️ **`TASK_STATE_FAILED` gets its own exit code rather than reading as "nothing happened"** — it arrives with no detail and the failure can land in the controller's task store *after* the executor has run, so a `create` on that path may have started a worker and still read FAILED; the caller reconciles against `list` rather than retrying. ⚠️ **The local backend gets its own URL variable, `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL_LOCAL`** — reusing `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL_DOCKER` would be a live bug rather than a shortcut, since the two controllers are separate deployments on this Mac (`:8092` for local, `:8090` for docker) and that name would send a local create to the docker backend the moment it is set. The token resolves at run time from the TeamVault key `mcp-start.py` already uses for `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN_DOCKER`, or from `$A2A_AUTH_TOKEN` when the wrapper has exported it; only the key name appears anywhere, and the printer redacts any token-shaped key in a reply rather than echoing it. Change set: `scripts/a2a-local-control.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **`/supervisor:open-a2a` is `/supervisor:open`'s parallel that routes every spawn through the a2a-worker-controller, the backend chosen by the row's `target:` frontmatter.** It runs `open.md`'s § Step 0 / § Step 0.5 / 0.6 / § Step 1 / § Step 1.5 **by reference rather than copying them** — `open.md` is 61 974 B, `check-budget.py` refuses a new `commands/*.md` body over 30 720 B outright, and a freeze on `open.md` rules out factoring a shared step file — so the new file owns only the dispatch and lands at 11 066 B. ⚠️ **The local backend is reached by this plugin's own client, not by `spawn_agent`.** The server cannot route `local` to the controller (`config.mjs:218` hardcodes `a2aUrls` to `{docker, k8s}`; `a2a-spawn.mjs:33` sets `A2A_SPAWN_TARGETS = ['docker','k8s']`, so `supervisor.mjs:1666` sends only those to `spawnA2AWorker`), and adding it there would silently change `/supervisor:open`'s no-target behaviour — so an absent `target:` goes to `scripts/a2a-local-control.py`, while `docker` / `k8s` keep going through `spawn_agent`, whose path *is* the controller. An unknown target is passed **verbatim** and the server's refusal is **relayed, never quoted** — a copy of that string here would be a second text going stale silently. A controller-created worker is addressed by **`worker_id`, never `pane_id`**, and its `session_id` is read from a later `list`, never from the create reply. ⚠️ **`docs/session-tiers.md`'s own lockstep diff carried a latent defect that this row exposed**: its class was `[a-z-]+`, which cannot match the digit in `open-a2a`, so the new row made the diff report a command file with no row *because of the checker's own regex* — a false failure whose remedy a reader would hunt for in the wrong file. The class is now `[a-z0-9-]+`. ⚠️ **`scripts/tests/test_check_spawn_mode.py` no longer hand-lists the spawn sites** — it derives them from the checker's own `SPAWN_SITES`, because the hand-held copy went stale the moment this command joined the set, and the failure surfaced as three tests asserting the *checker* was wrong. Change set: `commands/open-a2a.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `scripts/check-spawn-mode.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_spawn_mode.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.166.0
